A pad plays its slice as `;` does, held or tapped, and puts the cursor on the slice's start, as tracker-ce's pads select the slice they play; a pad past the last slice says so and plays nothing, and the status line names what the pads play. Every other key works on the page, so `;`, `l`, `0`, `zi` and the `Space` keys do, and leaving the page leaves the mode. **The playhead is a line,** after the author's comment on #274 that a cell's background is a character wide, about 15 pixels: a two-pixel line in the playhead's green, as it is everywhere, one image stored with `a=t` and placed with `a=p` once for each playhead, under placement ids 1 to 4, `X=` pixels into its character, after the ruler's marks and before the cursor's line, so the cursor is over one it meets. It's placed again only when it has moved a pixel, about 60 bytes, and taken off when its voice ends, so a page with nothing playing sends nothing. Like the cursor's line, it's sent again when the window changes size and taken off while anything is drawn over the page, and it follows `zc`: with the cursor as its column, it's its column's background in a dimmed green, as it is with no pictures drawn, in a render, and under anything drawn over the page. **What it shows:** each block's report says where up to four voices playing a sample through the watched track are (`Engine::playheads`), the track's note and its tail and then the preview voices, each with its bank entry, or none for a one-off file, in the pool's frames, and not a synth's or one fading out after it's cut. The page shows those of the entry shown, and the one-off file's only while it's what the page last played, so the explorer's file isn't drawn over another sample. It follows the newest report each frame, 33 ms, so it moves in steps of about 1,600 frames at 48 kHz, and goes after 250 ms without a report, as audio has stopped. **Checked** in kitty 0.50.0, run with its own config and cache folders under `script` to record what the program wrote, with a generated two-second beat cut by `:slice 8`: idle, it wrote no picture commands; `;` stored the line once and placed it 7 times across slice `00`, then took it off; `Space c` placed it 85 times, a frame at a time, from the cursor to the end; with two notes in the phrase and `Tab`, each note's line ran across slice `00` and went at its end; and `i`, `f` and `q` played slice `03`, moved the cursor to it, and said there's no slice `08`. **Left for later:** the selection (#276); the `!` preview of a render, which can play through the same path; lighting the pads that have a slice, as tracker-ce does, for which the ruler's numbers stand in; more than four playheads; and moving the playhead between reports. Added on 2026-11-10.
116. **The sample page edits its entry's points: `Enter` splits the slice at the cursor, or at the playhead while the entry plays, so a break can be sliced by tapping along; `J` joins a slice with the next and `x` takes the point under the cursor away; `=` `-` nudge that point a pixel, `+` `_` a column and Cmd-h/l a frame, between its neighbours, the cursor going with it; `g s` `g e` `g l` `g L` put the start, the end and the loop's ends at the cursor; with `Snap` on, a point placed goes to the nearest zero crossing; `m a` names the slice; `v` selects frames that `;` plays, with `'<` `'>` `''`; and two settings rows hold `Snap` and the slice's own gain, pan, tune, reverse and fades, which `K` reaches and `=`, `-`, `K` and `x` change, with `>` `<` and `~` from the waveform** (#276, the eighth step of the sample editor's design, #268, M22). **Prior art:** Vim's line keys, as #268 maps them: `J` joins a line to the next, `x` deletes what's under the cursor, `m a` sets a mark, `''` goes back to where the latest jump left from and goes back again, `'<` and `'>` are the last Visual selection's ends, and `o` goes to its other end; tracker-ce's Sample Playback screen, read as prior art only, whose slice adjust moves a slice by its view's width over 600, a pixel of its 600-pixel waveform, whose add and remove keys renumber the slices (`functAddSlice`, `functRemoveSlice`), and whose snap to zero (`isSnapToZeroEnabled`) looks for a zero crossing in a zone around a point; Audacity's Find Zero Crossings, which moves a selection's edges to the nearest ones; and decision 67's Cmd-h/l, decision 107's pixel and decision 114's playheads. **Which point:** the one nearest the cursor within half a column of it, a slice's start first where two are as near, then the start, the end and the loop's start and end, as slicing is what the page is mostly for and `g s` `g e` `g l` `g L` place the others; the cursor's row names it, `on slice 03's start`, in its colour, so it's clear what `=` and `x` will move. **Nudges:** a pixel is decision 107's, the first frame of the pixel one along at the zoom shown, so the point's line moves exactly a pixel, closer than a frame a pixel a frame, and with no pictures drawn, as in a render, a column, as `h` and `l` move there; a column is the zoom's frames; and a frame is a frame, with Cmd-h/l taking that over from `=` and `-` on this page, and Cmd-k/j still `+` and `_`. A count moves that many. A slice's start stays after the slice before's and before the next's, inside the entry; the start before the end and the end after it; the loop's start before its end and its end after it, inside the file. Nothing passes a neighbour, so nothing renumbers, and a nudge is a `SetBankEntry` with the ids, the letters and the steps' locks as they were. The cursor goes with the point, so holding `=` keeps moving it. **Splits and joins** are decision 111's `Action::SplitSlice` and `Action::JoinSlices`, now bound: `Enter` on an entry with no slices cuts it in two, the part before keeping the id and the letters; `J` gives the slice the next one's letters; and `x` on a slice's start joins it to the slice before, so it's never a cut in the audio. `x` on slice `00` is refused while there are others, as it's where the slices start, and with it alone it's `:slice clear`. `x` on the start or the end puts the file's back, and on either loop point takes the loop away. **`g s` `g e` `g l` `g L`** after the phrase view's `g s` and `g e`: the end is one past the last frame, so `g e` ends the sample at the cursor's line, with its frame not played. With no loop, `g l` loops from the cursor to the end and `g L` from the start to the cursor. **Tap-slicing:** `Enter` splits at the newest playhead of the entry shown rather than the cursor while there is one: the page's own sound, from `;`, `Space c` or a pad, if it's still playing, or else the track's note of the entry, which the report puts before its tail. The cursor stays where it was, so the view doesn't jump while tapping. A report is up to 33 ms old, about 1,600 frames at 48 kHz, which a nudge tidies. **Zero-crossing snap** is `PlayParams::snap_zero`, an entry's, off by default and a serde default, so `FORMAT_VERSION` stays 6. A frame is a crossing if it's silent or the channels taken together change sign from the frame before it, read from the pool's frames at the file's frame. `Enter`, `g s` `g e` `g l` `g L` take the nearest within a column either way, the earlier of two as near, and a pixel's or a column's nudge the nearest within a column on from where it would have gone, so it always moves; a frame's nudge never snaps, as it's for placing by hand. None so near leaves the point where it was put. **The settings rows,** below the overview: the entry's row gains `Snap`, and a new row under it names the slice under the cursor with its letters, `Slice 03 'a`, or `Whole sample`, with its `Gain`, `Pan`, `Tune`, `Reverse`, `Fade in` and `Fade out`, its own in the text's colour and those it plays as the entry does dimmer, so the page is a row taller, 36 rows. With no slices they're the entry's own, without pan, which is the track's. `K` on the waveform lists the settings with their values, and choosing one puts the focus on it, in the cursor's colours; there `h` and `l` go along the rows, `0` and `$` to their ends, `=` `-` change it a small step and `+` `_` a large one (a dB and six, one pan and ten, a semitone and an octave, a millisecond and ten), `K` lists its values with the sample's own first, `x` puts a slice's back as the sample's, and `Esc` goes back to the waveform. `j` and `k` still go by slice, so a setting can be set slice after slice. `>` and `<` move the slice's gain a dB and `~` reverses it from the waveform, as immediate keys until the operators come. Gain goes to 24 dB either way, as `:sample gain` does, and tune to `MAX_TUNE`. The waveform's picture is compared without the slices' settings and the letters, so changing them sends nothing to the terminal. **Visual mode, as little as `'<` and `'>` need:** `v` starts selecting frames from the cursor, both ends included, the selection's grey behind the columns it covers and `VISUAL` on the status line; `;` plays it forward at the entry's gain and tune with a declick at each end; `o` goes to its other end, `v` or `Esc` ends it, and `gv` selects it again. `V`, the operators and `d'a` wait for the steps after this. **Marks:** `m` and a letter is `:mark`, on the slice under the cursor. After `'`, `<` and `>` go to the last selection's first and last frame, as jumps, and `'` goes back to where the latest jump left from, recording a jump itself, so `''` twice comes back, as Vim's do; it can leave the page, as the latest jump may have come from elsewhere. Each edit is one undo step. **Checked** in kitty 0.50.0 with its own config and cache folders, on Ableton's `Breakbeat 134 bpm.aif` cut by `:slice 8`: `Enter` split a slice where the cursor was, `=` `=` `+` and Cmd-l moved it two pixels, a column and a frame, `J` and `x` joined and took a start away, `u` brought each back, `K` put the focus on `Gain` where `=` and `+` raised it, `>` and `~` worked from the waveform, `m a` named the slice, `v` showed `VISUAL` and the selection, and `'>` and `''` went there and back. The audio device didn't run in that session, so tap-slicing is checked by the tests, against the engine's own playheads. Added on 2026-11-10.
117. **The Acid's knobs are fitted to Arturia's Acid V through the comparison harness: `Cutoff` runs from 170 Hz to 1.45 kHz on a taper, `Resonance` reaches the ladder nearly evenly and quietens the voice a little as it rises, `Env Mod` is squared and its sweep widened to Acid V's span, `Decay` runs from 40 ms to 600 ms with an accent taking the shortest, and `Accent` is squared and stronger** (#295, measured by #331's harness). The author found the knobs had "lots of dead feeling ranges" against Acid V's and ABL3's. Each knob was swept in 16 or 17 steps on both sides, Acid V on its Default preset with its sequencer off: `acid-cutoff`, `acid-cutoff-peak`, `acid-resonance`, `acid-envmod`, `acid-decay`, `acid-accent` and `acid-accent-apart` in `tools/compare/specs`, whose `knob` report now measures a held note's filter peak and bass, a fitted brightness decay, and accents. **A finding first:** Acid V accents every note from velocity 100, ours from `0x70`, so #331's renders at velocity 100 compared Acid V's accented notes with our plain ones. With plain notes on both, at 99, and #337's level, the old Acid's peak was within a dB of Acid V's at the cutoff's bottom rather than 11 dB under it, though Acid V's still climbed 5.5 dB across the knob to ours' 2.4. The specs now play plain notes at 99. **Followed from Acid V, where it agrees with a 303:** the cutoff's slow start, now the knob to the power 1.9 over 3.1 octaves, which is how an audio-taper pot behaves and what #295 expected; its range, set by where the filter rings at Res 90%, 1.18 kHz at the top where ours rang at 1.9 kHz, a range lower than Open303's 314 Hz to 2.4 kHz; the resonance thinning the bass from its first step, which Stinchcombe's analysis gives a 303, now by a feedback rising as the knob to the power 1.1 instead of the ladder's cube, and 4 dB of level lost at full, curved as the knob to the power 0.7; the accent shortening the filter envelope to the Decay knob's shortest, which Whittle describes. **Followed from Acid V where the 303's published figures don't settle it:** the Decay's span, 40 ms to 600 ms as time constants, as Whittle's 200 ms to 2 s doesn't say what it times and Open303 reads it as time constants, which left most of the knob slower than a sixteenth; and the Accent knob's square, 5 octaves of sweep at its height and 5 times the level envelope, so full Accent is about 4.5 dB louder and 1.2 octaves brighter at its start, and noon about 1 dB. **Not followed, as Acid V's own:** Env Mod does little over three quarters of Acid V's knob and then sweeps most of its range, a knee no common pot has, which would be the kind of dead stretch #295 is about; ours squares the knob, like an audio taper, and matches Acid V's sweep at either end, 1.4 and 1.25 times Open303's, around 0.6 of Open303's centre. Acid V's level climbing with the cutoff isn't added as a gain: with the new range ours climbs as much as Acid V's when held, about 25 dB over the knob with Env Mod at 0, and the 303's analyses give no gain that follows the cutoff. Acid V's Bass Boost, on in its Default, isn't a 303's. **Level:** `LEVEL` goes from 0.4 to 0.75 to make up what the shorter decay and the resonance's loss take, so a plain C-2 with every knob at noon is at −20.7 dBFS RMS, where Acid V at 0 dB is at −20.5 and the old Acid was at −20.4; against the other synth voices at their defaults on the same C-2, from −11.2 to −22.4 dBFS RMS, only String and Modal are quieter, and its peak, −6.0 dBFS, is within theirs, −2.8 to −12.1. **Where they still differ:** with Env Mod at noon the cutoff knob is about 0.3 octaves brighter than Acid V's at its bottom and top, and its middle matches; at the lowest cutoffs our ladder rings more than Acid V's; and four accents in a row build up a little less at Res 50%, while at Res 90% they build more. The engine tests that pinned the old feel now play `Accent` full where they want a strong accent, and count zero crossings with no resonance. Added on 2026-11-10.
118. **`Comp` has a `Type`: `Clean`, the compressor it was and the default, and `Bus`, an SSL G-bus's glue, modelled on the circuit as Jakob Erland's G-SSL clone documents it and tuned against the compressor in Arturia's Bus FORCE with `tools/compare`; `Clean`'s attack loses its long tail** (#338, its first step, and #334). **Prior art:** Ableton's stock Compressor and Glue, the one transparent and the other an SSL with Cytomic's soft clip, range and dry/wet; the SSL G-series bus compressor's switches; Giannoulis, Massberg and Reiss (JAES 2012), whose smooth decoupled peak detector `Clean` now uses. **The type** is a choice on the slot like Distort's, `Type` first, with no label, as choosing one chooses a sound; `VCA`, `FET` and `Diode` take the places after `Bus`, so a song's types keep theirs. Each type shows only its own settings, and each keeps them while another is shown, so changing type and back finds them as they were. `Threshold`, `Source`, `Mix` and `Gain` mean the same in both and are shared. `Bus`'s ratio, attack and release are switches, picked with `K` or stepped, by the same names as `Clean`'s knobs and with labels of their own, `BRTO`, `BATK` and `BREL`, as a slot's labels name one setting each: 2, 4 and 10 to 1; 0.1, 0.3, 1, 3, 10 and 30 ms; 0.1, 0.3, 0.6 and 1.2 s and `Auto`. It starts at 4:1, 10 ms and `Auto`, the G-bus's usual place on a drum bus. `Bus` has no `Auto` gain, as the G-bus has a makeup knob and no more. An effect now keeps up to 16 settings across its types and shows at most 10, and a slot's drawing comes after all five columns of the FX page, as `Clean` has nine settings. Existing `Comp` slots load as `Clean`. **Topology: feedback.** The G-SSL's detector hears a second, "dummy" VCA, fed the input and driven by the same control voltage as the main ones, so it hears what's already been turned down: Erland calls it a combination of feed-forward and feedback, mainly feedback. #188's body said feed-forward, as Sound On Sound's review of SSL's The Bus+ calls the original, whose new button adds a feedback mode; the circuit was taken over the review. Bus FORCE's own compressor measures as feed-forward, its attack the same at every Force. So the ratio switch sets how hard the rectifier drives the control voltage, `ratio - 1` times what's over, which settles at the ratio, while the loop speeds the attack up by as much: at 10 ms, `Bus` reaches 63% in 23 ms at 2:1, 12 ms at 4:1 and 6 ms at 10:1, where Bus FORCE takes 14 ms at each. The loop is solved within each frame, not a frame late, so a fast attack at 10:1 can't ring. **The gain law:** the control voltage is the reduction in dB, as the VCAs take theirs, charged by the attack while the rectifier asks for more and let go toward nothing by the release otherwise, as the timing capacitor is; the rectifier's peak holds over 10 ms so a wave's troughs don't let go of what its peaks asked for. Both sides share one detector. **Auto** is the G-SSL's fifth release position: a 0.47 uF capacitor with 750k across it, and 6.8 uF hung on it through 91k. A short peak charges only the small one, which empties into the big one in about 40 ms, but held down the big one fills over about 0.6 s and the pair let go over about 5 s: the release to 63% is 39 ms after a 20 ms burst, 335 ms after half a second and 2.5 s after two. GroupDIY's thread on the clone pairs the parts the other way round, 91k with 0.47 uF, which gives the same two speeds; Gyraf's pairing is used. **The knee:** fitted to Bus FORCE's static curve at each ratio, with its Force set where its top 10 dB has our ratio, 2.7, 4.3 and 5.25: the knee, in dB of what the detector hears, is 9.5, 6.5 and 5.5 dB, starting at the threshold, and its line sits 0.25, 1.75 and 2.5 dB over it, as the G-SSL's ratio switch moves its threshold to make up for itself. The curves come within 0.11, 0.17 and 0.19 dB RMS of Bus FORCE's from -40 to 0 dBFS, where `Clean` at the same ratios was 1.1 to 1.9 dB off. **Times:** from 1 ms up, the attack's time constant is fitted so that 4:1 reaches 63% and 90% of its turn down within 15% of when Bus FORCE does at the same time; Bus FORCE goes no faster than 1 ms, so 0.1 and 0.3 ms are what the switch says at 4:1. The set releases are their times, as Bus FORCE's are within 6%; its longest is 500 ms. **Colour: none.** Bus FORCE's compressor adds no 2nd harmonic at any level, and no harmonics at all until it turns down, so `Bus` adds none of its own. Its odd harmonics while it turns down hard with a 1 ms attack are its gain following the wave, -40 dB at 100 Hz against Bus FORCE's -64, as the G-bus does on bass at its fastest. **Mix, yes; soft clip, no.** `Mix` is the slot's own and costs nothing, and parallel compression is what a bus compressor's mix is for, as Glue's dry/wet and SSL's later units have it. A soft clip isn't the G-bus's, the reference's compressor path has none (its clipper is separate and was off), and a Distort, Tape or Limit slot after it does that, with #262's shared clip still to come. A range limit and a side-chain high-pass, which Glue and Bus FORCE have, are left for later. **`Clean`'s tail (#334):** it keeps its own law, but its detector is now Giannoulis et al.'s smooth decoupled one, the release first with no attack of its own, then the attack, where the attack and release had branched on the instantaneous level, so a sine's troughs let it release between peaks and its attack crept up on its last tenth. Against Bus FORCE at 10 ms, 90% now takes 23 ms, where it took 44 and Bus FORCE takes 28; at 30 ms, 70 ms against 144 and 74. The release reads a little later, 110 ms at 100 against Bus FORCE's 105, as the attack now smooths it too. Every render where `Clean` turns down changes, a steady level's reduction by up to about 0.4 dB, now on the curve it draws; one under its knee is bit for bit as it was, and no golden render had a compressor. **Measured** by five new specs, `comp-bus-static`, `comp-bus-attack`, `comp-bus-release`, `comp-bus-auto` and `comp-bus-harmonics`, the last with a new `distortion` report of THD and the 2nd and 3rd harmonics at stepped levels; the static report now gives the curves' RMS distance. Added on 2026-11-10. Decision 132 gives `Bus` `Auto` and turns `Gain` into `Makeup`, and decision 134 splits `Bus` into `Console` and `Glue`.
119. **The sample page names each of its modes on the status line's badge, `PADS` for `i`'s keys, `SETTINGS` for `K`'s focus on the settings rows and `VISUAL` for `v`'s selection, with the line under it saying what the keys do there and that `Esc` leaves; the waveform cursor's line is set back while the focus is on the settings; and an audition a mute or a solo silences says so, rather than playing in silence** (the author's reports after #336 that `;` and `Space c` played nothing and that `h` didn't go back). **What was found:** `h` is a pad in the keys mode, slice `05`, so with `i` on, `l` moved the cursor and `h` jumped to slice `05` or said there was none; with the focus on the settings, `h` and `l` went along the settings and `h` on `Snap`, the first, did nothing. Both modes showed only in a status message the next key cleared, and the settings' focus not at all on the badge. `;` and `Space c` were heard in a fresh song in kitty, with real key presses sent through System Events, the engine's playheads moving and the track page's meter showing them, and in tests through the real engine, measured by the output's RMS, so no fault was found in the path decision 114 built. The one silent case found: they play through the cursor's track, so a muted track, or another soloed, plays them in silence, with the playhead moving and nothing said, and the page shows neither. **Prior art:** Vim's and the program's own badges, `INSERT`, `VISUAL` and `KEYS`, each the mode's name in its colour; Vim's `showmode`, which keeps the mode on screen while it's on. **The badges:** `PADS` in the keys mode's yellow, as the track page's keyboard is `KEYS`; `VISUAL` in Visual's mauve, which before was in the page's blue; and `SETTINGS` in the cursor's rosewater, the colour the setting it's on is drawn in. **The hint** shows under the badge whenever nothing else is said there, so a message still shows first and the hint comes back after it: what the pads play, with `Esc leaves, for h j k l to move again`; the setting's keys, with `Esc goes back to the waveform`; or `; plays the selection, o goes to its other end; v or Esc ends it`. `h` or `l` at either end of the settings says it's the first or the last and that `Esc` goes back to the waveform, where they move. `Esc` leaving the settings or a selection clears what was said in them. **The cursor's line** is stored again in the cursor's colour set back, as the dimmed playhead column is, while the focus is on the settings, and as before when it leaves; as its column, its background is set back the same way. **Mute and solo** still silence the page's auditions, as they do the track's notes and the explorer's preview through the same voice; the status line says `Track 01 is muted, so it plays silently. Space m unmutes it`, or `Track 02 is soloed, so Track 01 plays silently. Space M clears the solos`. Hearing them whatever the mute says, as Ableton's browser preview goes to the cue, is left for the author to choose. **The guide:** `Space ?` gains **The sample editor**, after **Sounds, samples and kits**, whose section on the page now points to it. Added on 2026-11-10.
120. **`Res` turns each filter family on a taper of its own, fitted to Cytomic's The Drop, so each equal turn raises the peak about as much as The Drop's knob does, the peak climbs steeply over the last few percent before ringing, and the types that self-oscillate start to from about `D9`, 85% of the knob, where The Drop's circuits do over 100%** (#333, its first question, which the author agreed to). The old taper was a straight line into each filter's resonance, so the clean low-pass's peak had risen 15 dB by Res 50% and only 5 more by the top, while the Korg 35s' had risen only 3 to 4 dB by half way. **Prior art:** The Drop's manual says its circuits were "carefully adapted to make them more consistent in use", and measured, they are: OSR's, PRD's and MS1's peaks rise nearly evenly from 0 to 96% of their 100, by 15 to 21 dB, and then climb to ringing in the last 4%. **Pairings,** from its circuit table: the clean state-variable types with OSR, an OTA state-variable filter like the OSCar's, at no drive, the band-pass as its high-pass and low-pass in series and the notch the two in parallel; `Ladder` with PRD, a transistor ladder like the Prodigy's, at four poles; `MS20 LP` and `MS20 HP` with MS1, the MS-20's first Korg 35, and MS2, the later OTA one, for comparison. The Drop has nothing like the diode ladder or `Dirty`, so they follow PRD's curve, scaled to start from their own peak at Res 0 and reach PRD's just before it rings. **What's matched** is how far the peak stands over the passband three octaves away, against how far it stood at Res 0, so the taper is fitted and the filters' own shapes at Res 0 are left as they are; the ladder still thins the bass as a Moog's does, where PRD keeps it. **How:** `tools/compare` gains five `filter-taper-*` specs, written by `taper_specs.py`, which turn Res in 33 steps at 200 Hz, 988 Hz and 5 kHz, our knob's first 85% against The Drop's 0 to 100% and the rest against 100 to 125%, its knob's whole turn, and a `taper` report, which reads each step's sweep for the peak's height over the passband, its place, the bass and whether the filter is ringing by itself as the sweep ends. Each family's taper is 21 points a twentieth of the knob apart, read between them, of the share of its most resonance: what our old taper needed at each step to give The Drop's rise, to `CC`; then the share where it starts to ring at `D9`; then on to its most at `FF`, which is where it was, so full Res sounds as before and #337's tuning of where the diode rings still holds. The clean types never ring, so they climb from `CC` to their old highest peak, a Q of 10, at `FF`, which isn't raised, as nothing in the clean path would hold a higher one. A table was chosen over a formula as the curves have no common shape: the Korg 35 and the diode, whose feedback is on a cube, need to start fast, and the state-variable filter slowly. The Sound page draws from the same coefficients, so its curve follows. **Results,** the peak's height over the passband against The Drop's, up to its 96%, averaged over the three cutoffs: the clean low-pass from 5.1 dB apart on average to 1.4, and from 3.6 to 0.2 in how far it has risen from Res 0; the ladder from 1.3 to 0.2; `MS20 LP` from 4.5 to 0.7; the diode and `Dirty` from 5.2 and 4.1 to 0.4 and 0.7 against PRD's curve. Ladder, MS20 and diode now ring from Res `E0` at all three cutoffs, as The Drop's do, rather than `F0`; `Dirty` from `E0`, or `E8` at 5 kHz, where it's eased off. **Left as they are:** `MS20 HP` stands 3 to 4 dB higher over its passband than MS1's at every step, from Res 0, as its shape differs, though it rises as MS1's does; and the step just before ringing, `D8`, The Drop's 99.7%, differs most, by up to 18 dB, as both climb steeply there. Added on 2026-11-10.
121. **The `Acid` ignores velocity except as the accent's threshold: a note at `70` or above is accented, every plain note plays at one level and timbre whatever its velocity, and every accent from `70` to `7F` plays the same** (#295; the author: "it should not have velocity, just an accent threshold"). **Prior art:** a TB-303, whose steps are plain or accented with nothing between, and Arturia's Acid V, which plays it the same way. The author measured it through the harness on its Default preset with the sequencer off: velocities 10 to 99 render within 0.2 dB RMS of each other with the same brightness, and 100, 110 and 127 the same accent, about 2 dB RMS and 6 dB peak over a plain note. `tools/compare/specs/acid-velocity.toml` now plays ten velocities on both sides, a second apart, and reads each note's first 60 ms: Acid V's plain notes within a dB of each other over so short a window, and its accents 3.9 to 4.7 dB over them; ours were 24 dB apart from 10 to 127, and are now the same to 0.01 dB, with the accent 2.8 dB over them at the knob's noon. **The level:** a plain note plays as one at 99 did, where decision 117 fitted the level, and an accent as one at `70` did, where it fitted the accent, so both of decision 117's fitted points and its specs render bit for bit as before. A note at `7F`, or an empty Velocity cell, which plays at full, is about 1.1 dB quieter than it was, plain notes under 99 louder, and those from 100 to `6F` a little quieter. **The threshold** stays `70`, not Acid V's 100, as notes are written at `64`, 100, which must stay plain. **What uses velocity** does the same on the `Acid`: a groove's velocity, a sweep or a shape across the Velocity column, the `c` `v` ladder and the keyboard's `'` all only matter where they cross `70`. `RET`'s fade isn't velocity but the level of each hit, so it still fades and swells an `Acid`'s retriggers. Other voices are unchanged, their level still velocity's share of `7F`. Added on 2026-11-10. Decision 124 drops the exception for `RET`'s fade.
122. **The phrase view is laid out in time: a row of the loop takes a line for each of its pulses, a line being one step of the editing track's own `Speed`, and the other tracks' columns beside the grid line up by time rather than by row number; `z a` collapses it to a line a row** (#344, M18 "Time and feel"). The author noticed it playing the Blade test project, whose acid line is 7 rows over 16 pulses beside drums of 16 rows: matched by row number, the drums' rows `00` to `06` sat beside the acid's 7 rows, which read as a polyrhythm, while the beat and bar rules (decision 106) already went by time. **Prior art:** Renoise and the M8, where a line is a fixed unit of time; the Metropolix, whose stages are our rows, shows time in its overview; Elektron shows a track's scale on its page. **The line** is one step of the editing track's `Speed` (decision 82), so a row of N pulses takes N lines: its own, as before, and N-1 continuation lines. A continuation line is dim: a faint `│` under the note of a row that holds it (`SGL` and `HLD`), the note again, dim, for a `MLT`, which plays it on each pulse, and nothing for a rest or a row with no note. The FX band, a selection of the row and the rule under it carry on through it, so the row reads as one block, and its gutter has no numbers. A skipped row, of no pulses, keeps one line so it can be edited, and nothing starts beside it, as it takes no time. Rows outside the loop don't play, so they take a line each, at their numbers before the loop and on from its end after it. **Editing is unchanged:** the cursor, counts, selections, relative numbers and the view's `top` stay in rows, so `j` and `k` pass continuation lines by. Only the screen is worked out in lines, in `timeline.rs`: following the cursor keeps the whole of its row in view; `H`, `M` and `L` go to the top row, the row at the middle line and the last row whose lines all fit, so `L` never moves the view; `Ctrl-d` and `Ctrl-u` move by as many rows as pass half a screen of lines; and the view scrolls no further than the rows that fill the screen. **The other tracks:** each line shows, for each other track, the row it starts within that line's ticks, from its own rows' pulses and `Speed`, on the phrase its chain position gives, as its playhead does, with each track's row `00` lined up with this one's as before. A slower track leaves lines blank. Where more than one of a track's rows starts in a line, as a faster track's do, the first shows with `⋮`, U+22EE, in the cell before its note, which the playhead's `>` otherwise takes, so nothing is hidden without a sign. It's a plain character rather than a Nerd Font icon, as an icon there could be drawn over the note after it, with no room for `ICON_SPACE`; a column's width, and so the view's, is unchanged. A longer track still gets lines past the phrase's end, as it had rows. A selection reaching other tracks lights the rows it covers by row number, as that's how it edits them. **Playheads and beats:** the cursor's track's `>` is on the line of the pulse playing, so it walks down a long row, which is lit as before; another track's is on the line holding its place, beside its row's note where the row starts in that line, and alone where it started on an earlier one. The beat and bar rules and the yellow numbers go by each line's start, from the same scan of pulses decision 106 used, moved to `timeline::starts` and shared with the other tracks' columns, so a beat inside a long row is ruled under the continuation line before it. **The collapse key** is `z a`, Vim's key for toggling a fold, as the continuation lines are what a fold would hide; nothing was bound to `z` in the phrase view, Insert's `z` for the octave aside. Vim's `zM` and `zR`, close and open all, were left out, as there's one view-wide switch. Collapsed, the view is as it was: a line a row, the other tracks by row number. It lasts the session, starts laid out in time, and the status line says which it's in. When every row is one pulse and every track shown is at the same speed, the timeline is line for line what the rows were, so it only looks different where it differs, as the issue asked. **Left as they were:** an FX column with a lane of its own counts the track's steps, not its rows, so a lane's values stay on the rows' own lines; a `RATE` lock changes how long a step plays, but the lines go by the track's `Speed` alone; and a beat is still `BEAT_STEPS` of the track's own steps, so at `1/8` the rules mark every two of the song's beats. `just screenshots` redraws the gates and the gate list, whose phrase has `HLD3`, `MLT2`, `SGL0` and `RST2` rows. Added on 2026-11-10.
123. **Groups, the model and the engine: a sound track's `Group` puts it in one of four fixed group tracks, `G1` to `G4`, after the sound tracks and before the returns; a group sums its tracks and plays the sum through its own slots, level and pan to the master, with sends of its own, while each track's sends still go from the track; and stems render a group as one by default** (#188, part 1 of the author's table of 2026-11-10; the mixer strips and a group's track page are part 2, and marking and folding on the overview part 3). **Prior art:** Ableton's group tracks, whose tracks go to the group and the group to the master, with its own devices, mixer and sends, while each track's sends still leave from the track; Maschine's groups, each with its own FX over its sounds; Ableton's export, which renders a group track as one file and its tracks on request. **In the song:** a new `TrackKind::Group(n)`, numbered from 1, and four tracks of it in every song's list of tracks, as `kind: Group(1)` and so on, between the sound tracks and `A`, so `FIXED_TRACKS` is `MAX_GROUPS + 3`. `MAX_GROUPS` (4) is the one number: `TrackKind::FIXED` is built from it, and `ensure_fixed_tracks` now puts any fixed track a song lacks in its place, so a song saved with fewer groups gets the rest before its returns, with no chain in any section. A sound track has `group: Option<u8>`, saved only when set. `Song::group_track_of` finds a track's group, `Song::fixed_track` a fixed track's place, and `Song::heard` says whether a track plays by the saved mutes and solos, by the rules below. **The format version stays at 6**, as `group` has a default and the missing groups are put in on load; a side effect is that a song saved before this loads with four empty groups, which wasn't asked for and costs nothing. **Order and summing:** in `Engine::render`, the sound tracks first, each track's dry sound, after its level, pan and mute fade, into its group's sum or the master's; then each group, as a return is played, `TrackState::process_group`, which runs the sum through its slots, level, pan and mute fade, and sends from what comes out, after its level, as a track does; then the returns, with what the tracks and the groups send; then the master. The sums are a `[[f32; 2]; MAX_GROUPS]` on the stack, and each playing track's group is looked up once a run, so nothing is allocated, and a group with nothing coming in and nothing ringing in its slots does no work, as a return does. A track's sends are unchanged, so a snare can go to the reverb on its own while its kit goes through `G1`. **Mute and solo:** muting a group mutes its tracks, which are cut to their tails as a muted track's are, so their sends stop too; soloing a track in a group keeps that group heard, but not the group's other tracks; soloing a group plays all its tracks, except one muted itself; a group soloed with nothing in it plays nothing. While a sound track or a group is soloed, a group is heard only if it or one of its tracks is. While a return is soloed, a group is heard dry only if it or one of its tracks is soloed, and its sends still reach the returns, as a track's do. A track that joins or leaves a group while playing is heard by its new group's mute and solo at once. `Engine::swap` compares the old and new songs' groups with an iterator, so it doesn't allocate. **Groups aren't a `Source`:** a compressor's, Warps' or a gate's `Source` still names the sound tracks and the two returns, numbered as before; hearing a group, to duck a bass under the whole kit, is left for later. **`:group`:** `:group 1 1-4` puts tracks 01 to 04 in group 1, `:group 2` the cursor's track, and `:group off` or `:group off 1-4` takes them out of any, each one undo step through `Action::SetTrackGroup`, which takes a run of tracks and refuses a fixed track, so a group can't join a group. Tracks are numbered as the screen shows them, from 01. **On the track page:** a sound track's Sound page has `Group` under `Send B`, with the rest of its output column a row lower, as the track's output and where it goes; it shows `Off` or `G1` to `G4`, `=` and `-` step through them, `i` takes `2`, `g2` or `off`, and `x` takes the track out. **What part 1 gives a group to edit:** it's a fixed track like the returns, so `W` and `B` reach it, its Sound page has `Level`, `Pan`, `Send A` and `Send B`, its FX page its three slots, where a `Comp` with its `Type` on `Bus` (decision 118) is its glue, and its Mod page its envelopes and LFOs; the mixer shows it a strip between the sound tracks and the returns, with level, pan, sends, mute and solo, and the Song view a column, as it does `A`, `B` and `M`. Its icon is `nf-md-call_merge`, U+F00F8, tracks merging into one, and it's named `G1` and titled `Group 1`. **Stems:** `:render --stems` and `vimtracker render --stems` render each group with a track that would be heard as one stem, `G1.wav`, or `G1-DR.wav` once it's named, soloed so its tracks play through its slots and its sends, in place of a stem for each of them; a track in no group is its own stem as before. `--split` renders a stem of each track instead, those in groups too, each through its group's slots, and means `--stems`; it's `--split` rather than `--tracks` so that `--t` still completes to `--tail`. A muted track is left out of its group's stem, and a muted group has none. `RenderOptions` gains `each_track`. **Left out, for parts 2 and 3:** a group strip that shows its tracks, a track page made for a group, marking a group's tracks on the overview and folding them; and groups as a `Source`. Added on 2026-11-10. Decision 138 gives every track four slots, a group's among them.
124. **The `Acid` ignores `RET`'s fade and swell, so every retriggered hit plays at the note's own level, plain or accented; and its accent is fitted to Acid V's level, brightness and build-up across the whole `Accent` knob, about 4.7 dB over a plain note at noon and 9.8 dB full** (#295; the author, 2026-11-10). **Retriggers:** a 303 has no level but its accent, so decision 121's exception for `RET` goes: on the `Acid`, `RET`'s first digit changes nothing, as velocity changes nothing, and every hit is the same plain or accented note, rendering bit for bit as a steady `RET` does. The accent already carried on to every hit with the rest of the note, and still does. Other voices still fade and swell. **What was found:** with every knob at noon our accent was 2.8 dB RMS and 2.8 dB peak over a plain note, where Acid V's is 3.9 to 4.7 dB RMS and 4.4 to 6.0 dB peak (`acid-velocity`); across the knob our level climbed from 0.3 to 6.9 dB, Acid V's from about −0.5 to 9 to 10 dB, roughly even in dB from a quarter of the way up. Acid V's accent gets brighter only over the knob's top half, yet is as bright as a plain note at its start even at `00`, where ours was 0.4 octaves darker from its shorter envelope; and four accents a sixteenth apart end 0.15 to 0.4 octaves brighter than the first on Acid V, where ours didn't build at all at Res 50%, while accents with a sixteenth's rest between them build on neither. **The fit:** `acid-accent` and `acid-accent-apart`, each 17 steps of the knob, fitted by Nelder-Mead over the probe's renders against one set of Acid V's, weighing a dB of level as a tenth of an octave of brightness or build-up. The knob now scales the level and the cutoff on separate tapers, where one square scaled both: the level by the knob squared, up to 5.7 times the level envelope (from 5); the cutoff by 3.25 octaves at the sweep's height (from 5), times 0.18 plus the rest on the knob to the power 3.3, so `00` keeps a little. The sweep's capacitor keeps its 147k and 100k on 1 µF, which fitted as well as times of its own; what reaches the cutoff from it is 3.5 times what balances the pulse, so accents a sixteenth apart build and an eighth apart hardly do. **Before and after**, averaged over the knob's 17 steps, then the largest gap, from the harness's reports: accent level, AAAA 1.84 and 3.08 dB to 0.65 and 1.36, A.A. 1.44 and 3.52 to 0.68 and 1.35; brightness, AAAA 0.10 and 0.27 octaves to 0.03 and 0.09, A.A. 0.11 and 0.26 to 0.04 and 0.18; build-up, AAAA 0.25 and 0.35 octaves to 0.12 and 0.25, A.A. 0.02 and 0.06 to 0.04 and 0.11. Acid V renders differ from run to run by a few tenths of a dB, so these move a little between runs. **Kept:** a plain note is bit for bit as it was, −18.38 dBFS RMS on a C-2 at Cutoff `40` and the other knobs at noon; the accent's envelope stays the Decay knob's shortest, and the soft clip stays. **Where they still differ:** the soft clip rounds a full accent's peaks to about 5.6 dB over a plain note's where Acid V's reach 12, and at noon 3.8 dB to its 4.4 to 6.0; our build-up grows with the knob, from nothing at `00` to 0.6 octaves full, where Acid V's builds 0.15 octaves even at `00`, a carry-over the accent knob doesn't drive; and our level builds about 1.5 dB over four accents in a row, where Acid V's comes out either about 1 dB or about none, varying with the render. Added on 2026-11-10.
125. **The comparison harness compares ours with several references at once, and where Arturia's Acid V and AudioRealism's ABL3 agree, that's the Acid's target; where they disagree, both are shown and the measure is flagged, to be settled by ear or by the circuit; `run.py --suite nuance` runs M27's nuance specs and ranks our gaps from that consensus** (#347, M27 "Acid nuance"). **Why two:** one emulation's choices, a knob's taper or its accent's build-up, are indistinguishable from the circuit's, and decisions 117 and 124 fitted the Acid to Acid V alone; two independent models of the same circuit tell what a 303 does where they agree, and mark what's still a choice where they don't. Recordings of a real 303 or a clone would outrank both (#353). **Specs:** a spec's `[[references]]` lists them, each with a `name`, its plugin, parameters and state, and what it needs to be played the same: `channels`, `accent_from`, the velocity it accents from, and a `preroll` and `wake_note` for a plugin that needs one; a case changes each one's parameters by name under `references`, and can play `notes` of its own, which `vimtracker probe` now reads too, so a slide or a gate length is a case rather than a spec. A spec's single `[reference]` still works, as the first and only. **Accent and slide mean the same on every side:** a note's `accent = true` reaches each side at or over its own threshold, ours `70`, Acid V's 100 and ABL3's 101, and `false` under all of them; `slide = true` holds the note 1 ms into the next, which all three slide on. **ABL3 as a stock 303,** found by measuring its output: its Note mode (`MidiMode = 1` in its saved state; it loads in Pattern mode, where a key picks a pattern); trims at the 50% its manual says matches a TB-303, no drive, distortion or extra high-pass, its noise and flutter off, and its extra attack click off; mono; tuned as ours with no transposing; accented from velocity 101; sliding from 1 ms of overlap. After a reset it stays silent until it's played a middle C, and makes a burst of its own for 90 ms, so it renders after a second's pre-roll that starts with one. The details are in `tools/compare/README.md`. **The consensus:** for each measure and case, the references agree when they're within a tolerance: two semitones of frequency, a quarter of an octave of time, 1 dB, 0.1 octaves of brightness, a quarter of a semitone or 25 cents. Where they agree, their mean is the target, a log-mean for frequencies and times; our gap is ours less it, and its size in tolerances ranks it against gaps in other units, though a measure they agree on in fewer than a quarter of its cases isn't ranked. Levels each side sets for itself, such as a knob sweep's peak, are compared against the same side's at the knob's bottom. Each report gets a consensus table, and a knob sweep's plots a grey band where the two agree. **The suite:** decision 117's seven knob specs with ABL3 beside Acid V; accents `AAAA`, `A.A.` and `AA..` twice through at Res 0, 50% and 90%; slides up and down by a semitone, a fifth and an octave; two notes from 60 ms apart to 20 ms overlapping; a note held from a 128th to a quarter; and every knob at its bottom or top with every other's, 32 ways. Three new report kinds read them: `notes`, each note's brightness and level; `transition`, the pitch over time by YIN, the glide's times, the level's dip and the brightening a retrigger gives; and `gate`, the level as a note's held and its release. `nuance_specs.py` writes the specs. **Kept:** only outputs are compared, as decision 110 has it; the HTML reports stay out of git; no voice or engine changes, as #348 to #352 take the gaps this ranks. Added on 2026-11-10.
126. **Groups on the mixer and the track page: each group has a colour, which its strip's icon and name share with a `G1` under each of its tracks' strips; a group's strip lists the tracks it sums; a gap parts the sound tracks, the groups, and the returns with the master; a track muted or soloed by its group shows the glyph in the group's colour, where its own is green; a group's Sound page lists its tracks on the left and has its output on the right; an empty group is faint, not hidden; and a compressor's, Warps' or a gate's `Source` can be a group** (#188, part 2 of the author's table of 2026-11-10; marking a group's tracks on the overview and folding them are part 3). **Prior art:** Ableton's group tracks, a strip of their own before the returns, which can fold their tracks; Pro Tools' implicit mute, a track silenced by a solo elsewhere shown apart from one muted itself; Ableton's sidechain from a group track, which still hears the group while another track is soloed. **The colours:** `theme::group`, blue, peach, teal and mauve for `G1` to `G4`, far apart on the wheel and none the active green, coming round again past four so raising `MAX_GROUPS` needs nothing. They say which group, so they're the semantic kind of colour. **The mixer:** a fixed track's strip is named by its icon, `ICON_SPACE` and its name, a group's in its colour, bold under the cursor as before. A new row under the names has `G1` in the group's colour under each track in it, and under a group's name the tracks it sums, dim, numbered as the screen numbers them with a run of three or more as its ends, `01-04 07`, cut with `…` by its strip. A gap of two cells comes before the first group's strip and before `A`'s, and room is kept for both whether they're in view or not, so the number of strips doesn't change as the cursor scrolls. The meters start a row lower. **Mute and solo:** a sound track whose group is muted shows the mute glyph in the group's colour, and its strip dims as a muted track's does; one muted itself has the glyph in the active colour, as before. A solo is the same. The track page's title and the overview's column labels show them too, with a track's own over its group's and a mute over a solo, as the mute is what's heard. A group isn't marked when a track in it is soloed; it's heard, but nothing changed on it. **A group's Sound page:** `Tracks` down the left, where a sound track's machine is, each by its number and name with its own mute or solo after it, dim while it's muted, or `None`; a list longer than the page ends with `+N more`. `Level`, `Pan`, `Send A` and `Send B` move to the right, in the output column a sound track has them in, so a group's level is where its tracks' are. The title's icon is in the group's colour, and so is a sound track's `Group` value. Putting tracks in a group from this page is left for later, as `:group` and a track's `Group` do it. **Empty groups** are faint, on the mixer (the name and the settings) and in the song view (the column's name and its chains), rather than hidden, so the strips and columns keep their places and a group is there to fill. Hiding them belongs with part 3's folding: a folded group would be one column or strip, and an empty one could be folded away entirely, with the cursor's moves stepping over it as Vim's step over a closed fold. **Groups as a `Source`:** `SOURCE_NAMES` gains `G1` to `G4` after `A` and `B`, so the returns keep 65 and 66 and saved songs hear what they did; the groups are 67 to 70, so in the choices they come after the returns, out of the tracks' order. A group is heard after its slots and before its level, as a track and a return are. As a muted kick still ducks a bass, a group a compressor hears has its tracks keep playing into it while it's muted or soloed out: a sound track has a second fade, `feeds`, for what it gives its group, which leaves the group's own mute and the solos outside it to the group's fade, so what's heard is unchanged and a group nothing hears plays bit for bit as before. **A fade with nothing to fade settles:** a silent sound track's mute fade, and a group's or return's with nothing coming in, go straight to where they're going, so a muted track or group a compressor hears doesn't start its next sound with the end of a fade, which a muted track could before. Added on 2026-11-10.
127. **The `Acid`'s slide, accent and gate are fitted to where Acid V and ABL3 agree, and where they disagree to Robin Whittle's measurements of a 303: a plain note holds its level for 8 ms after its gate closes and then falls in a straight line to nothing over 8 more; a slide down takes 1.3 times as long as one up; the accent's sweep builds about a third as far as decision 124's, its envelope is 70 ms or the plain note's if that's shorter, and it adds nothing with `Accent` at 00; and the soft clip after the voice levels off 12 dB over full scale, so a full accent's peaks stand 12 to 14 dB over a plain note's** (#350, M27 "Acid nuance"). **Why:** decision 124 fitted the accent to Acid V alone, and decision 125's suite ranked our gaps from where Acid V and ABL3 agree. **Prior art:** the Devil Fish manual's notes on the TB-303 (Whittle): the end of a plain note is "about 16 ms – 8 ms of normal volume and 8 ms of linear decay"; the slide is the DAC's 100k into 0.22 µF, a 22 ms time constant; the accent's filter envelope is the Decay pot's shortest; and only the VEG drives the VCA, with a decay of 3 to 4 s, the accent sweep circuit adding to it. **How:** `run.py --suite nuance` before and after, and Nelder-Mead over the accent's constants against the consensus of `nuance-knob-accent`, `nuance-knob-accent-apart`, `nuance-accents` and `nuance-extremes`, every measure the two references agree on weighed by its tolerance, with a full accent's peak over a plain note's held to at least 12 dB; only outputs were compared. **The gate:** a plain note's level is held for 8 ms once its gate closes, then falls in a straight line to nothing over 8 ms, where its level and filter-envelope share are left at nothing, so the next note starts from rest; a note played again inside the hold carries on from its level. An accented note still falls with its 50 ms time constant, which the suite doesn't measure. With the track's note shape, whose 50 ms release still applies after it, the harness reads 20 dB down in about 17 ms and 40 dB in about 22, where Acid V and ABL3 read 15 to 22 and 22 to 27 on held notes, and ours was 8 to 12 and 17 to 20. **Where they disagreed, short notes:** held for a 128th to a 16th, Acid V's level takes 36 to 64 ms to fall 20 dB and 83 to 156 ms to fall 40, ABL3's 17 to 18 and 28 to 48; Whittle's 16 ms holds for any plain note, so ours ends every plain note the same way, nearer ABL3. **A 5 ms gap** now dips our level 1.4 dB, from 3.2, where ABL3's dips 0.1 and Acid V's 0.5 to 2.7 from run to run; what's left is the track's note shape releasing for those 5 ms, which every voice has and a 303 hasn't, left as it is. **Slides:** `Slide` at 00 was already near Whittle's 22 ms, 20 ms, and up both references and ours reach 90% in 45 to 55 ms; but both references take a fifth to a quarter longer down, 51 to 59 ms down a fifth or an octave where ours took 42, which an RC on the pitch alone doesn't explain, so a slide down's time constant is 1.3 times its own. A slid note's brightness over the 30 ms after the change was 0.07 octaves under the references', now 0.04, which is the filter envelope's fall at 250 ms rather than the slide. **Where they disagreed, the level across a slide:** the note slid to is 8 to 10 dB under the first on Acid V and 3 to 5 on ABL3; with only the VEG's 3 to 4 s decay driving the VCA and the gate held through the slide, Whittle's 303 loses little, so ours is left at 4 to 5 dB, with ABL3. **The accent:** the sweep's gain is 1.05 times what balances the pulse, from 3.5, and Res turns the accent from the pulse to the capacitor's swell as the knob to the power 1.4, from 1, so at full Res four accents a sixteenth apart build about 0.4 octaves, as both references do, where ours built 1.3; and with all knobs at noon but `Accent` full, 0.09 octaves, where both references' build 0.03 to 0.08 and ours built 0.62. The knob's cutoff taper is the knob to the power 2.7 times 3.33 octaves, with no share left at `00`, where 18% was, as neither reference's accent is brighter at `00` than its envelope makes it; the accent's level is 7 times the knob squared, from 5.7. **The accent's envelope** is 70 ms, or the plain note's when `Decay` makes that shorter, rather than `Decay`'s shortest, 40 ms: with the 18% gone, a 40 ms accent at `Accent` 00 was 0.2 octaves darker than both references', and at `Decay` 00 both play an accent at `Accent` 00 the same as a plain note, which the cap keeps. Whittle gives the shortest as 200 ms, which as a time constant is about the same. **Velocity:** an accented note now plays at a plain note's velocity, 99, rather than at `70`'s, as on a 303 the accent circuit is all that makes an accent louder; at `Accent` 00 it was 1.1 dB louder than a plain note, where both references' are 0.4 to 0.9 dB quieter. **Headroom (the comment on #350):** a full accent peaked only 5.6 dB over a plain note, as the soft clip, which levelled off at full scale, rounded it off; Acid V's peak 12 dB over and ABL3's 13 to 15, both well over full scale. The soft clip now levels off at 4, 12 dB over full scale, and a full accent's peaks reach 12 to 13.5 dB over a plain note's; the clip had been taking 1 dB off a plain note's peaks too, so `LEVEL` goes from 0.75 to 0.7121 and a plain C-2 at Cutoff `40` and the other knobs at noon stays at −18.38 dBFS RMS over its first 60 ms. The track's level, slots and the master's limiter come after it, as for any voice. **Results,** the suite's consensus, mean signed gap, before and after: the extremes' first accent, +2.55 dB to −0.12 and +0.21 octaves to 0.00; the first accent at Res 50% and 90%, +0.20 octaves to +0.02; build-up over the extremes, +0.17 octaves to −0.02; a slide's 90% time, −0.21 octaves to 0.00, and 10 to 90%, −0.13 to 0.00; the gate's release to 20 dB, −0.94 octaves to +0.17, and to 40 dB, −0.46 to −0.05, each where the references agree. **Still apart:** the level builds about 0.5 dB too much over four accents in a row, and 0.7 dB over the extremes' two at full Res, as the swell that brightens later accents also adds to their level; at full `Accent` a first accent is 2 dB louder than the 8.7 to 8.9 dB both references give, on the two of 17 steps they agree on, as the fit weighed the 32 extremes more; and a plain note's brightness in the extremes is 0.25 octaves under the consensus, the cutoff and envelope's, not this issue's. Acid V renders still vary by a few tenths from run to run. **Tests:** the voice's slide reaches 90% up an octave, a fifth and a semitone in 42 to 50 ms and down in 55 to 65; a plain note's level holds for 6 ms after release, is half down at 12 and gone by 18; a note played again 5 ms after it's let go never dips more than 3%, and 20 ms after starts from nothing; the build-up test now pins a third to a half of an octave rather than over 0.8; and the voice's samples stay within its ceiling of 4 rather than 1. Added on 2026-11-10.
128. **Groups on the overview: a group and its tracks are named in its colour wherever tracks are listed, and a grouped track's title carries its `G1`; in the song view and the mixer, `z a` folds a group's tracks into its own column or strip, or opens it, `zM` folds every group and `zR` opens every one; the cursor steps over folded tracks; an empty group is folded away until a track joins it; and folds are a view, lasting the session and not saved** (#188, part 3 of the author's table of 2026-11-10, M23; parts 1 and 2 are decisions 123 and 126). **Prior art:** Ableton's group tracks, which fold their tracks away in the arrangement, the session view and the mixer, and save the fold in the set; Vim's folds, whose `za` toggles the fold the cursor is in, closing an open one from any line in it, whose `zM` and `zR` close and open every fold, whose cursor stands on a closed fold's line, and whose `foldopen` opens a fold that a jump lands in, and which are lost when the file closes unless `:mkview` saves them; the explorer's tree, which marks a folder open or closed rather than drawing an arrow (decision 103). **Marking the tracks:** the song view's column names, the phrase view's labels over the other tracks' columns (decision 122's timeline among them) and the track list's names (`Space f b`) are in the group's colour, a group's own as well, as its strip and the `G1` under its tracks' strips are on the mixer (decision 126). Those labels are two or three cells, with no room for a `G1` beside them, so colour is the mark; an empty group is still faint. The chain view lists one track, in its title, so its title, the phrase view's and the track page's put the `G1` mark after the name, in the colour, from one `group::mark`, which the mixer's strip uses too. **The keys:** `z a`, `z M` and `z R`, Vim's, in the song view and the mixer, where nothing was bound to `z`. `z a` on a group folds or opens it, and on a track in one folds its group, as Vim's `za` closes the open fold the cursor is in; on a track in no group, or the song view's first three columns, it says so. `zM` folds every group, and `zR` opens every one, the empty ones too, which then show faint. The phrase view has none: its `z a` collapses the timeline, so `zM` and `zR` there would mean a different fold from it; it shows the folds made in the other two. `z o` and `z c` were left out, as `z a` does both. **Showing a fold:** a folded group's tracks are left out of the song view and the mixer, and its name gets `nf-md-arrow_collapse_horizontal`, U+F084C, two arrows pushed together, with `ICON_SPACE` after it, in its colour: `G1` in the song view, where the four cells of a column just hold it, and after the icon and name on the mixer. Only a folded group is marked, as Vim shows only a closed fold. In the phrase view's overview, a folded group has a column of its own where its first track's would be, its name and the mark over it and nothing under it, as a group's notes aren't shown there, and its tracks' columns are left out, but for one being edited. On the mixer the gaps go between the parts that are shown, so a part that's all folded away leaves no gap of its own. **The order:** unchanged. Ableton draws a group's tracks next to it, but here the groups stay after the sound tracks and the tracks keep their numbers and places, so a fold only hides its tracks, and an open group's tracks are where they always are, told by their colour. **The cursor:** `h`, `l`, `0` and `$` in the song view and on the mixer, and `W` and `B` in every view, step over folded tracks, counts counting only those shown, so `W` from the last track on the track page goes past empty groups to `A`. A fold closing over the cursor puts it on the group, as Vim's stands on a closed fold, or for an empty group, on the next column shown, or else the one before: the song view's column, or the mixer's track. The track the other views are on is left as it was, so a phrase being edited stays in front of the phrase view when its group is folded in the song view; it moves to the group if the mixer is shown on it. After every key, a song view or mixer cursor left on a folded track, by `:group` putting the track in a folded group, an undo or a jump, stands on its group the same way. The track list is the way in: choosing a folded track there opens its group, as Vim's `foldopen` does for a jump. **Empty groups:** a group no key has folded or opened is folded while it has no tracks and open once it has, so the four empty groups every song has are out of the way, the cursor goes past them, and a track that joins one is never put away in a fold. `z a` or `zM` folding a group, or `zR` and `z a` opening it, sets it, after which it stays as set while tracks come and go. **Not saved:** folds are in `App::folds`, not in the song, so folding isn't a change, needs no undo and doesn't mark the song unsaved. They last the session, and opening another project, or the same one again, starts it with its groups open and its empty ones folded away. Keeping them for each project was decided against: the default is what a project should open with, nothing else about how the views show a song is kept between sessions (the timeline's collapse, decision 122, the cursor and the jump list all go), and there's no store of view state for a project, which this alone would need, keyed by its folder in the cache, where Vim also needs `:mkview`. `just screenshots` draws nothing new, as no shot shows a group. **Left for later:** folding from the phrase view, and showing what a folded group's tracks are doing in its column. Added on 2026-11-10.
129. **The `Acid`'s chirp, its resonant peak sweeping down at high Res, is measured over time against Acid V and ABL3, and its filter is driven a third as hard, with less feedback, so the peak stays as high through the note as both references' and the filter falls evenly past it rather than off a cliff; the sweep's start and speed, and the accent's punch, are left as they were** (#348, #349, M27 "Acid nuance"). **Why:** the author, at high Res: "if we are getting the chirpy sounds in a similar way? ours feels a bit muted", and the suite had our peak at full Res about 2 dB short. **The measure:** `tools/compare`'s new `chirp` report follows each note's resonant peak every 2 ms from pitch-synchronous spectra, two cycles of the note through a Hann window, which puts each harmonic on its neighbours' zeros, each harmonic taken times its number so a sawtooth's are level: the peak's place, its height over the fundamental and over the harmonics an octave and more under it, the level an octave over it, and the note's first 20 ms over the 40 after, each the median over a window, as the height wavers about a dB as the peak crosses the harmonics. The harmonics are read at each side's own tuning, 3.8 cents sharp for ABL3, as a pitch tracker's reading moves with the sweeping filter's phase. `nuance-chirp` plays a plain and an accented C-2 at Res 75, 85, 95 and 100%, Env Mod 50 and 100%, Cutoff 20 and 50% and Decay 20 and 80%, 64 cases, and `nuance-chirp-riff` the Blade riff's Acid line from `confusion-pump-panel` once through; both are in `run.py --suite nuance`. **What it found:** at high Res the references disagree on the peak's height more often than not, Acid V's standing 2 to 3 dB over ABL3's, so where they disagree ours is compared with each. Our peak stood about 21 dB over the fundamental for the note's first 20 ms at Res 85%, Env Mod full and Decay long, and then sank to about 16, where Acid V's held at 19.5 and ABL3's at about 19; and past the peak our filter fell off a cliff, 17 dB in one harmonic, where both fall about 4 a harmonic. Rendered with almost nothing into it, our ladder has the references' even fall, so it was the saturation at its first stage, which Vital's ladder drives harder as the resonance rises: it flattened the peak once the resonance had built up and cut the harmonics just over it, which is muted. **The change:** the saw goes into the filter at 0.1 rather than 0.3, so a 303's level is nearly linear there and `Drive`'s 24 dB still pushes it into the saturation and the clip; and as the peak then stood far higher, the feedback is less, up to 0.68 of the ladder's most from 0.8, on the knob to the power 1.2 from 1.1. Both were fitted by Nelder-Mead over the probe's renders against the chirp's consensus, the peak's heights and the level an octave over it, with every ranked gap in the rest of the suite held to no worse than it was. `LEVEL` goes from 0.7121 to 0.6768, as the saturation takes less of a plain note, so a plain C-2 at Cutoff `40` and the other knobs at noon stays at −18.38 dBFS RMS over its first 60 ms. **Results,** ours less Acid V and less ABL3, averaged over the 64 cases, before and after, with the same reference renders: the level an octave over the peak, under it, −7.9 and −5.6 dB to −2.5 and −0.2, and on the riff −6.2 and −3.9 to −2.2 and +0.1; the peak over the fundamental at 20 ms, −2.6 and 0.0 to −2.3 and +0.3; at 60 ms, −2.2 and +0.7 to −1.4 and +1.5; at 150 ms, −0.5 and −0.3 to +0.7 and +0.9; and over the passband, −0.2 and +0.2 to +0.8 and +1.2, so ours now sits between the two through the note, a little over both after 100 ms. On the riff, where the references agree, the peak at 20 ms went from 0.5 dB under them to 0.1, and at 60 ms from 1.1 under to level with them. The suite's top ranked gap, the bass under a ringing filter at Res 90% against the knob's bottom, went from 3.2 dB too much to 1.1 too little, and the extremes' first note's brightness from 0.24 octaves dark to 0.17; full Res's peak over the bass, unranked, from 2.0 dB short to 1.8. Five ranked measures moved the other way by 0.07 to 0.13 of their tolerances, none past it: an accent's brightness over a plain note and four accents' build-up, by about 0.01 octaves, and the brightening at a retrigger or a slide, 0.01. The Blade riff's Acid track renders 0.7 dB louder, −18.5 dBFS RMS, its peaks 0.8 dB higher. **Left as they were, the sweep:** both references start the peak about 0.36 octaves lower than ours, 20 ms in, and fall more slowly at first, ours falling 0.46 octaves more than Acid V's and 0.70 more than ABL3's from 20 to 120 ms; theirs is slow at the top of its sweep and speeds up as it falls, where an exponential envelope through our ladder slows as it falls. A narrower sweep centred lower, 0.8 times as wide around half of Open303's centre, closes the start and the fall, from 0.36 octaves high to 0.04 low, but then a retriggered note's brightening and an accent's brightness part from both references, which agree on them, by four times their gaps: their peaks start lower yet sound as bright, which points at how the ladder's response changes at high cutoffs rather than at the envelope, so it's left for #352, the circuit. **Left as it was, the accent's punch:** at high Res both references' accents are about 1 dB louder over their first 20 ms than over the 40 after, and ours about 1 dB quieter, as Res turns our accent's level, and not only its cutoff, from the pulse to the capacitor's slower swell (decision 127). Taking the level from the pulse at every Res, as Open303 does, gives the punch, halves the level's build-up over four accents, from 0.5 dB too much to 0.2, and fixes the riff's accented starts, 1.5 dB short to 0.1; but at full Res an accent's first 60 ms comes out 10.5 to 14.2 dB over a plain note, where the references give 6.2 to 10.7 and the swell 6.3 to 8.3, so the extremes' first accent parts further from them, and it's left for the accent's next pass. **Kept:** only outputs are compared; nothing on the audio thread allocates; the knobs' ranges, the envelope and the accent are as they were. **Tests:** the peak stands 14.8 to 17.5 dB over the fundamental over a note's first 200 ms at Res 85% and Env Mod at noon, where it was 14.2, and 18 to 22.5 at Res 95% with Env Mod full; the harmonic an octave over the peak is less than 29 dB under it at two settings, where it was 30 to 34; and at Res 95% the peak starts between 4.5 and 7 kHz and is between 1.4 and 2.2 kHz at 150 ms. The gate-length test that finds where an Acid note falls 40 dB now reads a cycle of the note at a time rather than a millisecond: the cleaner resonance leaves a millisecond's quiet between a C-2's peaks 3 ms after the gate, which it took for the end; the note still ends about 16 ms after. Added on 2026-11-10.
130. **`Distort` gets two guitar pedals as types, after its six waveshapers: `Overdrive`, a Tube Screamer's circuit, and `Distortion`, an op-amp distortion fitted to Arturia's Dist OPAMP-21, each with a `Tone`; a `Tube` after TUBE-CULTURE, and a fuzz, are left for later** (#361). **Why:** acid records rarely use a bare 303; it usually goes through a guitar pedal, and a pedal's sound comes mostly from the filtering around its clipper, which `Soft`, `Hard`, `Fold`, `Sine`, `Crush` and `Down` have none of. As the compressor got its types (#338, decision 118), each pedal is its own model tuned against a reference rather than one average. **The order:** the pedals come after `Down`, so a song's types keep their places. They show `Type`, `Amount`, `Tone`, `Mix` and `Gain`; `Tone` is a new setting every `Distort` keeps, `tone` in songs, `TONE` on the grid, `80` when it starts, so it can be locked and modulated as the others are. **`Overdrive`:** an Ibanez TS-808's clipping and tone stages, from ElectroSmash's "Tube Screamer Analysis" and Yeh, Abel and Smith's "Simplified, physically-informed models of distortion and overdrive guitar effects pedals" (DAFx-07), with the schematic's values. The op-amp's gain branch, 4.7k and 47 nF to ground, passes only what's over 720 Hz; its gain, the feedback's 51k and `Amount`'s 500k log pot over the 4.7k, from 21 to 41 dB, is clipped by two 1N914s across the feedback, solved exactly by Newton's method, with the feedback's 51 pF taking the highs off as it rises; and what's clipped is added to the clean sound, so the bass passes clean under clipped mids, the Tube Screamer's mid hump and its tight bass. Then the 723 Hz low-pass, 1k and 220 nF, and the tone stage, which `Tone` turns from putting none of the highs back at `00` to one and a half times them at `FF`, and a roll-off at 12 kHz. Full scale stands for half a volt, a 303's volt from peak to peak, and its `Level` is set so the Blade riff of `confusion-pump-panel`, about -16 dBFS RMS, comes out as loud as it went in at the defaults. **No reference for it:** none of the plugins here is a Tube Screamer. Acid V's own `Overdrive`, isolated as the change it makes to its own held, open note, cuts the 4th to 6th harmonics of an A-2, 440 to 660 Hz, by 5 to 9 dB and lifts the top, the opposite of a mid hump, so it's recorded in `pedal-acid-v` rather than fitted; `pedal-overdrive` measures ours alone. **`Distortion`:** an op-amp distortion's structure, high gain, a clip, then a tone low-pass, as ElectroSmash's "ProCo RAT Analysis" lays it out, with its gain, clip, offset and level fitted to Dist OPAMP-21, after Tech 21's SansAmp, in its `Modern` mode, which Arturia describes as having no colouring or EQ, with its post EQ off. Its gain is 17.5 to 43.1 dB over `Amount`, so `Amount`'s place is OPAMP-21's `Drive`: `40` is 25%, `80` 50%, `FF` 100%. Its clip is far harder than a diode's, flat at its ceiling however hard it's driven, so it's `x / (1 + |x|^10)^(1/10)`; it sits 0.086 of its ceiling off centre, as OPAMP-21's does, whose 2nd harmonic is 28 dB under the fundamental just over the clip and falls as it's driven harder; it's preceded by two 22 Hz high-passes, as OPAMP-21's bass falls; and its ceiling is OPAMP-21's, 0.157, raised 3 dB to 0.22 so the riff comes out as loud as it went in. `Tone` is a 12 dB an octave Butterworth low-pass from 500 Hz to 12 kHz, gentler than OPAMP-21's `Normal` voicing and steeper than a RAT's `Filter`. Both pedals end with a 1.3 Hz coupling high-pass, which takes out the offset an uneven sound's clipping leaves, as OPAMP-21's output falls back from one over about 120 ms. **Aliasing:** both clip at four times the rate, two of the shared halfbands in a row (`QuadrupleOversampler`); `Distortion` at full drive on a 5 kHz sine still folds its 7th harmonic back 36 dB under the fundamental, which a 303 line, whose notes are far lower, doesn't reach. **Results** (`tools/compare`, 48 kHz, OPAMP-21 at `buffer_size=512`): the Blade riff through `Distortion` against OPAMP-21's `Modern`, the same samples into both (`pedal-riff`), has a spectrum whose shape is 0.6 to 0.7 dB apart over the third octaves from 40 Hz to 16 kHz at 25, 50 and 100% `Drive`, widest at 16 kHz, where OPAMP-21 is 1.7 to 2.0 dB brighter; ours is 2.7 to 2.9 dB louder, the ceiling's 3 dB; its crest factor is 0.5 to 0.9 dB more, its level over time 2.4 to 3.2 dB apart once the overall difference is out, and its quietest moments, the gaps between notes, 7 to 10 dB louder. On stepped sines (`pedal-distortion-harmonics`) ours sits 2.4 to 3.2 dB over OPAMP-21 at every level, so its curve's shape is within 0.7 dB through the knee; fully clipped, both put the 3rd harmonic 9.5 to 9.6 dB under the fundamental, and at 0 dBFS the 2nd 44.9, 51.3 and 64.0 dB under at the three drives, against OPAMP-21's 44.9, 52.4 and 66.6. Under the clip (`pedal-distortion-response`), ours with `Tone` open is within 0.6 dB of `Modern` from 31 Hz to 8 kHz once the 2.1 to 2.9 dB level difference is out; at our default `Tone` against `Normal`, within 1 dB up to 4 kHz and 5.8 dB brighter at 8 kHz, as its speaker falls far more steeply, which leaves the riff's shape 6.7 dB apart from `Normal`'s, most at 16 kHz. Acid V's own distortions brighten its held, open A-2 more than ours do theirs (`pedal-acid-v`): its spectral centroid goes from 368 Hz to 910 Hz through its `Overdrive` at 50% and to 1460 Hz through its `Distorsion`, and ours from 390 Hz to 651 and 820 at `Amount` `80`. The riff is -16.05 dBFS RMS going in, -16.05 out of `Overdrive` and -16.50 out of `Distortion` at the defaults; `FF` adds 2.9 and 2.0 dB. **Left for later:** a `Tube` type: Arturia's Dist TUBE-CULTURE, after Thermionic Culture's Culture Vulture, in its triode mode, clips with a 2nd harmonic about 7 dB under the fundamental over 20 dB of level while its output stays flat, and its `Bias` makes it uneven even quiet; no static curve came within 3 dB RMS of its harmonics over its `Drive` and `Bias`, so it needs the bias to move with the level, as a grid's current charging its coupling capacitor does, for a follow-up. A fuzz: Dist COLDFIRE's `Germanium` module measures as a soft, uneven fuzz whose output falls as it's pushed past -6 dBFS; it's left until a fuzz is wanted. And the steeper speaker roll-off of OPAMP-21's `Normal` mode, as an option on `Tone`. **Kept:** only outputs are compared; nothing on the audio thread allocates; with nothing going in, a pedal that's rung out rests at an exact zero, so no filter decays into denormals; the six waveshapers are as they were, bit for bit. Added on 2026-11-10.
131. **The `Acid`'s envelope pushes the cutoff less for each octave it asks for over where the knob puts it, along a tanh 7.3 octaves wide, so with Env Mod full the chirp starts where Acid V's and ABL3's do, about half an octave lower than before, and falls less steeply at first; Env Mod at noon hardly changes** (#352, M27 "Acid nuance"). **Why:** decision 129 left the sweep: averaged over `nuance-chirp`'s 64 cases, our peak 20 ms in sat 0.40 octaves over Acid V's and 0.36 over ABL3's, and fell from 20 to 120 ms 0.44 octaves more than Acid V's and 0.67 more than ABL3's. **What was measured first,** each against the suite's reference renders: our diode ladder resonates where it's told to, within 0.06 octaves from 200 Hz to 12.8 kHz at Res 95% and over, so the ladder isn't where the start goes wrong; a compression of the cutoff at high frequencies, weighted to high Res, fitted on top of the change here, gained nothing more; the gap is at full Env Mod, where on plain notes both references start 0.28 to 0.41 octaves under ours and, from Cutoff noon, are back where ours is about 45 ms in, while at noon they disagree, Acid V under ours and ABL3 over; and a scratch render at Res 0 to 100% shows both references' brightness falling more slowly than ours over a note's first 120 ms at every Res, so the shape isn't the resonance's. Fitting an exponential to each side's peak over time, both references' sweeps at full Env Mod start 0.5 to 0.8 octaves under where ours would at its start and fall more slowly. **What else was tried:** smoothing the envelope's path to the cutoff with an RC, as Open303's `rc1` can (Open303 leaves it at 0 ms), lowers the start and slows the first fall, but at 8 ms the brightening when a note is played again, 2 to 32 ms after it, parts from both references by twice its gap, and an accent's brightness over a plain note by four times, as the lag takes the top off every note's start; a narrower sweep with Env Mod full alone darkens the extremes' first note, ranked, by three quarters of a tolerance; and a longer decay slows the fall but lifts the peak's height at 150 ms and parts the brightening. **The change:** `softened_sweep` gives the envelope's sweep its octaves as they were under the knob's cutoff, and `7.3 tanh(octaves / 7.3)` over it, so Env Mod at noon's two octaves lose 0.04 at their top and Env Mod full's five lose 0.57. It models the control path running short of headroom on a large swing, its shape fitted where Acid V and ABL3 agree: the plain notes with Env Mod full.
