A plain C-2 at Cutoff `40` with the other knobs at noon stays at −18.38 dBFS RMS over its first 60 ms. **Results,** ours less Acid V and less ABL3, averaged over the 64 cases, before and after, against the same reference renders: the peak's place at 20 ms, +0.40 and +0.36 octaves to +0.24 and +0.21, and on the 13 cases where they agree, +0.29 to +0.09; with Env Mod full on plain notes, +0.28 and +0.41 to −0.06 and +0.08; the fall from 20 to 120 ms, +0.44 and +0.67 to +0.31 and +0.54; on the Blade riff, the start +0.27 and +0.20 to +0.21 and +0.15, and the fall +0.48 and +0.33 to +0.41 and +0.26. The heights and skirt decision 129 fitted move by 0.05 dB or less on average. Ranked, the extremes' first note's brightness goes from 1.89 to 1.67, the retrigger's brightening from 0.49 to 0.59 and four accents' first accent's brightness from 0.26 to 0.35, each about 0.01 octaves; nothing else moves by more than 0.06. **Left open:** with Env Mod full, an accented note's peak still starts 0.37 octaves over Acid V's and 0.82 over ABL3's, which disagree with each other on every case, for the accent's own pass; and from 20 ms on, plain notes with Env Mod full still fall 0.38 and 0.62 octaves further than the references' by 120 ms, which sit 0.45 to 0.6 octaves over ours by 150 ms, both falling as if their Decay were longer than decision 117 fitted Acid V's brightness at Res 0 to, which a longer decay can't follow without parting the brightening and the heights. **Tests:** at Res 95% with Env Mod full, Cutoff noon and Decay long, the peak starts between 3.7 and 4.8 kHz, where it started at 5.8, and falls 0.8 to 1.2 octaves from 20 to 120 ms, where it fell 1.37; at Cutoff 20% it starts between 2.2 and 3 kHz, where it started at 3.5; and `softened_sweep` leaves a sweep under the knob as it is, takes under 0.05 octaves from Env Mod noon's and 0.5 to 0.65 from Env Mod full's, and always rises. Added on 2026-11-10.
132. **`Comp`'s `Gain` becomes `Makeup`, in dB: 0 dB at `00` to +24 dB at `FF`, evenly, as the limiter's `Input` runs, labelled `MKUP` and read as `+12.0 dB`. It turns up what the compressor turned down, after `Auto` and before `Mix`, and `Bus` gets `Auto` too, both shared by every type, current and to come** (the author's report that the bus compressor only made things quieter, and #338). **Why:** `Gain` was the level scale every slot ends with, `E0` unity and `F8` its top, so it could make up at most 6 dB, where a bus compressor working hard, or `Clean` at a high ratio, takes more, and `Bus` had nothing else to turn it back up. **Prior art:** Bus FORCE's compressor make-up runs from 0 to +30 dB and sits on its compressor path, beside its dry path, as its parameter list has it; the limiter's `Input` here is 0 to +24 dB over `00` to `FF`. **Range and mapping:** +24 dB, as `Input` has, so the two dB boosts on the dynamics slots read alike: a step is about 0.09 dB, `40` is +6.0 dB, `80` +12.0, `C0` +18.1, and `FF` exactly +24. Bus FORCE's last 6 dB are left out, as Auto gives back any amount. It starts at `00`, unity, and goes no lower: the track's level turns a track down, and a makeup that cuts would be a second fader. Like `Gain`, it's locked by its label and an LFO moves it over its whole range, glided so nothing clicks. **Where:** on what the compressor turned down, before `Mix`, as Bus FORCE's make-up is on its compressor path, so compressing in parallel turns up the squashed copy under the dry one; with `Mix` full, as it starts, it's the same as a gain after. The compressor has no level-scale `Gain` now, the one slot that doesn't end with one, and the FX page's last column of names is two characters wider to fit `Makeup`. **Auto on `Bus`:** Auto compares what goes in with what comes out of the type over about a second, which doesn't depend on how the type turns down, so it moved out of `Clean` into the compressor every type passes through, and `Bus` shows it between its release and `Mix`, where `Clean` has it. Decision 118 left it off `Bus` because the G-bus has a makeup knob and no more, but that knob reached only 6 dB here, and Bus FORCE has an auto gain switch of its own. With it on, `Makeup` adds its dB on top: a 1 kHz tone held 24 dB over a -30 dB threshold comes out within 0.25 dB of what went in with Auto, and within 0.25 dB of `Makeup`'s +12.0 dB over that at `80`, at 2:1, 4:1 and 10:1. `Clean` sounds as it did: with Auto off, a slot plays bit for bit as before, and the golden renders are unchanged. **`Bus` under its threshold is unity:** `comp-bus-static`, at a -20 dB threshold, has ours and Bus FORCE's compressor path both giving back exactly what went in, to 0.00 dB, at every step from -60 to -20 dB and at each ratio. The first turning down is at -19 dB at 2:1, 0.02 dB in each, and at -18 dB at 4:1 and 10:1, where Bus FORCE takes 0.13 and 0.16 dB and ours nothing yet. The line's fitted offset, 0.25 to 2.5 dB, moves where the curve bends up, never down, so nothing was wrong there: a bus compressor sounded quieter only because it couldn't be made up. **No backwards compatibility:** a song's compressor `gain` is ignored on loading, so it plays at 0 dB makeup, but a song with a lock or modulation of a compressor's `gain` won't load, saying there's no setting called, say, `1.comp.gain`, as with any setting that's gone. **Tests:** `makeup_db` reaches +24 dB exactly at `FF` and gives 0 at `00`; `Makeup` at `00` lets a tone under the threshold through bit for bit in both types, and at `FF` raises it by 24 dB from the first frame; `Bus` under its threshold is bit for bit at each ratio from -60 to -20 dB; `Bus` with Auto comes out as loud as it went in over a held tone, and with `Makeup` adds its dB on top; an all-dry `Mix` is left alone by `Makeup`; the FX page shows `Auto` and `Makeup` on `Bus`, and the status line reads `Makeup` in dB. Added on 2026-11-10.
133. **The comparison harness can use Ableton Live 12's built-in devices as references, through a bridge: a control surface script in Live that takes requests on localhost, and Live's own clips and session recording to play the input through the device and record what comes out** (#364). **Why:** Live's devices aren't plugins, so pedalboard can't host them, and several are the references we lack: Glue Compressor is an SSL G-bus model, as our `Bus` is, with the G-bus's own ratios, attacks, releases and Auto release, which Bus FORCE hasn't; Pedal is the first overdrive reference, as no plugin here is a Tube Screamer. **The route:** a Live Remote Script, `tools/compare/live/VimtrackerBridge`, on `_Framework`'s `ControlSurface`, polled about ten times a second on Live's main thread, listening on `127.0.0.1:47364` only, one line of JSON a request. It keeps two audio tracks in whatever set is open, making them if need be: `vt bridge source` plays the input as an unwarped clip (`ClipSlot.create_audio_clip`) through the device under test, put there fresh for each case with `Track.insert_device` (Live 12.3 and later; the browser before), and `vt bridge sink`, armed with monitoring off, records the source at `Post FX`, after its devices and before its fader. Live's documented Object Model does all of it, so there's no probe set to ship and nothing generated that Live has to accept unseen; Max for Live devices with `sfplay~` and `sfrecord~`, generated as `.amxd` files without Max to check them, are kept as the fallback in the README. **Parameters** are set by Live's names and values as Live shows them (`Threshold = "-20 dB"`, `Ratio = 4`, `Release = "A"`): a continuous one by searching its range with `str_for_value` for the values that show what's asked and taking their middle, a list by its items; what Live then shows comes back and is checked against the spec, to its rounding, so a render that isn't what the spec says is refused. **Alignment:** what Live plays is a quarter second of silence, one sample at -30 dBFS, a second of silence, the input and a second more; the marker's place in the recording gives Live's delay and the device's latency, which are cut, so the output lines up with the input as ours does; the marker is quiet enough for a compressor or a pedal to pass it unchanged, and the second lets a compressor recover. `live_bridge.py null` checks the path is unity with no device. **Caching:** each render is kept in `out/live-cache/` by a hash of the device, its parameters and the samples sent, so a spec reruns without Live, and the measures, consensus and reports are unchanged. **Specs:** `live-glue-static`, `live-glue-attack` and `live-glue-release` set `Bus` against Glue, the first two with Bus FORCE beside it; `live-pedal-overdrive`, `live-pedal-distortion` and `live-pedal-riff` set Distort's `Overdrive` and `Distortion` against Pedal's two modes, its Gain at the same place as our `Amount`, unfitted. **Kept:** only outputs are compared, and Live's devices and files aren't opened; the install is a step the author runs, a symbolic link in the User Library's `Remote Scripts`, removed by `just live-bridge-uninstall`; none of it runs in CI, and its tests drive the bridge with stand-ins for Live's objects. Added on 2026-11-10.
134. **`Comp`'s `Bus` type becomes two, each its own model: `Console`, the SSL console's bus compressor fitted to Arturia's Bus FORCE, which is `Bus` renamed and plays bit for bit as it did, and `Glue`, a new model fitted to Ableton Live's Glue Compressor through the Live bridge, with Glue's own switches, its `Range`, and the shared `Source`, `Auto`, `Mix` and `Makeup`** (#338, which stays open for `VCA`, `FET` and `Diode`; #364's bridge). **Why:** the author asked for a model of each rather than one `Bus`, "glue and a nice name for the arturia matched one": Bus FORCE and Glue both model the SSL G-bus, but measure differently, and the first Live run (decision 133) showed `Bus` 2.9 dB RMS off Glue's curve at 2:1. **Names:** `Console`, as the SSL is a console's bus compressor and Bus FORCE models it; `Glue` after Live's device. The types go `Clean`, `Console`, `Glue`, and `VCA`, `FET` and `Diode` take the places after. Console's settings are `consoleratio`, `consoleattack` and `consolerelease`, labelled `CRTO`, `CATK` and `CREL`; Glue's are `glueratio`, `glueattack`, `gluerelease` and `range`, labelled `GRTO`, `GATK`, `GREL` and `RNGE`; each type keeps its own while another is shown. No backwards compatibility: a song saved with `Bus` or `busratio` won't load. **Glue's switches, as Live shows them:** ratio 2, 4 and 10 to 1; attack 0.01, 0.1, 0.3, 1, 3, 10 and 30 ms; release `0.1 s`, `0.2 s`, `0.4 s`, `0.6 s`, `0.8 s`, `1.2 s` and `Auto`, named as Live names them though they don't take that long. It starts where Live loads Glue: 4:1, 1 ms, 0.6 s and all of its range. `Range` is `00` to `FF` for 0 to 70 dB, read as `70.0 dB`, as Live's runs. **Left out:** the FX page shows ten settings (`MAX_PARAMS`), and Glue with everything asked for has eleven, so Live's `Peak Clip In` isn't a setting: it measured as a fixed odd soft clip after Glue's output and its dry/wet, not on the input, transparent to -2 dBFS, -1.08 dBFS out for -1 in and capped at -0.5 dBFS (0.944), with no aliasing over -108 dB, which a `Distort`, `Tape` or `Limit` slot after the compressor gives; `Range` was kept, as it changes how the compressor itself turns down. Glue's side-chain EQ is left out too, as `Source` is the side-chain here. **Prior art and sources:** Live's manual on Glue Compressor (made with Cytomic and "based on the classic bus compressor from a famous 80's mixing console"; no knee setting, the knee "gets sharper as the ratio rises"; Auto release with a slow and a fast time; Range limits how much it compresses, -60 to -70 dB emulating the hardware; the soft clip caps the output at -0.5 dB); Cytomic's The Glue, which Glue comes from; Jakob Erland's G-SSL circuit (https://gyraf.dk/gy_pd/ssl/ssl.htm), whose feedback detector and two-capacitor Auto release `Console` already models (decision 118). Only outputs were compared; Live and its devices weren't opened. **Measured** through Live at 48 kHz, every render cached: **Feedback:** Glue's 10 ms attack reaches 63% in 16.2 ms at 2:1, 12.2 ms at 4:1 and 6.1 ms at 10:1, as a detector hearing what it's turned down does; a feed-forward model fitted as well as it could be was 0.46 and 0.55 dB RMS off those two bursts. **The capacitor always lets go:** a tone held 10 dB over at 4:1 is turned down 6.8 dB with the 0.1 ms attack and 3.7 dB with the 30 ms one, and 6.5 dB at `.1` against 6.9 at `1.2` with the 1 ms one, as a timing capacitor charged by its attack and let go by its release all the while settles where the two balance; one that only lets go while not charging was 0.34 dB off at 30 ms. **The rectifier is the level each frame:** its curve at 100 Hz is its curve at 1 kHz to 0.01 dB, while its 3rd harmonic at 0 dBFS is -39 dB at 100 Hz against -59 dB at 1 kHz, the capacitor smoothing the wave's ripple; two tones at a sine's RMS are turned down 0.3 to 0.6 dB more, neither a peak detector (1.4 to 2 dB) nor an RMS one (none). **The knee:** at a -20 dB threshold, 2:1 is already 0.2 dB down at -40 dBFS, its ratio rising to 2.02 at -6 dBFS and falling back to 1.73 at 0; 4:1 starts at -21 dBFS and reaches 3.27 at 0; 10:1 starts at -16.5 and reaches 6.4. **Releases** let go as one exponential in dB each, time constants 80.5, 117.5, 160.3, 225.3, 303 and 413.5 ms for `.1` to `1.2`. **Auto** lets go to 63% in 48 ms after a 20 ms burst, 74 ms after 200 ms and 2.42 s after 2 s, a fast part and one of about 5 s. **Range** settles under itself: at 10:1 with a tone 30 dB over, `3 dB` turns down 1.28 dB, `6 dB` 3.42, `12 dB` 7.79, `20 dB` 13.28 and `40 dB` 21.02, the last as with none. **No colour:** the 2nd harmonic is under -84 dB at 1 kHz; the odd ones are the gain's ripple. **Latency** 0 samples. **The model** (`dsp/dynamics/glue.rs`): the detector hears the key turned down by the control voltage, solved within each frame by Newton's steps from below, which can't pass the answer as the knee bends up; the release lets go every frame, toward nothing, and the attack charges toward what the rectifier asks while that's more; what the rectifier asks, for the level in dB over the threshold, is nothing, then a power of how far into the knee, then a line, per ratio: starts at -46.8, -1.37 and +3.44 dB, widths 50.4, 9.58 and 6.85 dB, powers 6, 1.62 and 1.23, slopes 1.67, 4.35 and 14.3, each the ratio less one made steeper by about half, as the release always letting go leaves the capacitor short. Attack time constants 0.757, 1.338, 2.212, 4.764, 13.44, 38.56 and 123.1 ms; release ones as measured. Auto is the G-SSL's two capacitors with time constants 401.5 ms, 51.6 ms and 541.8 ms against `Console`'s 352.5, 42.8 and 619 ms. `Range` caps what the rectifier asks for, at the larger of 0.63 times it and 1.154 times it less 1.886 dB, fitted at 3, 6, 12 and 20 dB. All were fitted together to Glue's static curves at three ratios, its nine attack bursts and its five ranges, then checked against its releases, its 100 Hz curve and the two tones, which weren't fitted. The drawn curve is where a held sine settles: what the attack charges over a cycle balancing what the release lets go, within 0.05 dB of the compressor's own but where `Auto` hasn't yet filled its slow capacitor. **Gaps against Glue, before (`Bus`, now `Console`) and after:** static curve RMS from -40 dBFS 2.89, 0.77 and 0.08 dB to 0.05, 0.01 and 0.00 at 2, 4 and 10:1; turned down by the attack burst 3.7 to 3.9 dB at every attack against Glue's 6.8 to 3.7, now within 0.2 dB; 63% attack at 1 ms 4.0 to 2.1 ms (Glue 2.0), at 30 ms 33.5 to 26.7 (26.6), at 10 ms and 2:1 23.2 to 16.0 (16.2); 63% release at `.1` 99 to 80 ms (79.4), `.6` 596 to 224 (223.4), `1.2` 1179 to 411 (410.5), and `.2`, `.4` and `.8`, which `Console` hasn't, within 0.2 ms; Auto after 20 ms 39 to 47 ms (48.3), after 200 ms 55 to 74 (74.3), after 2 s 2485 to 2444 (2420); `Range`, which `Console` hasn't, 0.03 to 0.07 dB RMS at every range measured; at 100 Hz 0.014 dB RMS, its 3rd harmonic -39.3 dB against Glue's -39.2. **Specs:** `live-glue-static`, `live-glue-attack` and `live-glue-release` now set `Glue` against Glue alone, with Glue's `0.01 ms` attack and `.2`, `.4` and `.8` releases added; `live-glue-range` and `live-glue-detector` are new; the `comp-bus-*` specs keep their names and set `Console` against Bus FORCE. **Tests:** `Console`'s golden render is `comp-bus`'s hash unchanged; `Glue`'s curve comes within 0.15 dB RMS of Glue's at each ratio, its attacks at 1, 3, 10 and 30 ms and releases at `.1`, `.2`, `.6` and `1.2` within 15% of Glue's times, the ratio's speeding of the attack and Auto's two speeds within 15 to 20%; `Range` at 6, 20 and 40 dB turns down within 0.3 dB of Glue, and none at all leaves a tone bit for bit; a tone under each ratio's knee comes through bit for bit; no setting makes a NaN from silence, a tone, a step 80 dB over full scale or the smallest numbers; each frame solves its loop; the FX page shows Glue's ten settings, picks its seven attacks and releases with `K`, and reads `Range` in dB. Added on 2026-11-10. Amended by decision 135: `Glue` gets `Peak Clip` back, and the note on its aliasing is corrected.
135. **A slot shows up to twelve settings (`MAX_PARAMS`), two rows of six columns on the FX page, sized for the fullscreen kitty the author works in at 163 by 42, not for 120 columns; and `Glue` gets back Live's `Peak Clip In`, which decision 134 left out only for the old limit of ten, as `Peak Clip`, its eleventh setting** (#338, decision 134). **The rule, in the author's words:** "if we need more in the future lets reopen the decision instead of quietly removing the features. we can always move to having more rows, or some kind of tab/paging if needed". So a limit like this one that would leave out part of what's asked for is reopened rather than worked around. **The page:** each column's name has the room of its longest and a space: `Feedback`, `Ping-pong`, `High freq`, `Drive`, `Makeup` and `Peak Clip`, 9, 10, 10, 6, 7 and 10, the first a character narrower than it was, as `Feedback` is its longest; with the slot's number and effect, and the gap and drawing after the columns, the page is 148 wide. At 163 columns the view has 150 beside the main meter, so it ends two short of the meter's gap, and inside the yellow box the keyboard's mode draws, which takes a column each side, it still fits. The rows of a slot are worked out from `FX_COLUMNS`, so a later rise needs no new rows written out. **Drawings:** a compressor's curve and gain reduction meter, a limiter's meter and an EQ's curve (decisions 59 and 60) stay after all of a slot's columns, now the sixth, from column 138 to 147 of the page, whatever the effect uses, so every slot's lines up. **`Peak Clip`:** after `Makeup`, as it's after Glue's output and dry/wet, and at the very end of its chain, in a column of its own on the top row; its key is `peakclip`, its label `PCLP`, it's `Off` or `On` as `Auto` is, picked with `K`, and steps can lock it; it starts `Off`, as Live loads it. `Clean` and `Console` keep it while they're shown but never clip. Switching it fades in or out in a straight line over 5 ms, as a slot's bypass does, so it doesn't click on a loud sound, and switched out it leaves the sound bit for bit. **Measured** through Live, from renders cached by the agent behind decision 134 and one more, with Glue's `Range` at 0 dB so it turns nothing down and only the clip is heard (`live-glue-clip`): Glue's output is exactly a curve of what goes into the clip, sample by sample, with no filtering around it: under -2 dBFS (0.794) it's untouched, then it bends to -0.5 dBFS (0.944) and stays there from twice that height over the knee, +0.78 dBFS (1.094). The curve is a quartic, in `u`, how far past the knee in the knee's height: knee + height × (u − u³/4 + u⁴/16), which leaves the knee at the line's slope with no change in how it bends, and reaches the ceiling flat with none either. It puts out -1.08 dBFS for a -1 dBFS sine's peak. Every one of the 120,000 samples of Glue's stepped render is within 8e-7 of it. It's odd, so it adds only odd harmonics. It comes after both `Output` and `Dry/Wet`: with the output 12 dB up it clips 12 dB sooner, and at half dry the blend is clipped rather than only the wet half. **Amending decision 134:** its note that the clip has "no aliasing over -108 dB" came from a 1 kHz tone, whose aliases at 48 kHz land on its own harmonics, so none could show. Through a 2311 Hz sine, Glue's clip aliases at -64 dB at 0 dBFS, -43 dB at +3 and -39 dB at +6, which is what a curve at the base rate does; ours is the same curve at the base rate, and aliases to the same tenth of a dB. Oversampling was left out, as Glue doesn't, and it would cost the clip being transparent bit for bit under -2 dBFS. **Gaps against Glue:** the static curve is within 0.005 dB of Glue's at every level stepped, in every case but `output`, where ours is 0.05 dB louder under the clip, as its `Makeup` steps to 12.05 dB and not 12; sample by sample, ours differs from Glue's by -81 dB RMS at its stepped levels, -88 dB at half dry and -79 dB at 2311 Hz, most of it Live's `Output` set a few thousandths of a dB off 0 by its knob's rounding. **Prior art and sources:** Live's manual on Glue Compressor, whose soft clip caps the output at -0.5 dB, and the name Live's object model gives the switch, `Peak Clip In`. Only outputs were compared; Live and its devices weren't opened. **Specs:** `live-glue-clip`, with `on`, `off`, `output`, `mix` and `alias`; the first four were already cached, and `alias` is the one new render. **Tests:** `Peak Clip` matches Glue's measured samples within 1e-6, either side; a -1 dBFS sine peaks at -1.08 dBFS and one under -2 dBFS comes through bit for bit; switched out, Glue lets a sine stepped to +12 dBFS through bit for bit; another type with it on doesn't clip; it comes after `Makeup` and `Mix`; its 2nd and 4th harmonics are under -100 dB; it switches in over 5 ms without a jump; the golden renders are unchanged; the FX page at 163 columns shows Glue's `Peak Clip` in a sixth column after `Range`, its drawings at column 138, the EQ's ending at 147, nothing in the two columns before the meter, and it all fits in the keyboard's box; the cursor reaches it, and `K` picks `On`. Added on 2026-11-10. Decision 139 replaces the two rows of six columns with a device's three rows of up to four.
136. **Every track gets a `Trim`, a gain at its input for gain staging, so `Level` is left for the mix: a quarter of a dB a step either side of `80`, which is 0 dB and the default, from -31.75 dB at `01` to +31.75 dB at `FF`, with `00` off. On a sound track it comes straight after the voice, before the filter and its drive; on a group, a return and the master, before their slots. Like `Level` it's set outside patterns, never modulated, and glides** (the author's request for a utility gain on every track, so the faders can give the mix rather than the gross gain staging). **Why:** `Level` is the fader: it comes after the slots, scales the sends, and is where the mix is set, so turning a quiet sample up there also moves where it sits in the mix, and nothing set how hard the filter's drive and the slots were hit but the machine's own volume. **Prior art:** a console's channel strip has an input trim at its top, before its EQ and dynamics, for gain staging, and a fader at its bottom for the mix; Ableton Live's mixer has no trim, so a `Utility` goes first in a chain for it, whose gain runs from off to +35 dB with 0 dB its default. **Range and mapping:** evenly in dB, as the compressor's `Makeup` and the limiter's `Input` are, but cutting as well as boosting, so 0 dB is in the middle, at `80`. The step is the level scale's, a quarter of a dB, so `80` is exactly 0 dB, every `10` in hex is 4 dB, as on the fader, and the range, ±31.75 dB, takes in the +24 dB asked for and nearly Utility's +35. `00` is off, as a level's is, so it can silence a track, which `-inf dB` reads. The gain at `80` is exactly 1, so a song at 0 dB plays bit for bit as before, and the golden renders are unchanged. **Shown:** as hex on the Sound page, with a bar centred on 0 dB as `Pan`'s is, and in dB on the status line to its quarter, as `+0.25 dB`, `+12.0 dB` or `-inf dB`. **Where:** at the top of the Sound page's output column, above `Level`, so the column reads in the order the sound goes, as a channel strip does, and every setting under it there moves down a row: a synth with three or four settings has a Sound page a row longer, 13 rows, a sampler's is still 13 and one with eight still 16, and with the filter's curve it fits the 163 by 42 screen inside the keyboard's box. A group's, a return's and the master's Sound pages have it above `Level` too. On the mixer it's the row above each strip's `Level`, so its meters are a row shorter, and the mixer opens on `Level`, the fader, rather than its first row. **The master gets one,** as it has slots and its fader is after them: the trim sets how hard the mix hits the master's slots, and the fader what goes on to the limiter. **Not modulated:** it's staging, not performance, so it has no setting in the registry: no step's command, lock or `Dest` reaches it, which needs nothing more than not registering it; the status line reads it with its own text, as `Glide`'s and `Reset`'s are. **Smoothing:** it glides as `Level` does, over the same 10 ms, so moving it, or `00` and back, doesn't click; like `Level`, a silent track's starts moving when it next sounds. **Keys:** as `Level`'s: `=` and `-` a step, `+` and `_` 4 dB, `i` two hex digits, `x` back to `80`, and `/trim` on the track page or the mixer. Unlike a level it runs to `FF`, with nothing snapped. **No backwards compatibility needed:** a song saved without one loads at 0 dB, and 0 dB isn't written. **Left as they are:** a kit's gain still goes into its sound's sample bank entry, as decision 94 made it, not into the track's `Trim` or `Level`, and there's no `:ttrim` beside `:tvol`. **Tests:** the scale's ends and middle, and its text; a track's trim saves and loads, and 0 dB is left out of the file; `FF` turns a steady sample up by 31.75 dB from the first frame and `00` silences it; +12 dB of trim and 12 dB less level cancel with the filter clean and squeeze the output by more than 1 dB with `Drive` at `FF`; going to `00` and back glides with no step over a hundredth of the level; a group's trim boosts by its dB and silences, a return's silences its delay and boosts what's sent, the master's boosts the mix, and the group's and the master's come before a distortion in their first slot; on the Sound page it's above `Level` and reads in dB, `=`, `+`, `i`, `x` and `u` work on it and leave the fader alone; the mixer has it above each `Level`, every strip's to the master's, and opens on `Level`; the longest Sound page with its filter curve fits at 163 by 42 in the keyboard's box. Added on 2026-11-10.
137. **The track filter's circuits are checked against two references each, The Drop's circuit and an Arturia filter modelled on the same one, and fitted only where the two agree: the clean low-pass, high-pass and notch start from a Q of 0.5 rather than 0.707, `MS20 HP` gets a taper of its own, and `MS20 LP` rings 2.3 dB louder; the ladder, the band-pass and everything the references disagree on are left as they were** (decision 120's pairing, with decision 125's consensus). **The pairs:** `LP`, `HP`, `BP` and `Notch` at 12 dB with The Drop's OSR and Arturia's Filter SEM, the Oberheim SEM's state-variable filter; `Ladder` with PRD and Filter MINI, the Minimoog's ladder; `MS20 LP` and `MS20 HP` with MS1 and Filter MS-20. Filter M12, the Matrix-12's, could check the ladder's four-pole types and the 24 dB clean modes against a third circuit, and was left out. **How:** `tools/compare/filter_refs.py` maps each side's knobs by measuring, The Drop's, the SEM's and the MS-20's cutoffs being in hertz and the MINI's set where, with no resonance, it passes 12 dB under its passband, as four equal poles do at their cutoff; turns each Res in 33 steps at 200 Hz, 988 Hz and 5 kHz, our first 85% against each reference's turn up to where it starts to ring, measured for the Arturia filters (MINI 0.751, MS-20's low-pass 0.715 and high-pass 0.638) and The Drop's 100%, with the SEM, which never rings, set by its whole turn; and reads the slope at Res 0, the peak's height along the way to ringing, the peak's place, width and fall at the same height of peak on every side, the passband the resonance takes, the ringing's pitch and level at six cutoffs, and the distortion of a stepped sine. Where the two references agree within `consensus.py`'s tolerances, their mean is the target. Each Arturia filter starts from its `Default` preset with everything that isn't the bare filter off, as `specs/filter-refs-*.toml` record. Measured on the way: Filter MINI's `limit_resonance` does the opposite of its name, so off, as it loads and as `filter-ladder` and `filter-ringing` had it, it never rings; Filter MS-20 takes a new setting only at its next render, so `render_reference.py` gains `settle`, a buffer of silence with each case's settings first; the SEM's and MINI's inputs' coupling and the MS-20's other filter are taken out by dividing by their open responses. **The clean types:** both references' two-pole low-pass and high-pass pass 5.8 to 6.0 dB down at `Cut` with no resonance, two equal poles, a Q of 0.5, at all three cutoffs, where ours was a Butterworth's 3.0 dB; and 1.8 dB down an octave into the passband where ours was 0.3, and their notch is 2.4 octaves wide where ours was 1.8. So the low-pass, high-pass and notch now start from a Q of 0.5, and `CLEAN_TAPER` is fitted again to OSR's peak, which now starts level with ours, so the peak over the passband at each twentieth of the knob is OSR's, averaged over the three cutoffs and both slopes; the most stays a Q of 10. At 200 Hz and 988 Hz every Res-0 measure the two agree on is now within 0.1 dB, but one at 0.26, and the notch's width within 0.01 octaves, where they were 1.5 to 2.9 dB and 0.6 octaves out; at 5 kHz the high-pass and notch stay up to 0.9 dB off an octave and two past `Cut`, where the bilinear transform cramps the response near the top. The band-pass keeps its Q of 0.707 and its taper, now `BAND_TAPER`: OSR's band-pass is its high-pass and low-pass in series, 1.74 octaves wide with 12 dB slopes, and the SEM's a two-pole 2.5 octaves wide, and ours sits between at 1.88. The utility filters' Res 0 and the reverb's damping keep theirs too. Against `filter-taper-svf`, ours is now 0.58 dB from OSR's peak on average where it was 1.83 for the low-pass, 0.67 from 1.96 for the high-pass, and the notch 0.04 octaves wide of it from 0.17. **`MS20 HP`:** both references put its peak 3 to 4 dB under ours at a quarter, half and three quarters of the way to ringing, at every cutoff, and reach a 3, 6 or 12 dB peak 0.16 to 0.24 of the way later, so it gets `KORG_HIGH_TAPER`, fitted to their mean height up to 80% and the same as `KORG_TAPER` from there, so it still rings from `E0` and at full `Res` as before; now within 0.2 dB at a quarter and half way where they agree, between them at half and three quarters where they don't, and reaching each height within 0.01 of the way. Against MS1 alone its height is now 0.93 dB off on average, from 3.56, but its rise from its own Res 0 is 2.62 dB off, from 0.95, as MS1's high-pass falls at 12 dB an octave and starts lower, where the MS-20's falls at 6, as ours does; against MS2, the later OTA circuit, 2.96 from 0.88. **`MS20 LP`'s ringing:** at full `Res` it rang 1.7 to 2.9 dB under where MS1 and the MS-20 agree, from 640 Hz to 3.6 kHz, where `MS20 HP` rang with them, so its Korg 35 is given its input 1.3 times quieter and its output 1.3 times louder (`KORG_LOW_HEADROOM`), which leaves a quiet sound as it was and raises the saturation and the ringing 2.3 dB: within 0.7 dB of them now, and its distortion of a −6 dBFS sine goes from −36.5 to −40.9 dB, still between MS1's −27.7 and the MS-20's clean −80. **Left as they are, as the two disagree:** the ladder's bass loss, where the MINI thins the passband 10.8 dB at a 12 dB peak, as ours does, and PRD keeps it; the ladder's taper past half way, its tallest peak, how it falls past the peak, its ringing's pitch at full `Res`, where both ring flat of their cutoff but too far apart to agree, PRD by 91 to 99 cents and MINI by 110 to 300 against its 12 dB point, and ours, +3 to −27 cents, is kept on `Cut` so it plays in tune as an oscillator; its ringing's level (ours −18 to −19 dBFS, PRD's −15, MINI's −5), and its distortion, PRD's with a strong 2nd harmonic and MINI's odd; the clean types' taper, the SEM's peak staying under 1.2 dB to half its turn and then climbing to 17.7 where OSR's rises evenly; the MS20 types' slope at Res 0, where ours matches the MS-20 and MS1 is more damped; and the MS20 types' ringing pitch, ours within 2 cents and theirs ±60. **Left as they are, as they're structural:** the clean filter runs at the base rate on the bilinear transform, so near 16 kHz it falls further than both references, which oversample, 28 dB two octaves past a 5 kHz `Cut` against 21 and 24, and a 5 kHz high-pass's top rises 0.7 dB under a 12 dB peak where theirs rise 1.6 to 1.9; closing that is oversampling the clean path, at a cost in CPU. And the clean types are linear with no `Drive`, where OSR and the SEM both saturate a −6 dBFS sine at −44 dB: ours are clean by design, and `Drive` is where they saturate. **Kept:** only outputs are compared; the Acid's filter is the diode ladder and isn't touched; the ladder isn't changed. **Tests:** the clean low-pass and high-pass are 6 dB down at `Cut` at Res 0 and the notch 4.4 dB down an octave out, the band-pass still 3.3; the taper test's clean rises are OSR's and `MS20 HP`'s the references' mean; both Korg 35s ring at −7 to −4.5 dBFS from 640 Hz to 3.6 kHz at full `Res`; the specs `filter_refs.py` writes parse and prepare. The `fx` golden render changes, as its track filter is the clean low-pass at `Res 40`; `plain`'s doesn't. Added on 2026-11-10.
138. **A track has four FX slots (`INSERTS`), three LFOs (`LFOS_PER_TRACK`) and three envelopes (`TRACK_ENVELOPES`), up from three, two and two, for the scope of an Octatrack rather than an M8 or a Polyend.** **Why:** in the author's words, vimtracker is becoming their "Octatrack level tool, instead of the original more just M8/Polyend level tool", so the per-track capacity is raised once to cover it. **Prior art:** the Octatrack has three LFOs a track, with its LFO designer, an amp envelope and two effect slots; the counts here are the author's; the Digitakt II has three LFOs a track, later ones reaching earlier ones and never back (here it's the other way round, as the M14 settings design set it, and it carries on: `LFO 1` reaches `LFO 2` and `LFO 3`, `LFO 2` reaches `LFO 3`, `LFO 3` reaches neither, and every envelope reaches every LFO); the M8 has four mod slots an instrument. **Where the counts live:** each is one constant. The rows that were written out by hand per slot, LFO and envelope (`INSERT_TOPS` and `INSERT_BOTTOMS`, `FX_ROWS`, `ENVELOPES`, `LFOS`, `MOD_ROWS` and `FIXED_MOD_ROWS` on the track page, the returns' first slot in `Track::fixed`, and `default_envelopes`) are now built from them in loops, as statics, so the next change is only the number; the settings registry, the `Dest` lists, `K`'s command list, the labels (`4BYP`, `4.delay.feedback`, `DEP3`, `lfo3.depth`), the engine's arrays, the live values, the kitty images and the views already followed the constants. **New ones start off:** a fourth slot is `Empty`, a third LFO has no `Dest`, and a third envelope has no `Dest` and `Env 2`'s shape, a 200 ms decay to nothing, so it does something as soon as it's given somewhere to go; nothing about a new song sounds or looks different but the extra empty rows. **Saving:** the arrays are saved at their full length, as fixed tuples, so a song saved before this doesn't load, and `FORMAT_VERSION` goes from 6 to 7, which turns that into the clear message that it "uses format 6, and this version reads format 7" rather than a field error; there's no conversion and no tolerant loading of shorter arrays, by the author's rule, and their 303 project songs will be made again. **The pages at 163 by 42:** the FX page's four slots take the screen's rows 3 to 13, each slot's two rows and a blank, with its drawing beside it as before, an empty slot one row; the Mod page has three envelope rows, three LFO rows, the accumulator and arpeggio, and under them the three envelope shapes side by side, 30 columns each, 90 of the 150 beside the meter, all in the screen's first 23 rows. Neither page was redesigned (the FX page's redesign is a separate study), and nothing needed squeezing or leaving out. The manual's Mod screenshot is now 26 rows rather than 24, as the page needs 23 for its view and 120 by 24 leaves 22, which cut the shapes' last row; the page itself isn't fitted to 24 rows. **Real time:** everything stays a fixed array sized by its constant, so the audio thread allocates nothing more; `MemoryPool`'s capacity is `MAX_TRACKS × INSERTS × 2` and grows with it. A track's `TrackState` goes from 70,008 to 78,224 bytes, 8,216 more; the engine's 71 tracks go from 4.97 MB to 5.55 MB, allocated once at start. A `Track` in the song goes from 288 to 344 bytes and its `LiveValues` from 68 to 84. An empty slot is skipped, as before, an LFO with no `Dest` or no depth is skipped before its wave is worked out, and an envelope with no `Dest` is skipped in `modulate`, leaving only the start and release of its run on each note, a few stores. Measured on the engine alone, 60 s of 16 synth tracks with a filter envelope and one LFO each, best of five, in a release build on the author's laptop: 3.45% to 5.54% of a core over four runs with the old counts and 4.34% to 5.15% with the new, so nothing measurable; `vimtracker bench` wasn't used, as it couldn't be run with its config folder set aside. **Tests:** the golden renders are unchanged; the last slot plays and a step's `4.bypass:01` switches it out; a lock on `lfo3.depth` moves the third LFO, and the first LFO moves its depth; every envelope after the first opens the filter, and a third with a depth but no `Dest` changes nothing; on the app, `K` puts a delay in slot 4, a step locks `4BYP` from `K`'s list, the third LFO is routed to that delay's feedback and the third envelope to `L3 Depth`, and all of it comes back from a saved and loaded song; at 163 by 42 the FX page shows all four slots' two rows and the Mod page all three envelopes, their shapes and all three LFOs; the allocation tests fill the fourth slot. Added on 2026-11-10.

139. **The FX page is a rack of devices, option A of the FX page study ([`fx-page.md`](fx-page.md)): each slot is a full-width device 9 rows tall in a rounded frame, with its slot's digit, a place for its switch, its effect and its effect's type on the top border, its settings in three rows of up to four from a grid each effect states (`Insert::grid`), and its compressor's or EQ's curve and gain reduction meter in a framed panel on its right** (#374, step 1 of M28). **Why:** the author asked for three rows of settings, a framed curve and a page in Ableton's device language; the study compared a stacked rack (A), Ableton's horizontal chain (B) and a strip with one big device (C), and the author chose A, as it keeps every slot and setting on one screen with columns that line up for `j`, `k`, `{` and `}`, and four devices of 9 rows fill the 36 rows inside the Keys box exactly. **Prior art:** Ableton Live's devices (a title bar with the device's name and its activator, its controls, and a display such as the Compressor's curve), Bitwig's device chain, and Elektron's pages of up to eight settings in fixed places. **The grid:** each effect's settings sit by what they mean, not by their order: Glue, Clean, Console, EQ3 (a column a band) and Delay are placed by hand, as the study's table has them, and every other effect puts its own settings down each column in order, then a column of what comes out, `Mix` and `Gain`, last. A grid can have holes, which `h`, `l`, `0` and `$` step over, and `j` and `k` keep the column they aim for and land on the nearest setting in a row with a hole there, the left one of two as near. This replaces the columns of two, even settings over odd, of the FX page's first design and decision 135. Values use the Sound page's 9-cell bars, in cells of a 10-column name, a 3-column value, the bar and a 3-column gap, 27 columns; a list's choice spans the value and the bar. The model's tests check that every effect's grid, in every type and mode, places each of its settings exactly once apart from its type, fits 4 by 3, has its output in its last column, and has names under 10 characters and choices of at most 14. **The type in the title:** `Insert::type_param` is the setting that changes an effect's character, `Type` on Distort, Chorus and Comp and `Mode` on LPG and Comb; it moves into the title after the effect, where `k` from the grid's first row reaches it, `K` lists its choices and `=` and `-` step it, which frees a cell in the grid (Glue's 11 settings become 10). An empty slot keeps its full frame, titled `Empty`, with `K  pick an effect` inside, so nothing moves when an effect goes in. **The switch:** the title keeps one column after the slot's digit for the bypass switch that #375 adds, so the effect's name stays where it is when it comes; until then a bypassed slot shows as now, everything in it faint but for the cursor and its images, its title's effect and type too. **Focus:** the device with the cursor has its frame in `OVERLAY2` and its title controls bold, and the others `SURFACE1`; no colour marks focus, so yellow stays the Keys box's alone. **The panel:** the compressor's curve (4 by 2), its gain reduction meter (now 2 columns wide, so its box can carry the `GR` caption) and the EQ's curve (10 by 2) keep their sizes and images, each in a faint square box, right-aligned 2 columns in from the frame and centred in its rows; the limiter's meter is alone. Making them bigger and smooth is #376. **Navigation:** a slot is 4 cursor rows, its title and its grid's 3; `j` and `k` go down through them into the next slot's title, passing an empty slot's grid by; the title's effect is over the grid's first column and its type over the second; `{` and `}` keep the row and column in the next or previous slot, so they come back after an empty slot, and any other key first puts the cursor where it's seen, so an edit after `}` onto an empty slot acts on its effect. `b`, `K`, `dd` and `p` work from anywhere in a slot, as before. **Shared code:** the frame, the grid and the panel are page-agnostic widgets in `views/widgets/` (`device`, `grid` with its `CellSource` trait, `panel`), and the rack is `DeviceSpec`s from `Page::devices` with a `DeviceCursor` of device, row and column, so the Sound and Mod pages (the track pages study, [`track-pages.md`](track-pages.md), M29) can be written against them. Growing past four slots isn't built, but nothing rules it out: devices keep their size, and where fewer fit than there are, the page already goes by whole devices to keep the cursor's in view. **Tests:** the golden renders are unchanged, as this is the interface only; the app tests check the page at 163 by 42 with four devices, beside the main meter (rows 2 to 37 of the 150 columns) and inside the Keys box (rows 3 to 38, its bottom border under them); moving through each slot's title and grid over its holes, `0`, `$`, `gg`, `G`, counts and `{` `}`; the focused frame and bold name, an empty slot's frame and hint, and a bypassed slot going faint but for the cursor; and the curves and meters landing in their boxes. The manual's FX screenshot is now taken at the author's 163 by 42, as the devices need the full 150 columns. Added on 2026-11-10.

140. **A slot's bypass is a toggle in its device's title, `nf-fa-toggle_on` (U+F205) in green while the slot is in and `nf-fa-toggle_off` (U+F204) faint while it's bypassed, and every setting that's off or on shows the same toggle in place of the words: the FX page's `Off`/`On` lists (`Auto`, `Peak Clip`, `Ping-pong`, `Freeze`), an envelope's `Reset`, the LFOs' and the arpeggio's `Retrig`, and the sample page's `Snap` and `Reverse`** (#375, step 2 of M28, with the two-state rule of the track pages study, [`track-pages.md`](track-pages.md) §1.4, brought forward so one rule never has two looks). **Why:** a toggle reads faster than a word, and one glyph pair for every on and off is the explorer's (decision 101), so the same idea has the same look on every page. **Prior art:** Ableton Live's device activator in each device's title bar, and its on/off buttons in place of a value for a device's switches; Bitwig's power button on each device. **The switch:** it's in the column decision 139 kept after the slot's digit, so the effect stays where it was. While the song plays it shows what's heard (`heard_bypassed`), so a `1BYP`–`4BYP` lock flips it for its note and back, as the faint text already did (decision 88); stopped, it's the slot's own. It isn't a cursor stop, and `b` flips it from anywhere in the slot, as before. An empty slot has nothing to switch, so it keeps the blank room and no toggle. **Two-state settings:** the glyph alone, without the word, which the FX study had left open (its question 2); the status line still says `On` or `Off`, or what the setting does, and editing is unchanged (`=` and `-` flip them, and `K` still lists the words to pick from). It's green when on and faint when off, the cursor's colours under the cursor, and faint with the rest of a bypassed slot; on the sample page a slice that plays as the whole sample's shows it dim, as its other inherited values are. An effect's setting is a toggle when its choices are exactly `OFF_ON` (`Param::is_toggle`), so a new effect's switch gets one without page code, and a two-way list of anything else, such as Chorus's type, stays a word. **Size:** each glyph is followed by a no-break space (`glyphs::ICON_SPACE`), which kitty and Ghostty don't draw a symbol over, so every toggle is drawn as big as the explorer hint's; in a title the no-break space is the first column of the gap after the switch, so nothing after it moves, and in a cell it fits in the room the word had. The sample page's settings row runs on as text, so a toggle there is two columns where `off` was three. **Shared code:** `views/widgets/toggle` has the glyph, its colours and its text, and the device widget, the FX, Mod and sample pages and the hints all use it; `Device::switch(on)` draws the toggle and `Device::switch_room()` the blank. **Left to #376:** the faint version of a bypassed slot's curve, as part of its one drawing path with three shades; until then a bypassed slot's images keep their colour (decision 59), and its text fades as before. **Tests:** the toggle follows `b`, and while playing a lock's bypass either way, with the effect's name where it was and an empty slot's room blank; the toggles on the FX page (Auto, Peak Clip, Ping-pong) and the Mod page (envelope Reset, LFO and arpeggio Retrig) in their columns, flipped by `=`, the status line's word, and the columns after them unmoved; Snap and Reverse on the sample page, flipped by `K` and `=` and by `~`, with what's after Reverse unmoved; and in the model, that exactly Auto, Freeze, Peak Clip and Ping-pong are toggles. Added on 2026-11-10.

141. **Every curve is drawn by one path, `kitty::plot`, at the terminal's own pixels with a smooth 2-pixel line, and the FX page's graphic panels grow to the study's sizes: the compressor's curve 13 by 5 characters with its gridlines and threshold, a gain reduction meter 5 rows over 20 dB with a scale and a held mark, and the EQ's curve 33 by 5; each panel's image is kept in three shades, dim, focused and faint, the faint one for a switched-out slot** (#376, step 3 of M28; [`fx-page.md`](fx-page.md) §3 and §4, [`track-pages.md`](track-pages.md) §1.5). **Drawing:** `kitty/plot.rs` replaces `line_png`'s 3-pixel dots for the compressor's and EQ's curves, the Sound page's filter curve and the Mod page's envelopes. A picture is the size of the characters it covers times the character's size the terminal reports (`Graphics::cell`), so at the author's 15 by 39 pixels the compressor's is 195 by 195, the EQ's 495 by 195, the filter's 360 by 117 and an envelope's 420 by 156, and nothing is stretched. The line is 2 pixels wide, and each pixel near it is covered by `clamp(1.5 - d)` for its distance `d` from the nearest segment, in eighths; the curve is worked out at two points a pixel, with a point at each of an envelope's stage ends so a 1 ms attack still reaches the top and its corners stay sharp. The PNG is still a 4-bit palette, now with an alpha for each entry in its `tRNS`: entries 1 to 8 are the line's colour at 1/8 to 8/8, 9 a gridline (`OVERLAY0` at 55%) and 10 a fainter one (35%), and it's always compressed. **Inside the compressor's curve:** dotted gridlines at -20 and -40 dB both ways, the dotted 1:1 diagonal, and a dashed line up from the bottom to the curve at the threshold, `threshold_db` of the setting for every type; the curve is `compressor::curve_db`, which is each type's own gain computer, Glue's and Console's included, knee and all, as decision 59 had it. The 0 dB edges are the panel's faint box, which is its frame. **Inside the EQ's:** a dotted 0 dB line and fainter dotted lines down it at 100 Hz, 1 kHz and 10 kHz, as §1.5 has for frequency plots; §4's ±12 dB lines were left out, as §1.5, the later standard, doesn't have them. **The filter's curve and the envelopes** are drawn by the same path at their sizes and places as they were, with the line alone: their gridlines (decades and 0 dB, a baseline and the stages) come with M29's sizes. **Shades:** `PanelImages` holds each panel's image by `(PanelKey, Shade)`, `PanelKey` being the filter, the sample's waveform, an envelope or a slot, with what it was drawn from; it replaces `Graphics`'s separate `images`, `filter`, `waveform` and `slots`. A slot's curve is faint (`theme::faint`) while it's switched out as it's heard (`heard_bypassed`, so a step's `nBYP` lock flips it), which replaces decision 59's "a bypassed slot fades its text but not the curve"; otherwise it's the active green with the cursor in its device and dim without. Each shade is sent once, so moving the cursor or switching a slot out and back sends nothing more. The sample's waveform moved into the same cache and is still drawn as dots. **The meter:** text, as before, so its pumping moves every frame for nothing: 2 characters by the panel's 5 rows, hanging from the top over 0 to -20 dB, half a dB an eighth, three times finer than decision 59's 1.5. Its scale is faint text beside its box, `0`, `-8` and `-16` at the tops of rows 1, 3 and 5, which makes the panel 25 columns, as the study has it. The deepest it turned down lately is a faint mark below the bar, held as the main meter holds its top (`meter::Held`: 1.5 s, then falling 24 dB a second), `App::slot_reduction`, brought up to date each frame from what's reported: a `▔` at the top of the row that depth ends in, or a `▁` at its bottom, whichever is nearer, so it's within 1 dB; a meter that turns nothing down and holds nothing shows the faint `▔` at its top, as before. Limiters show the meter and its scale alone. **Image cost:** an image is sent only when what shapes it changes, or for a shade not yet sent. One that changes again within a frame of being sent for a change keeps the last for a frame (`PANEL_FRAMES`, the scope's `SCOPE_FRAMES`, #214), so a curve following an LFO is sent at most every other frame, 15 times a second; a single change after a rest is sent at once. Measured in tests: the compressor's curve is about 0.95 to 1.0 KB of PNG and 1.3 to 1.4 KB sent, the EQ's 0.5 KB flat and 1.4 KB with its bands moved (1.9 KB sent), the filter's 0.7 KB and an envelope's 1.0 KB, against 9.3 KB for an envelope as dots before, as they're compressed. Under an LFO moving a compressor's threshold and an EQ's band at every frame, each is sent 15 times a second, about 20 KB a second for the compressor and 29 KB for the EQ. Drawing one takes 0.3 ms for the EQ and 1.2 ms for Glue's curve in a release build. **Storing again after a clear:** the panels' images are retired with the sample page's when the window changes size, and also when the character's size in pixels does, as a font change does, so each is drawn again at the new pixels under a new id. **Left for later:** the ball on the compressor's curve at the input level, the dots at the EQ's bands, and the other effects' graphics in §4. **Tests:** the curve's line within a pixel of the compressor's own curve (0.3 dB at 195 pixels) for all three types, and of the EQ's sum, the engine's own tests keeping Clean's settled reduction within 0.05 dB of the ratio, every type's steady sine within 0.25 dB of `curve_db`, and `response_db` within 0.1 dB of sines through the EQ, all unchanged; an image sent again only on a change, and every other frame under an LFO; the meter's scale, its fill at 0.5 dB an eighth and its held mark; the panels' places at 163 by 42; the three shades' colours; the images' sizes at two character sizes and again after a clear; and the filter's curve and the envelopes at their sizes. Added on 2026-11-10.
142. **`vimtracker bench` says where the time goes: `--tracks` times each part inside the engine, `--device` times the real callback while sending the device silence, and `--paced` plays each block when its time comes** (the author asked on 2026-11-10 why the status line shows about 30% while `bench` said about 5%). **Prior art:** Reaper's performance meter, which lists each track's and each effect's share of the CPU, and Bitwig's and Ableton's per-device CPU readouts; Apple's audio workgroups documentation, which says the system decides cores and clocks for the device's I/O thread from its deadline and load. **The breakdown** times parts rather than muting tracks on a copy, since a muted track's voice still plays and a slot can't be taken away without changing what the next one hears: a `Stopwatch` in the engine reads the clock once between parts, so the parts add up to the whole, and each track times its slots that have an effect in it. Both do nothing until `Engine::time_parts` is called, beyond checking an empty option, and the clock's own cost, measured on the bench's thread, is taken off each figure; flat out, the parts add up to within a few hundredths of a percent of an untimed run. **The device run** opens the output the program would, with its rate and buffer from `audio.ron`, and its callback does what the program's does, then writes zeros over the buffer, so nothing is heard. It reports the callback's time, `Realtime::process`'s and the engine's, and the callback thread's own CPU time, and whether the system gave the thread a real-time policy. **Each worst block** now says how much of it the thread was on the CPU, as a flat-out block that the system put aside for another program looks like a spike in the engine otherwise, and `bench` says how long starting to play took, which a callback does ahead of its block. **What it found** on an Apple M5, for the author's song: the engine takes about 5% of a core flat out, and the same 5% to 6.5% in the callback at 64, 128 and 256 frames, but 11% to 21% at 512 and 1024, where the system runs the light, long-period callback on slower cores or clocks; the engine's share grows less than its work as a song gets heavier (a song taking 30% flat out took 43% at 512). The queues, the report and the copy to the device add under 0.2%. Added on 2026-11-10.

143. **A comparison that plays a song's line reads it from the song each time it runs, and no 303 pattern's transcription is kept in the repository: `nuance-chirp-riff` is a stub naming a project folder outside it, by default the author's `~/Music/vimtracker/projects/303-patterns/confusion-pump-panel`, and `vimtracker notes` reads that song's Acid track with the program's own model, never writing to it, for every side to play.** (#391, after decisions 125, 129 and 130). **Why:** the line embedded in `nuance_specs.py` as `RIFF_ROWS`, `RIFF_BPM` and `RIFF_KNOBS`, called the Blade riff, was transcribed from a website whose famous 303 patterns sound wrong to the author, from a project since deleted; and the project's rule is that 303 pattern transcriptions stay out of the repository. The line the author plays is in their own song, which they keep editing. **Prior art:** a test that needs material it can't ship reads it at run time and is skipped where it's absent, as pytest's `skipif` does for a missing fixture. **How:** `vimtracker notes <project> <out.toml> [--track NAME] [--machine KEY]` (`track_notes.rs`) writes one sound track's notes once through its chain, the first in any section that gives it one, with the song's tempo and every one of its machine's settings. Rows are timed as the sequencer plays them, each pulse a step at the track's speed: a `Single` held its gate's share of the way through its last pulse (its row's own length, or the track's `Gate`, or the machine's, `80` on the Acid), a `Multi` on each pulse, a `Hold` to the next row's note and sliding into it, a `Rest` or `OFF` ending what sounds, and a chain's transposes added; a track whose rows use FX, lanes, nudges or a groove other than `Straight` is refused, naming the row, rather than read wrongly. `tools/compare/song_line.py` turns a stub with a `[song]` table into the whole spec in `out/songs/` each run, from `nuance_specs.song_spec`, and `spec.read` reads that in the stub's place, so the probe, the reference renders and `made_by` all get the same notes. The folder is `run.py --song`'s, else `VT_COMPARE_SONG`'s, else the stub's; with no `song.ron` there, as in CI or on another machine, the spec and any whose input it renders are skipped with a message and a suite carries on. Every side gets the song's Cutoff, Res, Env Mod, Decay and Accent and its wave, Acid V's waveform at 0 or 1 and ABL3's `303 Saw` or `303 Square` (both squares' even harmonics measured 15 and 18 dB down); the track's drive, filter and effects are left out, so every side is the voice alone. Notes keep the nuance suite's velocities, 99 and `70`, by the song's accents. The spec files keep their names, as decisions 129 and 130 cite them; `pedal-riff` and `live-pedal-riff` now render `out/inputs/song-acid.wav` from the case `song`, and the titles say "a song's Acid line". **The earlier numbers:** decisions 129 and 131's results on the riff, and decision 130's `pedal-riff` results, with the pedals' `Level` and ceiling set so the riff came out as loud as it went in, were all measured on the old transcription at 136 BPM. They stand as history, aren't comparable with runs from now on, and the constants fitted with them are left as they were. **First run,** on the song as saved at 23:11 on 2026-11-10: 136 BPM, twelve plain notes over a bar, three of them sliding, on the square, with Cutoff `54`, Res `E0`, Env Mod `05`, Decay `66` and Accent `0D`; the references at `buffer_size=512`. With Env Mod nearly off there's little chirp: ABL3 shows a peak 2 to 3 dB over the fundamental on most notes where ours and Acid V show none over a dB, and the references agree on a peak's place on only one of the ten notes any side shows one on. Where they do, ours sits about an octave under both, 175 to 197 Hz against Acid V's 329 to 384 and ABL3's 415 to 513, 1.02 octaves under their consensus on the note they agree on, and stands 4.0 dB less over the passband and 1.7 dB less over the fundamental at 20 ms; the note's first 20 ms over the next 40 is 1.5 dB from the references on average over the three notes they agree on, −0.4 dB on balance. Over the whole line ours is the darkest, a spectral centroid of 241 Hz against 280 and 344, and the loudest, −18.0 dBFS RMS against −20.6 and −22.3. Through `Distortion` against Dist OPAMP-21 (`pedal-riff`), the shapes are 4.2, 1.8 and 0.5 dB apart at 25, 50 and 100% `Drive` in `Modern`, most at 16 kHz, where ours is 16.7, 6.8 and 1.9 dB darker, and 5.3 dB apart against `Normal`, ours 18.7 dB brighter at 16 kHz; ours is 2.7 to 2.9 dB louder, by design, and its level over time within 0.1 dB once that's out. `live-pedal-riff` wasn't run, as it needs Live. Added on 2026-11-10.

144. **The mixer's blank row under its title names its three parts, each on a rule over its strips in the title rule's style, `─ Tracks ───`, `─ Groups ───` and `─ Returns ───`, with the cursor's part brighter** (#387, step 8 of M29, §2.4 of [`track-pages.md`](track-pages.md)). **Why:** the mixer is strips, not devices, and frames would cost it a strip or more, but a rule on the row that was blank costs no room and tells the parts apart as the devices' titles do. **Style:** a device's top border and title, as §1.2 has them: the rule in the frames' `SURFACE1` and the name dim (`OVERLAY2`), as a device's identity is, and with the cursor in the part the rule `OVERLAY2`, as a focused frame's, and the name in the text's colour, so both go up a step. **The parts** are the ones the mixer already parts with its gaps: the sound tracks, the groups, and the returns with the master, which has no gap of its own, so it's under `Returns`, as the study's mockup has it. **Width:** each rule starts over its part's first strip and stops a column short of its last strip's edge, so three blanks part it from the next. A part too narrow for the rule, as a lone group's strip is at 6 columns, shows its name alone, cut with `…` if even that doesn't fit, as the other cells are. **Nothing else moves:** the title, the strips' names, the routing row, the meters and the settings, `Trim` over `Level` (decision 136), stay on their rows. **Tests:** at 163 by 42, each name over its part's first strip, the tracks' rule as wide as its strips less a column, the cursor's rule and name brighter and the others' not, and moving with the cursor; a lone group's name alone; the title, names, routing, `Trim` over `Level` and `Solo` on the last row where they were. Added on 2026-11-10.

145. **The track page is a component, `TrackPage`, which holds the page showing and its cursor (its row and field, the field `j` and `k` aim for, and the fine digits) and runs its own moves, given the cursor's track, while its edits stay `App`'s for now; `track_page.rs` becomes a module with a file for each page's settings, the rack, the controls, the mixer's cursor and the three kinds of edit; and the track page's tests leave `tests/app.rs` for `tests/track_page.rs` and `tests/fx_page.rs`** (#380, step 1 of M29; the track page's part of #320 and #323; [`track-pages.md`](track-pages.md) §4). **Why:** M29's Sound and Mod pages are written against the rack the FX page has, and the study asked for one cursor model to be moved off `App` before them, not three tables and a rack; and every step of M29 adds track page tests to a file of 13,900 lines. **Prior art:** ratatui's component template, a struct per page with its own state and `key`, and `SamplePage` (decision 102), which keeps its own cursor on `App`. **The shape:** `App::track_page` is a `TrackPage`, in place of `track_page`, `track_row`, `track_field`, `track_wanted` and `track_fine`. Its methods take the cursor's track as an `Option<&Track>`, as what's on a page depends on it, and read nothing else: `rows`, `place`, `control`, `devices`, `page_rows` and `device_cursor`, which was `App`'s; `move_to` and `settle`; and `key(cmd, times, track)`, which runs `j`, `k`, `h`, `l`, `0`, `$`, `gg`, `G`, `{`, `}` and the pages either side and returns an `Outcome`: `Done`, `Refused` with what the status line says (`{` and `}` off a rack), or `Passed`, for `App::run_track_page` to run as an edit or as what the key means anywhere. **What isn't built:** #320's `Ctx`, which would let the edits move onto the page too. The edits change the song through `App::apply`, the history, the status line and the picker, all of which `Ctx` is to lend, so giving the page its track and leaving the edits where they are is the smallest step that moves the cursor and its keys and changes nothing else; drawing stays in `views/track.rs`, which reads the page's state. **The module:** `track_page.rs` keeps `Page`, `TrackPage`, `Outcome` and `App::run_track_page`, and every public path under `track_page` is as it was. `sound.rs` and `modulation.rs` hold their pages' row tables and a `devices` that's empty, and `fx.rs` the slots, `DeviceSpec::slot` and `b`, `dd` and `p`; `Page::devices` asks each, so the Sound and Mod PRs (#381, #383) each give their page's devices in their own file, and the cursor goes through them as it does the FX page's. `device.rs` has `DeviceSpec` and `DeviceCursor`, with `DeviceSpec::new` building a device from a model's `Grid`, which the slots now use and `Machine::grid` can; a device's panel and width, which §4 lists, come with the pages that use them. The rest: `controls.rs` (`Control` and its names and values), `mixer.rs` (the mixer's cursor), `editing.rs` (`=`, `-`, the fine digits and `x`), `pickers.rs` (`K`) and `typing.rs` (`i`). Items moved whole, with `pub(super)` where another file of the module calls them. **The tests:** the Sound and Mod pages', the jumps' and the track page's drawings go to `tests/track_page.rs`, the FX page's devices, moves and panels to `tests/fx_page.rs`, and the helpers more than one file uses to `tests/common/mod.rs`; the dest lists, the locks typed in the phrase view and the mixer stay in `tests/app.rs`. The tests are unchanged but for `app.track_page.row` and its like, and one more, for `TrackPage` on its own: through a rack with no `App`, past an empty slot and back with `}`, and refusing `{` on the Mod page. Nothing visible changes: main's 2,035 tests all pass as they did, 2,036 with the new one, and the screenshots are the same. Added on 2026-11-11.

146. **Notes are named as Ableton Live names them: MIDI note 60, middle C at 261.63 Hz, is `C-3`, where it was `C-4`, so every note's octave number is one lower than before and its pitch and MIDI number are the same; it's one convention, with no setting** (the author's decision on 2026-11-11). **Why:** the author moves between vimtracker and Live all the time, and a note should read the same in both. **Prior art:** Ableton Live, Logic and Cubase call MIDI 60 C3 and MIDI 0 C-2, as Yamaha's numbering does, and so does the Deluge (`noteCode / 12 - 2`); scientific pitch notation, MIDI's own documents and vimtracker until now call it C4; trackers vary (Renoise sends MIDI 48 for its C-4, and Furnace numbers its octaves from -5). A plugin's own sequencer, such as ABL3's, names notes its own way, so a note there may still read differently. **Range:** a step still holds MIDI 12 to 127, now octaves -1 to 8, from `c_1` to `G-8`. **Below zero:** the note cell stays three characters, so octave -1 is written as Furnace writes its octaves under 0, a small letter, then `_` for a natural or `+` for a sharp, then how far below 0: `c_1`, `c+1`, `d_1` up to `b_1`, then `C-0`. Live writes these C-1 to B-1; a small letter can't be read as octave 1, which `c-1` could, and nothing is cut short. **Keys:** the keyboard's octave keeps the same notes on the same keys, `a` on middle C, and only its number changes: `Oct` starts at 3 and goes from -1 to 8, held as a signed `App::octave`. **The code:** the octave sums are in one place, `step::octave_of` and `step::octave_start`, which the step text, the keys and `Scale::degree` use. **Songs:** the same text now means a note an octave higher, so `FORMAT_VERSION` goes from 7 to 8 and an older song is refused with the version message, with no conversion, as the project's rule has it; the author's songs are converted by a one-off script outside the program. `vimtracker notes`, `probe` and `tools/compare` carry MIDI numbers, which don't change; only their comments' and titles' names do. **Tests:** every test that writes a note's name was moved down an octave in name, so each plays what it did, and the golden renders are byte for byte the same; new tests check that `C-3` is MIDI 60 at 261.63 Hz and that every note from 12 to 127 is three characters and reads back as itself, `c_1` and `G-8` at the ends; and that the keyboard starts at `Oct 3` with `a` on 60 and stops at -1 and 8. Decisions before this one keep the names they were written with. Added on 2026-11-11.

147. **Playing from the top resets only the track states a song has had tracks in since the last reset, and an effect that works each side alone works out one side for both while a sound is the same on both sides, bit for bit: each effect says what it does with such a sound, `Mono::OneSide`, `Mono::Linked` or `Mono::Stereo`, and the slots pass on whether a frame's sides are the same** (after decision 142's `bench`, which found both costs). **Why:** `play_from(0)` took 0.8 to 1.8 ms, almost all of it `reset_tracks` going through every setting of all 71 track states, though the author's song has 12, inside the callback that starts playing: up to 75% of a 64-frame callback. And `Distort`'s Overdrive (decision 130) on the author's Acid voice was about 41% of the engine, clipping at four times the rate on both sides of a sound that's the same on both. **Prior art:** a host running an effect once for a mono track, as Logic Pro's and JUCE's mono and mono-to-stereo plug-in layouts and Reaper's per-effect channel setups do, where an effect that treats each side alike costs half on a mono sound; and a dirty list, which resets only what's been touched. **The reset:** `Engine::used` is how many track states, from the first, the song's versions have had a track in since the last reset: a reset sets it to the song's tracks, and a new version raises it to its own if they're more. As tracks come and go the fixed tracks' states move with them, but never past the most there have been, so a state past `used` still has the settings of an empty track that the last reset gave it, as only a track of the song changes them. Such a state, unless it's sounding or ringing, has only the rest reset (`TrackState::reset_all_but_settings`): its random generators from the seed, conditions, accumulators, arpeggio, last note and LFO starts, all cheap. Every state used since, including a removed track's still fading, is reset in full as before. **Mono:** `InsertState::mono()` matches every effect with no catch-all, so a new one has to choose, and `Stereo` is always safe. `OneSide` is for an effect whose every side goes through the same steps with its own state and nothing between them: all eight `Distort` types (the pedals' filters and oversampling, the shapes' oversampling, and `Crush` and `Down`, which keep nothing a side), EQ3's bands, LPG's filters (its follower is one for both) and Vowel's formants (its limiter is linked). Each keeps its per-side state in `Sides<T>` (`dsp/blocks/sides.rs`): while the frame's sides are the same and so are the states, bit for bit (`SameBits`, as a float's `==` has `0.0` and `-0.0` alike), the left is worked out alone and given to both, and the right's state waits; the first frame whose sides differ copies the left's state to the right's and works out both, so a switch can't click or jump: what comes out is exactly what working out both every frame gives. It goes back to one side once the states are the same bits again, which a filter's may never quite reach after a stretch of stereo, or when the effect starts afresh, as a pedal does at rest and any slot does with a new effect or type; until then it works out both, as before. `Linked` is for one state for both sides and the same done to each: the three compressors, whose detector is one for both and whose gain and Glue's `Peak Clip` are the same on each side, and an empty slot. A mono sound comes out mono, with nothing to skip. `Stereo` is the rest: Delay, Reverb, Chorus, Phaser, Flanger, Plate, Pitch, Grains, Warps, Reso and Comb, which spread or mix the sides, and Limit and Tape, which keep their sides in the effect pool's buffers and weren't looked at for this. **The flag:** a track looks once a frame at whether its sound is the same on both sides, after its filter, and each slot is told and tells the next (`InsertState::process_sides`): a `Linked` slot passes it on without looking, and any other looks at what it put out, one comparison, as a `OneSide` effect's states can still differ for a while after the sound's sides did. The check is per frame, not per block as first asked, as the slots run a frame at a time, between the frames of every other track for keys and sends: changing that is a bigger change than this one, and a frame's check is one comparison of two words. Deciding each frame is also what lets a switch be exact. **Measured** flat out on a copy of the author's song with `bench --tracks`, at 48 kHz in blocks of 512, alternating the two builds on a machine running other builds: starting to play took 0.94 to 1.05 ms before, and 0.21 to 0.24 ms after. The Acid's Overdrive went from 1.92 to 2.14% of a core to 1.06 to 1.09%, from 39 to 41% of the engine to 28%, and the engine as a whole from 4.7 to 5.5% to 3.7 to 3.9%; the kick's `Distort` went from 0.05% to 0.02%. The Chorus after the Overdrive, which spreads the sound, the compressors, Glue on `G1` and Clean on the master and the kick, which have nothing to skip, and the master's EQ3, whose input the Chorus has made stereo, are the same within the noise: 0.04 to 0.05% for the Chorus, 0.19 to 0.21% for Glue, 0.14 to 0.16% for Clean on the master and 0.02 to 0.03% for EQ3, before and after. **Tests:** goldens made from the engine before this change (`play-start`, and `sides-` for each oversampled `Distort` type) are bit for bit the same after it: playing from the top again, tracks added and removed while playing and between plays, a removed track's notes and `Overdrive` ringing when playing starts, a new seed, and fewer tracks than ever; and each type on a sound the same on both sides, a stereo one, and one that switches mid-note, for a single frame and after a rest. A track added after a track was added and removed between two plays plays at its own level, not a hold the removed one left in its state. Every effect claiming `OneSide` or `Linked`, as it starts and with its types and modes set to do something, comes out bit for bit as working out both sides every frame does on noise that's the same on both sides, then not, then the same, then apart for one frame, then silent and the same again, and says its sides are the same only when they are. Added on 2026-11-11.

148. **A selection stays through every change that moves or fills its values, until `Esc`, with each press its own undo and the status line keeping the running total; and Cmd with `h` `j` `k` `l` is `-` `_` `+` `=` by one rule, in every mode that binds those, the shape panel included** (the author's decisions of 2026-11-11, after decisions 67 and 116). **Prior art:** Vim's popular `vnoremap > >gv` and `vnoremap < <gv`, which put the selection back after each shift so it can be pressed again; Vim's `gv` after `Esc`; Elektron's trig selection, which stays held while a parameter is turned; and decision 67's Cmd keys, which were added mode by mode. **What stays:** `=` `-` `+` `_`, `>` `<`, `C-a` `C-x` and their Cmd keys keep the selection, so `+ + -` goes in one press after another while the loop plays; `r` fills and then comes back to the selection as it was, cursor and all, so another `r` picks again, and `Esc` from `r`'s prompt comes back to it too, rather than ending it; and `.` on a selection keeps it when what it repeats is one of these. `d`, `x`, `y` and `S` end it as before, and so does `~`, which the author didn't list and which cycles marks rather than moving values. The cursor stays where it was after each, where before it went to the selection's top. **Undo:** each press is one undo step, as it was, and `u` and `C-r` are now bound in a selection, where they were unbound: each undoes or redoes one step, the selection stays, and the total goes back or forward with it. An undo past the selection's own presses takes back what came before it, and the selection still stays. **`Esc`** ends a selection and now remembers it for `gv`, as Vim does, since a selection that stays is mostly ended with `Esc`; before, only an operator remembered one. **The total** (`grammar::Adjustments`) counts each press that changed the song in the unit of the selection's first column: a note in semitones, an octave 12, or in degrees with the scale on, as `=` and `-` move through it then; a nudge in 24ths of a step; anything else in its own small and large steps, which can't be added as one number since a large step is 16 on a hex value, 7 on an `ACU` and a digit on a `RET`. It reads `+11 semitones` or `+1 large step, -2 steps`, `0 steps` once presses cancel out, and starts again after a fill, as a filled column has nothing to be relative to. It's the status message after each press, and stays on the message line while the selection moves, until `Esc`. **One rule for Cmd:** `bindings::CMD_ADJUSTS` pairs `=` `-` `+` `_` with `D-l` `D-h` `D-k` `D-j`, and `bindings()` puts each Cmd key beside its key, with its description, in every mode that binds the key and not the Cmd key itself, in place of the `ADJUST` and `ADJUST_SELECTION` rows added mode by mode. The shape panel reads its keys itself, so `adjusting_key` turns a Cmd key into its key there, and Cmd keys type nothing while a value is typed; before, the panel dropped them. **The audit:** the phrase grid, its selections, the chain view, the song view, the track page's Sound, Mod and FX pages with the rack's titles and their `Type`, the mixer's `Trim`, `Level` and `Pan`, and the project page all take `=` `-` `+` `_` as `Increase` and the rest, so their Cmd keys do the same by the rule, as before. The audio page has `=` and `-` and no large step through its list, but its Cmd-k and Cmd-j stepped through it as Cmd-l and Cmd-h do, and are kept as rows of its own. The sample page keeps its Cmd-l and Cmd-h, a frame's nudge where `=` and `-` move a pixel, as decision 116 made it: the same move, finer, and on a settings row the same as `=` and `-`; its Cmd-k and Cmd-j now come from the rule, as `+` and `_`, as before. Pickers, Insert, the command line, the jump search and the guides have no `=` to stand for and take no Cmd keys. **Tests:** repeated `+` on a column selection across two tracks, with the third untouched and an undo step each; the running total, in steps, large steps, semitones and degrees, kept while moving and gone after `Esc`; undo and redo while selected, one press at a time, the selection kept, and past the selection's own presses; `d`, `x` and `y` ending it and `gv` bringing it back; `.` keeping it; `r` and its `Esc` coming back to it; the Cmd keys doing what their keys do, to the song and the cursor, on every page above, in a selection and in the shape panel; the audio page's four; the sample page's on a point and on a setting; and every mode that binds a key binding its Cmd key to the same command. Added on 2026-11-11.
