149. **A sound track's Sound page is four full-width devices, Machine, Filter, Output and Play, built from the FX page's frame, grid and panel; the machine's type and the filter's type and mode are in their titles, and each machine states its own grid** (#381, M29 step 2, after the track pages study's §2.1). **Why:** the page was two halves with no structure, the machine and filter on the left and two unrelated groups on the right, paired row by row by four tables (`SAMPLE_ROWS`, `SYNTH3_ROWS`, `SYNTH_ROWS`, `SYNTH8_ROWS`), so the pairing, not the meaning, decided what sat beside what. **What's where:** Machine has the machine and its type in its title and its own settings in `Machine::grid()`; Filter has its type and mode in its title and `Cut` `Res` `Drive`, then `Low cut` `High cut`; Output has `Trim` `Level` `Pan`, then `Send A` `Send B` `Group`, the signal's order down each column as decision 136 has it; Play has `Pitch` `Glide` `Gate`, then `Speed` `Groove` `Reset`, then `Choke`. Four devices of 9 rows fill the 36 rows inside the Keys box at 163×42, as the FX page's slots do. **The type** (`Machine::type_param()`), as an effect's type is: the sampler's `Sample`, `Acid`'s `Wave`, 6-op's `Patch`, and `Chord` and `Chiptune`'s chord; other synths have none. In the title it reads as the name of what it's set to (`Saw`, `E.PIANO 1`, `M`), as an effect's type does, and the sample as the bank shows it (`07 hat open`). The sampler's stays `Control::Sample`, so `K` lists the bank and `g d` opens the sample page as before. **The grids:** most machines' settings go down the columns in order, as `Insert::default_grid` does. Two are placed by meaning, by the settings' saved keys: the sampler's `Slice` and `Play` (which part plays and how) in one column and `Start` and `Length` (where) in the next, and `Acid`'s in a TB-303's pairs, `Cutoff` `Resonance`, `Env mod` `Decay`, `Accent` `Slide`, then the Devil Fish's `Drive`. **The cells:** one value width for the page, 5, so a value and its fine digits read as `A000` with the fine pair dim, and a column is 29 wide (`CellWidths::SOUND`); a slice is a choice, its number then its name where the bar would be (`00   whole`), and the sampler's `Play` a word (`One-shot`). A word or a choice may now run on into the gap after its cell, short of the next column's name, as a slice's `of 16 at 1.234 s` needs; nothing on the FX page is that long, so it draws as before. **Moves:** the `TrackPage` rack does them, so `j` and `k` go title, rows, next title, keeping the column, and `{` and `}` keep the row and column with a count. One addition to it: a device's title with nothing in it, as Output's and Play's are, stands for its first settings row, so `}` onto it lands inside the device rather than on the row above, and `j` and `k` pass it by. **The panels:** the filter's curve keeps its size, 24 by 3 in its box, now in the Filter's panel at its right, and a sampler's waveform keeps its size in the Machine's; making them big, the sample's thumbnail and the Output's meter are #382. **What isn't changed:** a group's, a return's and the master's Sound pages stay rows of their output (M29 step 7); the Mod page (#383). **What changes for users:** the output is `}` away from the filter rather than `l` on every row, the machine's type and the filter's type and mode are in the titles, reached with `k` or `l`, and `Glide`, `Gate` and the other timing settings are in Play. **Code:** `vt-model`'s `Machine::settings`, `type_param` and `grid`; `track_page/sound.rs`'s `devices`; the drawing in `views/track/sound.rs`, split from `views/track.rs`; and the tests in `tests/sound_page.rs`, with the Sound page's tests in `tests/track_page.rs` now driving the rack and drawing it at 150×40, the view's size at 163×42. Added on 2026-11-11.

150. **While the system runs the audio thread slowly to save power, a dim leaf, `nf-md-leaf` (U+F032A), comes before the CPU figure at the top right, and on the audio page the line under the status line says how slowed and about what the figure would be at full speed, as `󰌪 CPU slowed ×3.6 by power saving, about 8% at full speed`; the figure itself is unchanged** (the author's decision on 2026-11-11, after decision 142). **Why:** decision 142 found that the figure is the callback's time over the buffer's, and that at a 512-frame buffer macOS runs the light callback on an efficiency core or a low clock, so the author's song read 20 to 30% where it takes about 5 to 6% at full speed. The figure stays as it is, how close each block comes to its deadline, and the leaf says, in the author's words, "that the cpu usage is not my maximum rate, rather it is a weaker cpu doing things with more effort". It shows for any cause: an efficiency core, a low clock, Low Power Mode or heat. No battery glyph. **Prior art:** Windows 11's energy saver, a leaf on the battery icon while the system holds the machine back to save power; macOS's own Low Power Mode, a yellow battery; Ableton's and Bitwig's CPU meters, which say how close the audio comes to its deadline, as ours does, and nothing of the clock. **The leaf** is before `CPU`, not after the figure, so on the right-aligned readout nothing else moves when it comes and goes; it's dim whatever the readout's colour, and followed by a plain space, as the status line's `󰀦` is. The audio page's `Keeping up` row has it too. **The explanation:** the top-right readout has no hover or explanation of its own, so it's said where a page says what it's showing: on the audio page (`Space 7`), the line under the status line, while there's no message, as the sample page's mode hint is. It names the cause as `heat` when `NSProcessInfo`'s `thermalState` is serious or critical, `Low Power Mode` when `isLowPowerModeEnabled` says so, and `power saving` otherwise, and the figure at full speed is the CPU figure over the factor, `under 1%` below half a percent. **Measuring the slowdown:** four times a second of audio, after its block and after the CPU figure is taken, so it isn't counted in it, the callback times a fixed kernel (`vt-engine`'s `slowdown.rs`): 1024 rounds of 32 one-pole filters in single floats side by side, fed by Knuth's MMIX generator, which gives the same answer for a seed in every build and allocates, locks and calls nothing; the clock is `Instant`, which on macOS reads shared memory. A look is the fastest of 3 runs over the kernel's time at full speed, at most ×8, and the lower of that and the last look's, as an interrupted run only reads slower. The callback stores it in thousandths in `AudioStats::slowdown`, beside the load. **Why 32 filters:** the engine's DSP is bound by how many sums a core does at once, which an efficiency core's narrower units cut more than its clock alone. A first kernel of 8 filters, bound by one chain's latency, read ×2.1 at 512 frames where the engine was ×3.0 slower than flat out; 32 filters read ×2.3 where it was ×2.8, and track the engine at both buffers below. 64 ran out of registers and read less. **Full speed:** worked out as the program starts, on a thread of its own while the song loads, at the user-interactive QoS that asks for a performance core: the fastest kernel time in 100 ms. A machine busy with other work gave a full speed up to 1.8 times too slow, which would hide the leaf, so the fastest ever seen is kept in `full-speed.txt` in the cache folder, by the kernel's version and the kind of build, and the faster of that and the new one is used; a start in Low Power Mode or on a busy machine still has full speed to go by. **The alternative** the issue offered, the engine's own time against a flat-out estimate, was left: there's no flat-out figure while playing, as the work changes from step to step, and the kernel's is fixed. **Smoothing:** the main loop moves the factor towards the last look by 1 − e^(−t/2 s) each frame, from ×1, so a factor of ×3.6 shows the leaf in about half a second and full speed hides it in about 4 s; it shows above ×1.6 and hides below ×1.3. One look at ×4 for its quarter second doesn't show it. **Low Power Mode and heat** are asked of `NSProcessInfo` every 2 s from the main loop, never the callback, through `objc2-foundation` with its `NSProcessInfo` feature and `objc2` for an autorelease pool, both already in the tree through cpal, so no new crate is built; on other systems neither is asked. **`vimtracker bench --device`** now reports the factor, stopped and playing, with the figure at full speed it implies, and what the check costs, and `--buffer` asks the device for another buffer than the audio page's, as the bench's config folder couldn't be set aside. **Measured** on the author's Apple M5 MacBook Air with `vimtracker bench --device` on their `confusion-pump-panel` song, after #396, while other builds kept the machine's load between 5 and 14, so the figures are rough: flat out the engine took 4.9% of a core playing. At 512 frames the callback took 10.2% playing and the check read ×2.30 (×1.78 to ×2.70 over 61 looks), about 4.5% at full speed, against the engine's ×2.1 over flat out; the leaf shows. At 128 frames it took 4.8% and the check read ×1.30 (×1.22 to ×1.41), about 3.7%, where the engine was at its flat-out speed; under ×1.6, so the leaf stays hidden. The kernel took 1.54 µs at full speed, and 3.6 µs a look at 512 and 2.1 µs at 128, and the whole check cost 0.003% to 0.005% of a core, a twentieth of the 0.1% allowed. The first two runs, on an empty cache folder at a load of 9, found full speed at 2.29 µs and read ×1.22 at 512, too little for the leaf; the third found 1.54 µs and kept it, which is why the fastest is kept. **Tests:** the kernel's answer is pinned and the same in debug and release; a look's factor is its time over full speed, capped at 8, and one slow look alone doesn't count; the check looks four times a second whatever the buffer and allocates nothing (the real-time guard); the readout starts at ×1, goes nearly two thirds of the way in 2 s, shows over ×1.6 and hides under ×1.3, staying as it was between, and one quarter second at ×4 doesn't show it; the explanation's text for power saving, Low Power Mode, heat and under 1%; a kept full speed reads back only for its kernel and build; at 163 by 42 the leaf comes before `CPU` at the top right with the right end unmoved, and goes again; and the audio page says what it means on the line under the status line, gives way to a message, and other pages don't say it. Added on 2026-11-11.

151. **The Mod page is a rack of half-width devices, step 4 of M29 ([`track-pages.md`](track-pages.md) §2.2): `Env 1` to `Env 3` down the left, each beside the LFO of its number on the right, and the `Accumulator` beside the `Arpeggio` under them, four bands of 9 rows that fill the 36 rows inside the Keys box. Each envelope's and LFO's title has its destination, after the room for the switch #385 adds, and its settings are in two columns of three; the LFO's shape glyph leaves the grid. `h` and `l` go on into the device beside on the same row, and `j`, `k`, `{`, `}`, `gg` and `G` keep to the cursor's column of devices** (#383). **Why:** the study's mockup and the author's answer to its question 4: the Mod page was tables under column headings, with the envelopes' pictures in a strip cut off from their settings, and a device per modulator groups each one's settings with its picture and says what it's for in its title, as a slot's effect does on the FX page. **Prior art:** Ableton Live's devices and its LFO and Envelope Follower devices, whose map button names the destination in the device; Bitwig's modulators, each a small panel beside the device it moves; the Digitakt's LFO page, with its destination one of its settings. **The rack:** a `DeviceSpec` has a width, full or half (`DeviceWidth`), and `rack_places` lays a page's devices out in the order they're read, a full-width one in a band of its own and two half-width ones in a row sharing one, so the page gives `Env 1`, `LFO 1`, `Env 2`, …, and the FX page, all full width, is laid out as before. `rack_areas` in `views/widgets/device` splits a band into 74, a gap of 2 and 74 at 163 by 42 (73 and 73 in the Keys box), and goes by whole bands to keep the cursor's in view where fewer than four fit, as the FX page goes by whole slots. A return, a group and the master have no accumulator or arpeggio, so their page is three bands. **Grids:** an envelope's times (`Attack`, `Decay`, `Release`) down the left and its levels (`Sustain`, `Depth`, `Reset`) down the right; an LFO's `Shape`, `Rate` and `Depth` down the left, `Phase` and `Retrig` down the right and a hole under them for #86's `Fade`; the accumulator's `Up`, `Down`, `Moves` and `Limit`, `Adds`, `Reset`; the arpeggio's `Rate`, `Mode` and `Oct`, `Retrig`. Cells are the study's compact ones, `CellWidths::COMPACT`: a 10-column name, an 8-column value, no bar and a gap of 3, 21 columns, where `bar_cells` of 0 now means no bar, so the FX page's cells are as they were; the arpeggio's value is 11 wide, for `Up-down-inc`, as it has no panel to line up with. **Titles:** the identity, dim, then a switch's room, then `Dest` as the title control, which `k` from the first row reaches and `K` picks; `---` is dim, as an empty slot's `Empty` is. The accumulator and the arpeggio have nothing to switch or aim, so their titles are their names. **No mode in the titles yet:** none of the modulators has a mode until M20 (#45, #46, #85, #86), so a title is its destination alone, and the mode goes after it when one comes; `Shape` stays a word in the grid, as the mockup has it. **The shape glyph goes,** and with it `glyphs::lfo_shape` and its four waves (decision 104), as #384's drawn cycle will show the shape beside the word, and §1.5's fifth rule keeps a panel from saying what's already on screen. **Envelopes' panels:** each envelope's shape stays the size it was, 28 by 4 (`DIAGRAM_WIDTH`), now in a faint box against its device's right side, 2 in from the frame; that leaves the second column 4 or 5 characters for its value before the box, which `100%` and `+7F` fit, so the grid stops 2 short of the box rather than running under it. #384 makes them the study's H size, 24 by 5, and draws the LFOs' cycles and phase dots. **Moves:** `h` before a row's first setting goes into the device on the left, to the last setting on the same row, and `l` past the last into the one on the right, to the first, a count going on across; nothing is right of the LFOs, so `l` stops there. `j` and `k` go on into the title of the device below or the last row of the one above, on the same side, so they never cross from the bottom of one column to the top of the other; `{` and `}` go up and down a device in the same column, keeping the row and column, so `3}` goes from `LFO 1` to the arpeggio; `gg` and `G` are the top and bottom of the cursor's column, and `0` and `$` stay in the device. A device whose title has nothing, as the accumulator's and the Sound page's `Output` haven't, puts the cursor on its first row, as decision 149 has it; and any row with nothing goes to the nearest row with something in the same device, never the device before it. Off a rack, which is now only a fixed track's Sound page, `{` and `}` say they go between devices and the page has none. **Code:** the Mod page's devices are in `track_page/modulation.rs`, which no longer has `ENVELOPES`, `LFOS`, `ACCUMULATOR`, `ARPEGGIO`, `MOD_ROWS` or `FIXED_MOD_ROWS`, and its drawing in `views/track/modulation.rs`, apart from the Sound and FX pages. **Left for later:** the on/off switch and its faint state (#385); a modulator that goes nowhere drawing its contents and panel dim, which the study's §1.6 has, beyond its dim `---`; and the H-size panels, the LFOs' cycles and their phase dots (#384). Added on 2026-11-11.

152. **Each envelope and LFO has an on/off switch, `b` on the Mod page, shown in its device's title with the FX slots' toggle: switched out, it's faint, what it moves glides to where it would be without it over 5 ms, and it keeps running underneath, so it comes back in time; a step can't switch one** (#385, step 6 of M29, the author's answer to the track pages study's question 1, [`track-pages.md`](track-pages.md) §1.4 and §2.2). **Why:** to hear a track without its wobble and back without losing the settings, which setting a depth to `00` and remembering the old one was the only way to do. **Prior art:** Ableton Live's device activator, which its LFO and Envelope Follower devices have like any other; Bitwig's modulators, each with its own power button; the FX slots' bypass (decisions 37, 88 and 140). **The model:** `bypassed` on `Lfo` and on `Envelope`, saved only when it's set, so a song with none switched out saves as it did; the field defaults to off when it's missing, so the format needed no new version. `SetLfo` and `SetEnvelope` carry it, so `b` is one undo step, and every edit of a modulator's settings keeps it. **The sound:** each modulator has a share of what it moves, from 1 while it's in to 0 while it's out, which glides a straight line over `BYPASS_FADE_MS`, the slot bypass's 5 ms, by the frames rendered since the modulation was last worked out, so a note's extra update doesn't hurry it. The share scales what the modulator adds, so out it adds nothing, as if its depth were 0, and once it has faded the engine skips it, as it skips no depth. Playing from the top puts each share where its switch is, so a song that starts with one out plays exactly as with no depth. Underneath it runs on: an LFO works out where it is from when it started, and an envelope's stages still follow the notes, so back in, an LFO is in phase with one that was never out, and an envelope is where the held note has it. On `Vol`, `Env 1` is each note's own shape rather than a movement, so switched out, notes have the plain shape a new track's have, `Envelope::note_shape()`, from the next one, as when it's moved off `Vol`; a note already sounding keeps the shape it started with. **The page:** the toggle goes in the room decision 151 kept in each envelope's and LFO's title, green while it's in and faint while it's out, and it isn't a cursor stop: `b` flips it from any row, as in a slot. Switched out, its destination and settings are faint but for the cursor, through the grid's `faded`, as a bypassed slot's are, and an envelope's shape is sent in `PanelImages`' faint shade, its own picture kept, so switching back sends nothing. The LFOs have no panel until #384, whose drawn cycles take the faint shade the same way. `b` on the accumulator or the arpeggio says it has no switch, and on the Sound page that `b` works on the FX and Mod pages. **No step lock:** a slot's `1BYP` is in the settings registry as each slot's own (decision 88), but an envelope has nothing in the registry at all, as none of its settings can be locked, so `E1BYP` would be a new owner for one setting, and `L1BYP` alone would make the LFOs and envelopes differ; and for an LFO, `DEP1 80` on a step already takes it to no depth for its note. A modulator's switch is for hearing the track with and without it, which is a choice of mix rather than of pattern, so it's left to `b`. **Tests:** an LFO on `Cut` switched out glides to the held value through several values, with no jump and never turning back, lands within 5 ms and a control block, and then stays through the rest of its cycle; switched back, it fades up and is then in step, block by block, with one left in; an envelope on `Cut` glides out and back; a song that starts with an LFO and an envelope switched out renders exactly as one with no depth; `Env 1` switched out leaves notes the plain shape; saving writes `bypassed` only when it's set, and loading reads it back; `b` from LFO 1's `Rate` and from `Env 1`'s title, its status, one undo step, and the toggle, its colour and the faint contents in the drawn page, the device beside unchanged; `b` refused where there's no switch; an envelope's faint picture sent and its own placed again. The golden renders are unchanged. Added on 2026-11-11.

153. **The Sound page's graphics, step 3 of M29 ([`track-pages.md`](track-pages.md) §1.5 and §2.1): the filter's curve grows to the large panel, 49 by 5 characters, with gridlines and a dashed mark at `Cut`; a sampler's Machine device shows a thumbnail of the slice it plays, with the sample page's teal start line and red end line at its `Start` and `Length`; and the Output device has a text meter of each side's level read right after `Trim`, so the trim can be set by eye** (#382; the meter's tap is the author's decision of 2026-11-10). **Why:** decision 149 gave the Sound page its devices but kept the curve and the waveform at their old 24 by 3, and nothing on the page answered "how hard is this track hitting its filter" or "what part of this sample is playing" without going to the mixer or the sample page. **Prior art:** Ableton's Auto Filter and its EQ Eight draw the response big with a frequency grid and a handle at the cutoff; Simpler's and the Digitakt's sample displays show the start and end over the whole sample; a console's channel strip has an input meter after its trim, and Ableton's Utility is set by watching the clip's meter. **The filter's curve:** the large panel at the terminal's pixels, 735 by 195 at the author's 15 by 39, drawn by `kitty::plot` as decision 141 has it, over a dotted 0 dB line and fainter dotted lines at 100 Hz, 1 kHz and 10 kHz, as the EQ's is (§1.5's frequency plots), from -36 dB to +24 dB as before. The mark at `Cut` is a dashed line up from the bottom to the curve, as the compressor's threshold is, at `Cut`'s own frequency, which is its share across, as both run evenly in pitch from 20 Hz to 20 kHz; an open filter, which is out of the way, has none. It follows the `Cut` and `Res` that play, as before, and is sent at most every other frame. **The thumbnail:** `sample_wave::Thumbnail` works out the slice the track's `Slice` is on, from the entry's points in the file's frames, and cuts it as the engine's `Region` does a note: `Start` and `Length` are shares of the slice from where it starts in play order, `Thru` runs on to the entry's end, and a reversed slice starts at its end, so its teal start line is the later one. It's drawn by the sample page's own `Drawing`, both channels in one lane, at one pixel for each of the screen's, with as many frames a character as leave the end's line whole inside the picture. What plays is the text's colour while the cursor is in the Machine, rather than the active green, which is hard to tell from the start's teal, and dim otherwise; the rest is fainter. It's drawn from the settings as they're set, not as modulation moves them, so it's sent once for each sample, slice, start and length, and only for a change (§1.5's rule 1); a file not yet analysed has none until it is. It replaces the old waveform's dots (`waveform_png`, now gone). **The Output meter:** the panel is text, 49 by 5 in its box: a blank row, `L` and `R` as horizontal eighths from -60 to +6 dB, 1.5 dB a character, each character in the level colour at its middle (`theme::level`), with a faint `▏` in the character of the highest it was lately (`meter::Held`, 1.5 s then falling 24 dB a second), and a faint scale, `-48` to `0`, each number ending under the character its level starts in. It's the peak, as a gain stage is set by its peaks. **The tap:** `TrackState::shape` keeps each side's highest level right after it multiplies by the trim and before the filter (`take_in_after_trim`, an `abs` and a `max` a side a frame, no allocation), and so does a group's, a return's and the master's `slots_and_level`, before its slots. `Engine::take_after_trim` takes the watched track's and starts every track's again each block, and the report puts it in `LiveValues::after_trim`; `Handle::latest` keeps the loudest of the reports since the last frame while they're for the same track. The mixer's meters, after the fader, are as they were. **Cost of the moving curve**, measured in a test (`the_filter_curve_under_an_lfo_on_cut_is_sent_at_most_every_other_frame`) that runs the engine, the app and `Graphics::sync` every 33 ms with an LFO sweeping `Cut` over a bar on a resonant `Acid`: 61 sends in 4 seconds, 15 a second, never two frames running; each a PNG of 1.5 to 2.0 KB, 1.85 KB on average; 37.6 KB a second written for it, base64 and commands included, while the scope over the main meter adds about 64 KB a second in the same frames; and 0.25 ms a frame to draw and send it in a release build. That's about a third more than decision 141's moving EQ curve (29 KB a second) and well under the scope, so the moving curve stays at 4 bits, and the 2-bit fallback the study kept in reserve isn't needed. What kitty itself spends showing it wasn't measured, as that needs a window. **Tests:** the curve within a pixel of `track_response_db` for five filters, with its 0 dB line, its 1 kHz line and the mark at `Cut`; an open filter with no mark; the thumbnail's lines at `Start` and `Length`, a slice, `Thru` and reversed; an image sent again only for a change, in each shade once; the meter's bars, held mark and scale; the level after the trim reading 12 dB more for 12 dB of trim whatever the filter and fader do, in the engine and in the app; the tap not allocating; and the four devices fitting 163 by 42 and the Keys box. Added on 2026-11-11.

154. **The Mod page's graphics, step 5 of M29 ([`track-pages.md`](track-pages.md) §1.5 and §2.2): every envelope's and LFO's panel is the study's half size, 24 by 5 characters (360 by 195 pixels at the author's 15 by 39); an LFO's panel draws one cycle of its wave from its `Phase`, with the engine's own wave function, and a dot rides it where the LFO is now, placed by the terminal with pixel offsets across and down and moved only by placing it again** (#384). **Why:** decision 151 left the envelopes at their old 28 by 4 and the LFOs with no picture, so `Phase` was a number you couldn't see and nothing showed where an LFO was in its cycle. **Prior art:** the Digitakt's and Analog Rytm's LFO pages, which draw the wave with a moving dot; Ableton Live's LFO device, whose wave display shows where it is; Bitwig's modulators, which animate their shape. **Envelopes:** drawn by `kitty::plot` as before, now over a dotted baseline and fainter dotted lines down the picture between attack and decay, decay and sustain, and sustain and release, as §1.5 has for time shapes, so each time in the grid has a part of the picture to point at; sent again only when their settings change. **LFO cycles:** `plot::LfoDrawing` is what a cycle is drawn from, its wave, its `Phase` in degrees and whether its depth is negative, which turns it upside down, so the picture is sent again only when one of those changes, never for `Rate`, the depth's size, the destination or `Retrig`. Each column is `vt_model::lfo::wave`, which the engine plays, at the cycle's position plus `Phase`; a jump inside the cycle (a square's middle, a saw's from a phase) has a point either side of it so it's drawn straight, a triangle's peaks have points so they stay sharp, and the right edge holds the end of the cycle rather than the next one's start, so a saw's fall isn't drawn up the box's edge. It's full height, over a dotted centre line. `Random` and `Smooth` draw the centre line alone, with their phase and sign left out of what's compared, as their values aren't known ahead and a made-up staircase would be untrue (decision 104's reason). A switched-out LFO's cycle is the faint shade, as decision 152 did for envelopes, kept beside its own so switching back sends nothing. **The engine's report:** `LiveValues::lfos`, for the watched track, is each LFO's `Option<LfoNow>`, how far round its wave it is (its live phase included, in 65,536ths of a turn) and the wave's value there before its depth (as an `i16`), filled in where `TrackState::modulate` already works each LFO out, in a fixed array, so the report never allocates; it's none for an LFO that isn't worked out (no destination, no depth, a destination not on the track, or switched out once its 5 ms fade is done), and `Engine::live` clears all three while the transport is stopped, as a note heard while stopped works them out once. **The dot:** a round, smooth-edged image `dot_pixels(cell)` across, a little over half a character and odd, 9 pixels at the author's, where the study said 5: at 2 device pixels a point on a Retina screen, 5 is barely wider than the 2-pixel line and hard to find while it moves. It's stored once in the dim shade and once in the focused one, with `a=t`, and each LFO's dot is a placement of one of them (placement id 1 to 3), so it follows its device's focus. `Graphics::place` works out each dot after the screen is drawn: across at where the LFO is in its wave less the cycle's `Phase`, up at its value, upside down for a negative depth (a random wave's dot included, though its centre line isn't flipped), centred there and kept inside the picture, then split into the character and the pixels into it across and down. `Placement` gains `down`, sent as `Y=`, left out when it's 0 so the sample page's lines and marks send what they did. The track page's drawing gives back where each LFO's picture is (`views::track::draw_finding_cycles` and `CyclesAt`, through `GraphicPanel::inside_at`), which `draw_screen` keeps in `App::lfo_cycles_at`, none for a cycle not drawn whole, as when the rack has gone by bands. A dot is placed again only when it moves or changes shade, taken off (`a=d,d=i`) while the song is stopped, for an LFO switched out or going nowhere, off the Mod page, and while anything is drawn over the page (`views::sample::overlaid`, as the sample page's lines are); its images are sent again after the window changes size, as a clear frees directly placed images. **Cost, measured** in `three_lfos_dots_move_by_placing_alone_at_about_60_bytes_each`, which runs the engine, the app, `Graphics::sync`, the drawing and `Graphics::place` every 33 ms for 4 seconds with a sine at a quarter, a triangle at an eighth and `Smooth` over a bar: 360 placements in 4 seconds, 90 a second, all three moving every frame; 61 bytes a placement, 5.4 KB a second, against the moving filter curve's 37.6 KB (decision 153) and the scope's 64 KB; the cycles sent again 0 times; 0.04 ms a frame to work out and write the placements in a test build. What kitty spends placing them wasn't measured, as that needs a window. **Left for later:** a modulator going nowhere is still drawn dim, not faint as §1.6 has it, as for envelopes since decision 151; the envelopes' riding dot, which needs each envelope's stage and level in `LiveValues`. **Tests:** each drawn wave within a pixel of `lfo::wave` from three phases and both signs, its jumps straight and its centre line there; the random waves' centre line alone, and what changes a cycle's picture; each envelope's shape within a pixel of its stages, its sustain flat at its level, its baseline and its stage lines; the dot round and solid; the engine reporting the watched track's LFO where its wave and phase say, moving on at the rate, none for one going nowhere or switched out, none while stopped, and never allocating; the dot within a pixel of where the reported phase and value put it on the cycle, flipped for a negative depth, placed again with no image sent as it moves, nothing sent when it doesn't, swapping image with the focus, gone when switched out, under the help, when stopped and off the page; a cycle sent again only for its wave, phase or sign, faint when switched out and its own placed again when back; and the panels' places at 163 by 42 and in the Keys box, with the terminal told where each cycle is. The golden renders are unchanged, and the Mod page's screenshot now plays three steps, so LFO 1's dot shows at the bottom of its triangle. Added on 2026-11-11.

155. **A group's, a return's and the master's Sound page is the Output device, step 7 of M29 ([`track-pages.md`](track-pages.md) §2.3): the same grid as a sound track's, with holes where a fixed track lacks a setting; under a group's, a read-only Tracks of the tracks it sums; under a return's, a read-only Sends in of every track and group sending to it, with each send; and the master's `Limiter` moves off the project page into the master's Output, after `Level`** (#386; the move is the author's decision of 2026-11-10). **Why:** decision 149 made a sound track's Sound page a rack, and left the fixed tracks as the last rows of settings in a corner of an empty page that said nothing of what the track is or what feeds it, while which tracks send to a return could only be found strip by strip on the mixer. The limiter, the last thing the master does, was on the project page, away from the chain it ends. **Prior art:** Ableton's group track lists what's in it by folding, and its return tracks are fed by each track's send knobs with no list of their own; a console's aux return and Bitwig's FX tracks are the same, which is the gap Sends in fills; a master limiter sits at the end of the master's chain in Ableton and Bitwig, and the Digitakt's and Octatrack's master FX and its output settings are on the master's own page. **The Output:** `sound::devices` gives a fixed track one device, `Output`, from the same columns as a sound track's: a group has `Trim` `Level` `Pan` and `Send A` `Send B` with a hole where `Group` is, as a group can't be in one; a return has `Trim` `Level` `Pan`, with holes where the sends and `Group` are; the master has `Trim` and `Level`, a hole where `Pan` is, and `Limiter` in Send A's place, beside `Trim`, as the study's mockup has it, so down the columns it reads as the master plays: `Trim`, its slots, `Level`, the limiter. The Output keeps decision 153's meter after the trim on a group and a return, whose tap is before their slots; the master's has none, as the main meter beside it already shows what it plays (§1.5, rule 5). Every page is now a rack, so `TrackPage::page_rows`, `GROUP_ROWS`, `RETURN_ROWS`, `MASTER_ROWS` and the fixed tracks' row-table drawing are gone, and `{` and `}` on a fixed track's Sound page have only its Output to go to. **What feeds it:** drawn under the Output as a device of the same frame, not focused, and not one of the page's devices, so the cursor never stops in it, as §2.3 has it; jumping to a track from it is left for later. Its places are the grid's, three down each of four columns as the Output's settings go, and past 12 the last says `+N more`, which is the study's own limit. A group's Tracks are each track's number and name, in the group's colour, dim while muted, with its own mute or solo glyph after it (decision 126), or `None`. A return's Sends in are each sound track and group whose send to it isn't zero, in the song's order, a track by its number and name and a group by its letters, as `G1`, each in its group's colour if it has one, cut to the name's room, then its send as the Output shows one, `0C00` with the fine part dim, and its bar; there's no live value, as the engine reports only the watched track's. Both are worked out from the song each frame, so they follow `:group`, a send turned up or down, and undo, at no cost to keep. **The limiter:** `Control::Limiter`, which only the master has, drawn as the toggle glyph from `widgets/toggle.rs`, green on and faint off. `K` and `i` switch it, as on the project page; `=` puts it on and `-` takes it off, doing nothing if it's already so; `x` puts it back on, as a new song has it. Each goes through `:set limiter`'s own `Line::SetLimiter`, so it's one undo step with the same status (`Limiter off: renders keep levels over 0…`), and the status line's value says `on` or `off`. `song.limiter`, how it's saved and what the engine does with it are unchanged, and `:set limiter` stays. The project page loses its `Limiter` row (`ProjectControl::Limiter` is gone), and the guides that sent you there, the project page's and the meter's, now send you to the master. **Screenshots:** a new one, `track-return`, of return B's page with three tracks and group 1 sending to it, in the manual's introduction; the project page's changes as its row goes. **Tests:** each fixed track's Output shows every setting it has and none it hasn't, and the keys reach each; a group's Tracks follows `:group` in and out and undo, shows mutes and solos, `+5 more` past 12, and `None`; a return's Sends in lists each sender with its send and bar, follows sends turned up and down to nothing, is return A's or B's own, says `None`, and `+N more` past 12; the master's `Limiter` is a toggle in its colour, and `K`, `i`, `=`, `-` and `x` switch `song.limiter`, each one undo step, `-` twice leaving none; only the master has it; the project page shows no limiter; and the Output and what feeds it fit 163 by 42 and the Keys box, with the meter on a group and a return and none on the master. The golden renders are unchanged. Added on 2026-11-11.

156. **The web manual carries its own Nerd Font symbols: Symbols Nerd Font Mono from Nerd Fonts v3.5.1, cut down to the symbols `glyphs.rs` names, 32 of them in a 3.2 KB WOFF2 that the manual's CSS adds to its body and code fonts for the private use areas only; and the screenshots draw each symbol as its outline from the same font, an SVG path defined once a picture and placed in each cell that shows it** (the author noticed on 2026-11-11 that the manual's icons didn't show). **Why:** a browser has no Nerd Font, so the guides' toggles, leaf, transport and track icons showed as boxes, and an SVG shown with `<img>` can't use the page's fonts at all, whatever its `font-family` asks for. **Prior art:** Nerd Fonts' own cheat sheet and most projects' documentation load a Nerd Font web font; mdBook's `additional-css` for a theme's fonts; `pyftsubset` for cutting a font to the characters a page uses. **Considered for the pictures:** the cut-down font as a data URI in each SVG's `<style>`, about 4.3 KB a picture, which worked but in Chrome sometimes drew the first symbol as a box while the font was still loading, as nothing draws a picture shown with `<img>` again; drawing outlines needs no font, can't race, and only carries the symbols the picture shows. **Vendored, not linked:** the font and its `LICENSE` (MIT) and a `NOTICE` naming the icon sets' licences (Material Design Icons, Apache 2.0; Font Awesome, CC BY 4.0; Fira Code's spinner, OFL 1.1) are in `docs/manual/src/symbols`, so the manual needs no CDN. **Kept in step:** `just symbols-font` (`scripts/symbols-font.sh`) downloads the pinned release, cuts it to the symbols in `glyphs.rs`, and writes `screenshot/symbols.rs` with `scripts/symbol-paths.py`; a test fails when `glyphs.rs` names a symbol with no outline. **Size:** an outline is drawn one cell wide and as tall, in the middle of the cell, as kitty draws a Mono symbol in one cell. Added on 2026-11-11.

157. **Every on/off toggle is drawn over two cells: its glyph, then a plain space in the same colours, which kitty and Ghostty draw the glyph across, so it's twice as wide as the one-cell pill it was** (the author's request of 2026-11-11, "the on/off toggles are too small I want them bigger"). **Why:** `nf-fa-toggle_on` and `nf-fa-toggle_off` are twice as wide as tall, and decision 140 kept each to one cell with a no-break space after it, as decision 103 keeps the explorer's icons, which shrank the switch to a small dot that was hard to read at a glance. **Prior art:** Ableton's device activator and Bitwig's power button are about as big as a letter is tall; the Elektron boxes' on/off parameters fill their value's place. **Tried in real windows,** kitty 0.50 and Ghostty 1.4.0, each captured on its own, on the FX page with a Glue, an EQ3 bypassed and a Limit, and on the Mod page: (a) the same glyphs before a plain space, which both terminals draw across two cells; (b) `nf-md-toggle_switch` and `nf-md-toggle_switch_off` (U+F0521 and U+F0522) before a plain space, which stayed one cell and no bigger, as Material Design's square box leaves the pill a third of it. (a) was the clear choice in both, and with the cursor's background over both its cells it reads as one block. **Width:** nothing moves. A toggle already took two columns, the glyph and its no-break space, so a device's title, whose second cell is the first column of the gap after it, and each setting's value cell, the sample page's `Snap` and `Reverse` and the master's `Limiter` are as they were. `widgets::toggle::span` and `text` are both cells together, `toggle::WIDTH` is 2, and an empty slot's room is that wide, so no caller can leave the second cell to something else. **The explorer's hint** had its name straight after the no-break space, which the wider glyph now covers, so a space goes between the switch and its name, as `H`, the switch, a space and `hidden`; that makes the sample explorer's hint 56 wide, so the explorer's list (`EXPLORER_WIDTH`) grows from 54 to 57, keeping a dash of its border after the hint with the preview off, as before. **The explorer's icons stay** at one cell before `ICON_SPACE`, as decision 103 has them, so every row's icon is the same size; the same plain space would make most of them bigger, but not the narrow ones, so rows would differ. **Screenshots:** the SVGs, which draw each symbol as its outline (decision 156), draw a switch before a plain space across both cells too (`screenshot::symbol_cells`), so the manual shows what kitty and Ghostty do; every other symbol keeps to its cell there, as before. **Tests:** a toggle is two cells on or off and ends in a plain space; on the FX page, Auto switched on under the cursor and the slot bypassed change exactly those two toggles' glyphs, every other character where it was, each toggle's second cell a space in its colours and back again; the same on the Mod page with an LFO's Retrig and its switch; the sample page's Snap and Reverse with both cells in their colours; the sample explorer's hint whole with the preview on and off; and in a screenshot, a switch before a plain space drawn across two cells and one at a line's end across one. Added on 2026-11-11.

158. **The Roto-Control gets a crate of its own, `vt-roto`: typed SysEx (API 1.6, every command each way), the CCs on channel 16, and the serial API (1.3, every request, reply and notice), a handshake with no I/O, a link on a thread of its own that finds the Roto's ports by name and reconnects, a fake Roto in memory that speaks both protocols, and `vimtracker roto-probe`; vimtracker answers the Roto's ping as Bitwig Studio; and nothing it does reaches the song yet** (#410, step 1 of M30, [`roto-control.md`](roto-control.md)). **Why:** the author's rule is that the Roto is a second, separate way of working with its own view of the song, so it needs a protocol layer, tested without hardware, that the later steps (#411 to #415) build the view on. **Prior art:** Melbourne Instruments' MIT-licensed Ableton, Bitwig and Logic integrations, whose tables, CC numbers and handshake order this adapts (the crate's `NOTICE`), and the PDFs' errata they show, listed in `sysex.rs` and `serial.rs`. **The crate** depends on no other vimtracker crate, as the Roto knows nothing of songs; `vt-tui` uses it, for now only for the probe. **Bitwig, not Ableton or Logic:** the ping's answer must name one of the three; Bitwig's mode is the one driven by the program throughout, with macro plugins of as many pages as it says and each control's name and feel sent live in `LEARN PARAM`, which suits devices defined in code, and it keeps vimtracker's session apart from Live's; Logic's learns by sweeping parameters, and Ableton's ignores a macro plugin's page count. **The handshake** is the scripts': `DAW STARTED`, the Roto's `PING DAW`, the answer, `ROTO-DAW CONNECTED`, then the firmware and API versions; nothing else is sent before it's connected, every ping is answered, and a ping while connected starts it over, as another program's `DAW STARTED` does. **The link's thread** takes midir's bytes and the edit thread's requests on one channel, splits the bytes into messages, answers pings itself, decodes 14-bit knobs, leaves 5 ms between SysEx messages as Ableton's script does, and checks every second that the ports are there, saying `Lost` and starting again when they come back; the edit thread will take its events each frame, as it takes the loader's. **The serial port** is opened only while it's needed, so the Roto-Setup app can open it otherwise, and of the Roto's two USB serial ports the one that answers `GET FW VERSION` is the API's; `SerialLink::ask` refuses any request that changes the Roto. **Dependencies:** `midir` 0.11.1 (CoreMIDI and ALSA, sharing cpal's `alsa`, so no new system library) and `serialport` 4.10.1 without `libudev`, so it builds on Linux with only the ALSA headers the release already installs. **Checked on the author's Roto** (firmware 3.3.0, API 1.6, read-only): connected in 456 ms; it doesn't say its mode on connecting, so the fake doesn't either and the mode is read over serial; 5 of its 64 PLUGIN setups are Ableton's; and a stored knob's reply showed two-byte values are 8-bit, high first, which the PDF doesn't say, and is a test. **Open for the author** (§8 of the note): stored setups, which vimtracker's 55 device types would nearly fill, or live macro devices; sharing with Live and the setting's default; a device's knob order; and the transport buttons. **Tests:** every SysEx message, serial request with its reply, notice and CC written and read back, each list checked to cover every kind; the documented bytes of several; a real Roto's reply read; the handshake against the fake in order, from a plug-in's ping, and again after a ping; nothing acted on before connecting; the link connecting on its thread, passing on a knob and sending a knob's position, and reconnecting after an unplug; a setup written in a config update and read back, refused outside one, and the store full at 64. Added on 2026-11-11.

159. **The Roto-Control shows vimtracker's devices live, as Bitwig Studio's script shows a device's remote controls, storing nothing on it; Roto support is a setting on the audio page, off by default and kept for the machine in `roto.ron`; a device's knobs go row by row through its grid, then its title; and the transport buttons Live has beyond play, stop and record stay unassigned** (#411, step 2 of M30, the author's answers of 2026-11-11 to the four questions of [`roto-control.md`](roto-control.md) §8). **Why:** stored setups would take 55 of the Roto's 64, beside the author's five Ableton ones, and every device added later would take another; live devices take none and are sent as they're needed. Off by default, so opening vimtracker with Live running never takes the Roto from it. Row by row, so a column's settings are always four knobs apart. **Prior art:** Bitwig Studio's Roto-Control script, whose macro plugins this follows (`PLUGIN DETAILS` type macro with its pages, `LEARN PARAM` answering the Roto's `CONTROL MAPPED`); Live's own MIDI preferences, where a control surface is chosen per machine and set to None to let another program have it; Logic Pro's script, which colours its controls with `SET PLUGIN CONTROL DETAILS`. **The mapping** (`vt-tui`'s `roto/devices.rs`) is pure, a track and its song in and the Roto's devices out: each `DeviceSpec` of the Sound page, then the FX page's slots, then the Mod page's, is a macro device of one or two pages of eight knobs, its grid row by row with holes kept, then its title's controls, an empty last page left off. A knob's feel is its setting's kind, as #411's table has it: a list of up to 24 clicks through its choices, named up to 16; a longer list, or one that changes with the track or the song (`Dest`, the song's samples, the accumulator's 28 degrees), turns smoothly; an off/on setting is two steps named `Off` and `On`, as a macro device maps no switches; a centred value (`Pan`, `Pitch`, `Trim`, a depth, an EQ band) has a centre notch; anything else turns smoothly. Names are the track page's, an effect's settings by their own, cut to 12 characters, which none needs today. **Colours** are by the setting's family wherever it is, from the app's palette through `colour::nearest`: level and dynamics green (as the meters at a safe level), the filter and EQ blue, pan teal, time and pitch yellow, modulation mauve, character peach, and a choice of machine, effect, sample or type in the text's colour; a setting in the registry goes by its units, so an effect's `Gain` is green and its `Low cut` blue. **The send path** (`vt-roto`'s `live::PluginView`, with no I/O) answers the link's events, which `App::tick` takes each frame: the plugin list on connecting and on each `SET PLUGIN MODE`, then for each `CONTROL MAPPED` a `LEARN PARAM` and a `SET PLUGIN CONTROL DETAILS`, or `UNMAP CONTROL` for a hole; `ROTO SELECT PLUGIN` chooses a device and the arrows' page needs no answer; and as a stepped or off/on knob turns, its step's name in `SHOW CONTROL VALUE`, as the Roto draws no value of its own (Ableton's script sends every value so). Nothing opens the serial port, so no setup can be written. **For now** PLUGIN mode shows the devices of the track the cursor was on when the Roto connected or was put in PLUGIN mode, so each kind can be checked on the Roto; choosing the track from the Roto, and the knobs' edits, are #413's, and MIX mode #412's. **Checked on the author's Roto** (firmware 3.3.0), each read back with the serial API's read-only `GET CURRENT PLUGIN` and `GET PLUGIN KNOB CONFIG`: the app's own path showed `Acid`'s knobs with their names, colours and feel; the filter's second page, `1 Delay`'s two pages (its `Time` of 18 steps, `Ping-pong` of 2, `Effect` of 19), `LFO 1` and the arpeggio read back the same; colours work in live mode; and the stored setups were the same five Ableton ones before and after. It also showed four things the PDF doesn't say: `SET PLUGIN MODE` carries a byte, `00`, which `sysex.rs` now reads; `LEARN PARAM` sent before the Roto asks, or for a page it isn't showing, is dropped; `SET PLUGIN CONTROL DETAILS` numbers a knob from 1 across the pages, as `LEARN PARAM` does; and a macro plugin's arrows send `PAGE LEFT` and `PAGE RIGHT`, so the page shown is taken from the knobs the Roto asks for. The author, at the Roto, saw the names, the colours, the knobs' feel and the pages as sent, and no value as a knob turned: step names in `LEARN PARAM` read back empty and aren't shown, which is why each step's name is now sent as it turns, and with that the author saw each step's name flash up as the knob turned. `vimtracker roto-probe` now says `DAW STARTED` as it ends too, so it gives the Roto back as the app does. A smooth knob's value is the setting's as the app formats it, which needs the knobs to edit the song first, so it's #414's. **Turning it off,** or quitting, sends `DAW STARTED` and stops the link's thread before the Roto's ping can come back, so a running Live with the Roto's script answers it and takes the Roto. **Tests:** every device of every machine, effect, filter and fixed track places each of its settings once, row by row then the title, with a knob for each the track has; every name fits 12 characters uncut; every position is on the knob and every list 2 to 24 steps; each kind's feel; a knob where its setting is; each family one colour and the families seven; against the fake Roto, which now answers `DAW SELECT PLUGIN` with `SET PLUGIN MODE` and `CONTROL MAPPED` as the real one does: the list as macro devices of their pages, each knob learned with its name, feel, position and colour, holes unmapped, the second page, a device chosen with SEL, the same list again answered with nothing new, a stepped or off/on knob turned showing its step's name and a smooth one or a hole nothing, the page following the knobs asked for and the arrows, and a whole session through the link's thread with no serial request, no setup stored and `DAW STARTED` last; in the app, off until turned on, `=` on the audio page connecting and showing the cursor's track's devices, `-` handing it back with nothing sent after, the Play device's `Speed` turned to the top showing `1/4`, the setting saved and read back, and the row saying it's connected with the firmware. Added on 2026-11-11.

160. **The Roto-Control's knobs edit the song as keys do, on the Roto's own track and device whatever the cursor is on, touch to let-go one undo step; each value is shown on the knob's screen as the app writes it, at most 12 times a second; what the song changes goes back to the knobs' motors, never to one being touched; the Roto chooses its track with FUNC and its device with SEL held, and switches an FX slot or a modulator with SEL tapped, as the vendor's MODE, FUNC and SEL model has it; and off/on settings stay two-step knobs, as the Roto refuses switches on a live device** (#413, step 4 of M30, [`roto-control.md`](roto-control.md) §4.2). **Why:** the author's rule is that the Roto is a second, separate way of working, so a knob must edit its own setting whatever the screen shows, and the Roto must choose for itself what it shows. **Prior art:** the vendor's Ableton and Bitwig scripts, whose button model this follows as the Roto's manual describes it (FUNC's track list, SEL held to choose and tapped to switch devices on and off, both counted from the bank shown), whose `VALUE_UPDATE_FPS` of 12 paces the values, and whose `sendPluginSelect` sends a device's choice only from the bank shown; a held key's squashed undo step (`History::squash_to`) for a gesture. **Edits** (`vt-tui`'s `roto/edits.rs`, pure) are the way back from #411's mapping: a list's step, a value's share of its range with its fine digits, an envelope's time by `=`'s steps, an LFO's rate the nearest of its own kind; a knob left where its setting is gives no action, so it leaves no undo step; each knob's last move of a frame is one `App::apply`. A gesture closes 300 ms after the last move with no knob touched, so a turn without a touch is one step too. **Values** are the status line's text in units where the setting has them (`-14.8 dB`, `1.21 kHz`) and the track page's otherwise (`1/16`, `Overdrive`), shown as a knob turns or is touched, and as the keys, an undo or a song move it. **Feedback** (`vt-roto`'s `PluginView::refresh`) sends only what changed after each new version of the song: the list if a device's name, state or pages did, a knob learned again with the Roto's own hash if its name, feel or colour did, and otherwise its position as CCs; a touched knob isn't moved, and goes where its setting is once let go, and a knob just turned takes its new position unsent. **Tracks** (`live::TrackList`) are every track in the song's order, a sound track as `Track 01` or with its name, a fixed one by its title, a group's tracks in its colour and a group marked as one; the Roto's first track is the cursor's when it first connects and its own choice after. **Switching** maps the Roto's device enabled state to an FX slot's bypass and an envelope's or an LFO's switch, both ways; the other devices, the machine, `Filter`, `Output`, `Play`, `Accumulator` and `Arpeggio`, have no switch, so they're always on (an empty slot always off), and a tap on one is ignored and the list sent again; the real Roto flips such a light for a moment as it's pressed, its own drawing, and shows it as sent the next time the list comes up, and a value shown on a button in the list (`Always on`) wasn't seen, so it isn't sent. Whether `Accumulator` and `Arpeggio` should get a switch of their own is the author's to decide. **The bank is the Roto's:** it stays where SEL's arrows left it, as sending it from the chosen device's bank turned the Roto back to the first bank on a toggle. **A choice wins over a switch:** with SEL's switches up, holding SEL and pressing a device makes the real Roto switch it as the button goes down and choose it 3 to 4 seconds later as SEL is let go, which neither vendor script guards against; tapping SEL, then holding it and pressing a device, makes the real Roto switch it as the button goes down and, as SEL is let go, show it and ask for its knobs, with `ROTO SELECT PLUGIN` 3 to 4 seconds later or without it, which neither vendor script guards against; so after a `SET PLUGIN ENABLED`, within 10 seconds and with nothing else between, a `ROTO SELECT PLUGIN` for the same device or a request for knobs vimtracker didn't cause means the Roto chose the device it switched: the switch is taken back with `History::take_back`, leaving no undo step and nothing to redo, or switched back as an edit if the song changed meanwhile, and the device is answered with its own knobs, which the Roto otherwise showed under the device chosen before. **SEL's views are the firmware's:** the Roto doesn't tell a Live or Bitwig host when SEL is held or which view is up (`SET PLUGIN ENABLE MODE` and `SET PLUGIN SELECT MODE` are Logic Pro's), so the lights can't be put out while SEL chooses, and holding and letting go of SEL leaves the tapped view with nothing sent by vimtracker, which is the vendor's to answer. **Switches on buttons were tried and refused** on firmware 3.3.0: `SET PLUGIN CONTROL DETAILS` of type Switch, a `LEARN PARAM` past the 64 knobs and the buttons' light CCs gave a live device no switch, the Roto sent `CONTROL MAPPED` for knobs alone, and the serial API's read-only `GET PLUGIN SWITCH CONFIG` answered `NoPlugin` for every switch; only a stored setup has switches, and nothing is stored (decision 159). **For #415:** following the cursor, and remembering the Roto's track between runs; MIX mode is #412's. **Checked on the author's Roto** (firmware 3.3.0, every message logged each way, the five stored Ableton setups the same before and after): `Output`'s `Level` edited and showed `dB` as it turned, `Play`'s `Speed` clicked through its divisions with their names, a knob let go and touched again didn't jump or push, FUNC chose tracks and paged through the groups, SEL's switches turned envelopes and LFOs off and on and stayed on the second bank, `Level` set from the keyboard turned knob 5's motor in one move each time, from the bottom to the top, with its value shown, a device chosen from the second bank was the one pressed, and tapping SEL then holding it to choose a device chose it with its own knobs and left it switched as it was. **Tests:** every knob of every kind of device left where its setting is edits nothing, and turned to either end or a third of the way makes an edit the song takes that then holds; a smooth knob sets the fine digits; values read as the app writes them; against the fake Roto, a knob edits the Roto's track while the cursor is on another, touch to let-go is one undo step and an untouched turn one too, a stepped knob goes through its list showing each name, a touched knob is never sent its own value and goes where the keys put its setting once let go while another knob follows the keys with its value shown, values are paced with the last always shown, SEL switches a slot and an LFO counted from the second bank and `b` shows on the Roto with the bank kept, a tap on the accumulator changes nothing, a choice with SEL's switches up leaves the device as it was and no undo step, FUNC chooses the track and pages to the master; and in `vt-roto`, a refresh sends only what changed, the bank stays and buttons count from it, and the track list pages in eights. Added on 2026-11-11.

161. **The Roto-Control is laid out from data, not from the screen: every kind of device has a declared layout of named pages, each eight knob slots over eight button slots holding a setting by its key, a gap on purpose, or the row's end, by the same musical rules for every device; the Roto plays and tweaks and the app builds, so no machine, effect or effect type is chosen on it; colour is by the setting's family from one table, in vivid schemes; and the devices with off/on settings are stored on the Roto as vimtracker's own setups, so those settings are on its buttons, lit from the song, while every other device stays live** (#419 and its comment, the author's requests, rules and the middle path chosen on 2026-11-11, [`roto-control.md`](roto-control.md) §4.3). **Why:** the track page's grid is laid out for the screen, so following it row by row (decision 159) put an envelope's `Attack` and `Release` far apart and an effect's settings wherever the grid had room; the Roto is a row of eight knobs over eight buttons and a separate way of working, so it gets its own order. **The layout** (`vt-roto`'s `layout::Layout`, `vt-tui`'s `roto/layouts.rs`) is one shape for every machine, effect, track device and modulator: `Page { name, knobs: [Slot; 8], buttons: [Slot; 8] }`, a `Slot` being `Setting`, `Gap` or `Unused`; a setting by the key songs store it under (`cutoff`, `glueratio`) or, for the track's own, a name of its own (`level`, `attack`); with an optional colour or turn per slot; and `stored` per kind of device. The mapping (`roto/devices.rs`) reads only the layouts. **The rules:** the Roto plays and tweaks, the app builds (the author's), so a machine, an effect and an effect's type or mode are chosen with `K` in the app, as each swaps the device's whole layout under the hand, while musical lists stay (a ratio's steps, an LFO's shape, Acid's `Wave`, the filter's type and mode); what's played most comes first, in the order the sound meets it (an envelope's attack, decay, sustain and release; cutoff and resonance; threshold and ratio, then times; a 303's cutoff, resonance, env mod, decay and accent, which the author called perfect); an effect's `Mix` and `Gain` are always knobs 7 and 8 and every track's `Level` knob 1 and `Trim` knob 5; a device keeps to one page, its off/on settings on that page's buttons beside its main knobs, unless it has more than eight knobs or buttons or a page meant for it, as a modulator's `Dest`, a long list easy to bump, alone on its second page; and a button is always under a knob of what it goes with, as the Roto draws a button's label only under a knob that's set, so a restart is under what it starts again (`Reset` under `Attack`, `Retrig` under an LFO's `Phase` and the arpeggio's `Mode`), Glue's `Auto` under `Makeup` and `Peak Clip` under `Mix`. The author approved Acid's, the envelope's and the arpeggio's layouts on the Roto. **A stored knob's feel is its setup's:** the Roto keeps at most 16 steps a stored knob and a live answer can't add more, and a device can't mix stored and live knobs, so the LFO stays stored for its labelled `Retrig` and its `Rate` turns smoothly, its value shown and a note value staying one, the author's choice over a live LFO with 24 clicks and `Retrig` on a knob. **Text is ASCII,** as the Roto's font draws nothing else: `°` is written `deg` (`90deg`), `×` `x`, a dash `-`. **Prior art:** Elektron's pages, where a parameter keeps its knob from one machine to the next, and a Digitakt's envelope in ADSR order; a TB-303's panel; Ableton's and Bitwig's mixer strips, level first; Bitwig's remote-control pages. **Page names** are in the layouts for the guide and the note only: SysEx API 1.6 has no way to show one (`MACRO PLUGIN PAGE INDEX`, `PAGE LEFT` and `PAGE RIGHT` carry only an index), and neither vendor script sends one. **Buttons, tried on the author's Roto** (firmware 3.3.0): on a live macro device its buttons send nothing, not even their CCs, take no light from a CC and show no label, so #419's option (c) is out; on a setup stored over the serial API they show their stored names and colours, send CCs 20 to 27 on channel 16, and light as the program sends those back, and a stored knob clicks through its steps once its `CONTROL MAPPED` is answered with `LEARN PARAM`, macro flag off, as Ableton's script does (unanswered, it sends nothing). **The author chose the middle path** of #419's comment, option (b) for the devices that need it: the Compressor's three types, Delay, Grains, each envelope, LFO and the arpeggio, and the master's output are stored, 9 setups, and every other device is live; a live device's buttons go on two-step knobs instead, above their buttons where those are free, so one layout drives both, and a stored kind is shown so until its setup is on the Roto or if it can't be written. **Push buttons, lit from the song:** a press switches the setting, one undo step, and the light is sent back from the song, so keys and undo light it too; the Roto's `TOGGLE` mode keeps a state of its own, which drifted from the song on the hardware. A light is its family's scheme when on and scheme 70, dark, when off, as Ableton's stored switches are. **Writing only vimtracker's own** (`vt-roto`'s `store.rs`, the author's terms): each setup is under `PluginHash::of("vimtracker:<kind>")` and named `vt ...`; the store is listed before anything is written, and a setup under one of those hashes that isn't named so stops it, as does a store without room; every change goes through `Guarded`, which refuses anything but vimtracker's own hashes, and never sends a factory reset; a setup is written only when it's missing or its version, a hash of every byte written, differs from the one kept in `roto-setups.ron` in the cache folder; and `vimtracker roto-probe --clear-own` lists vimtracker's, asks for `yes`, and clears those alone. It runs on a thread of its own as the Roto connects, the serial port tried three times a second apart. A notice the Roto sends straight after a reply, as `PLUGIN SELECTED` when a device is chosen meanwhile, is taken as a notice, which before failed the writing. **Colours** keep decision 159's families, now one table (`FAMILY_COLOURS`) that the guide's legend repeats word for word, with an effect's `Mix` as level and a ratio or time chosen from a list as level or time; the schemes are the vivid ones of the same hues (19 green, 22 blue, 20 teal, 17 yellow, 24 violet, 15 orange, 13 white), as the nearest to the app's pastels read too alike on the Roto. **Checked on the author's Roto** (firmware 3.3.0): the five Ableton setups the same before and after every run, read with the read-only `roto-probe`; the 9 setups written beside them, and rewritten only when their layouts changed; and at the Roto, the author saw Acid's, the envelope's and the arpeggio's pages as they should be, Glue's `Auto` and `Peak Clip` on its one page, labelled, switching both ways and lit from the song, LFO 1's `Retrig` labelled under `Phase`, its `Rate` showing each value as it turned and its `Phase` in `deg`. The first checks found what the rules above answer: a button over an empty knob had no label, the LFO's `Rate` sent 24 steps live turned smoothly, `°` showed as `?`, and the Glue's buttons were on a second page when the writing had failed on a notice and it fell back to live. **Tests:** every kind of device has a layout and every layout a kind; every setting but the app's own placed exactly once, by a key its device has, live and stored; no machine, effect or type picker on the Roto; no row with a setting after its end, no row ending in a gap, no empty page; one page unless the first is full or the next is `Dest`, and buttons on the first; every button under a knob; no more than eight knobs and eight buttons a page and eight pages a device; the ADSR order, the modulators' first pages, `Dest` alone on their second, `Mix` and `Gain` on knobs 7 and 8 and `Level` and `Trim` on knobs 1 and 5; off/on settings on buttons and only they, and their devices stored; a live device's buttons on free knobs; a slot's colour and turn; an LFO's rate smooth, a note value staying one; every name, step and value the Roto is sent in ASCII it draws, a phase as `0deg`; the colour table and the guide's legend; every stored setup fitting the Roto with a stable version; in `vt-roto`, a button placed above its button and the overflow page, a setup written once and not again until it changes, written again with no record, another program's setup under vimtracker's hash and a full store left alone, the guard refusing every other kind of change, clearing removing vimtracker's alone, and a stored knob's steps; and against the fake Roto, the 9 setups written beside Live's under vimtracker's hashes alone, the Glue listed as stored, its knobs answered as a stored device's, `Auto`'s button switching it both ways with one undo step a press and its light following, the keys lighting it too, an empty button doing nothing, and without the serial port the devices live with their buttons on knobs. Added on 2026-11-11.

162. **The mouse wheel and the trackpad are a knob on the cursor's setting: each tick up or down is `=` or `-` where the cursor is, in the setting's finest step, with quick ticks taking more of that step at a time up to one of `=`'s; Shift, or scrolling sideways, is `+` or `_`; one gesture is one undo step, ended by 300 ms without a tick or by any key; and it's a setting on the audio page, `Wheel`, off by default and kept for the machine in `wheel.ron`, which asks kitty and Ghostty for their mouse reports only while it's on** (#425, the author's request of 2026-11-11). **Why:** a precise, portable complement to Cmd-hjkl and the Roto, on a laptop with nothing else plugged in. **Prior art:** Ableton Live and Bitwig Studio, where the wheel over a control turns it; the Roto's touch to let-go as one undo step (decision 160), whose `History::squash_to` and 300 ms pause this shares; macOS's own scroll acceleration, which turns a quick flick into a long move and a slow one into a short move; Live's MIDI preferences and decision 159's Roto row, for an input that's off until turned on, per machine. **Where the pointer is** is ignored, and clicks and drags do nothing: the cursor says what's turned, as with the keys and the Roto. **The keys it stands for:** a tick is a press of `=`, `-`, `+` or `_` through `App::press`, in whatever mode binds them, so it works wherever they work by decision 148's audit, the shape panel and a locked selection included, where it moves every value and keeps the selection as `=` does; it does nothing while keys are typed before it (an operator waiting, a register) or where `=` means nothing (Insert, the command line, a picker), and `.` still repeats the last change made with keys. **Finest step:** on a value with fine digits (the Sound, Mod and FX pages' sweeping settings, a phrase's sweeping FX value) the cursor is put on its fine digits for the tick and back after, so a tick is a 256th of `=`'s step; on the tempo, its hundredths; on the sample page, a point's frame, Cmd-l and Cmd-h, where `=` moves a pixel; everywhere else, `=`'s own step. A selection moves by `=`'s step, as its values are whole. **Speed:** a 256th a tick alone would take 65,280 ticks to cross a value, so a tick on a finer step is worth the square of how many ticks came in the last 100 ms, itself included, up to one of `=`'s steps: a slow turn is one fine step a tick and lands anywhere, a steady scroll a few dozen, and a flick of 16 ticks in 100 ms a whole step each, so a sweep crosses the range. This is the one choice beyond the issue, which asked for the finest step and expected a trackpad's bursts to give acceleration naturally; the curve is `wheel::tick_size`, for the author to tune by feel. **Shift** is `+` and `_` where the cursor is, with no fine part and no speed: the large step, as the keys take it. macOS turns Shift and a mouse's wheel into a sideways scroll, so sideways is the large step too, right raising and left lowering. **Undo:** the gesture's undo depth is kept at its first tick; once the wheel is still for 300 ms (each frame checks) or any key but a modifier is pressed, everything since is squashed into one step, and in a selection `grammar::Adjustments` now keeps each undo step's changes together and joins the gesture's, so `u` and `C-r` take the running total back and forward with it. **Capture:** the main loop sends `CSI ? 1000 h` and `CSI ? 1006 h` (buttons and wheel, as SGR reports, no motion) when the setting turns on and their `l` when it turns off, on quitting and after a panic; in kitty and Ghostty, Shift and a drag still select text while it's on. **Tests** (`tests/wheel.rs`, through crossterm's `MouseEvent`): off until turned on with nothing done, on and off from its row, saved and read back; up and down a semitone, Shift, right and left the large step, and a hex value's large step; clicks, drags and moves doing nothing; a gesture one undo step after a pause or a key, two gestures two; a phrase FX value's fine digits with carry, the cursor left on the value, and a burst of 20 ticks moving `0x9D8`; the Sound page's `Cut` in fine steps and on its fine digits; the tempo in hundredths; a locked selection moved, kept, its turn one undo with its total, and redone whole; nothing with an operator waiting or in Insert; and `.` repeating the key's change. Added on 2026-11-11.

163. **The wheel's feel, amending decision 162: up and down and sideways are one knob, the fingers up or right raising and down or left lowering, with Shift alone the large step; the value follows the fingers, as a level bar fills, flipped under macOS's natural scrolling, read as the program starts, with an audio page row, `Direction`, to turn it against them; and a tick is the same visible step through a slow or steady turn, a 16th of `=`'s on fine digits, so the change is in proportion to the swipe, rising in a straight line to one of `=`'s only on a fast swipe; a tick the other way too soon is a bounce and let go; and each frame makes what the ticks owe as one press** (#425, the author's report of 2026-11-11 that up and down felt janky, approved by feel on the author's MacBook trackpad after two rounds). **Why:** the author found sideways nice and up and down janky: it didn't move, then moved, stuttered, and sometimes went the wrong way. A log of the raw reports from the author's trackpad in kitty, 1,082 of them in 37 gestures, showed why. A slow turn up or down arrives as up and sideways ticks about half and half (`DLLDLDLL`), as kitty's columns are half as wide as its rows, so a finger's slight drift crosses a column before a row; the pairs always agree, the fingers going up and right or down and left. With sideways the large step, 151 of those drifting ticks moved the value 16 of `=`'s steps each, in the midst of fine ones, which was the jumping and the lost control; and a slow tick moved a 256th of a step, too little to see. Ticks of a careful turn come 4 to 10 a second, and a fast swipe's at the screen's 60 a second, now and then two at once; strokes end within about 200 ms, with no long momentum and no bounce back. Natural scrolling, on by macOS's default, sends the fingers going up as the terminal's down and right as left, so the value went against the fingers on both axes. **Prior art:** Ableton Live, where a knob takes the trackpad either way, and macOS's own pointer, whose acceleration leaves slow moves in proportion; level bars, which fill to the right and up. **One knob:** a tick up or right is `=`'s way and down or left `-`'s, the same size, so a diagonal swipe adds up; Shift, with either, is `+` or `_`, and a mouse's Shift-wheel, which macOS sends sideways, keeps its Shift. **Direction:** `wheel::natural_scrolling` asks `NSUserDefaults` for `com.apple.swipescrolldirection`, on unless it's been written off, through objc2-foundation, which vimtracker already uses, with its `NSString` and `NSUserDefaults` features; under natural scrolling both axes are flipped, so the value follows the fingers whichever way the system scrolls. `WheelSetting::reversed`, the audio page's `Direction` row under `Wheel`, turns it against them: `=`, `-`, `K`, `i` and Enter switch it, `x` puts it back, and it's kept in `wheel.ron`; the row is dim while the wheel is off. **The least step** is one a slow tick is seen to move: a 16th of `=`'s step on a value's fine digits, the first fine digit's step; a tenth of a BPM on the tempo; a 16th of a pixel on the sample page; and with the cursor on the fine part itself, its own `=`, a 256th or a hundredth. **Speed** is a count of ticks that decays with a 100 ms time constant, so it rises and falls smoothly, not in whole ticks: at or below 3, about 25 ticks a second, every tick is the least step, so twice the swipe is twice the change; from there it rises in a straight line to one of `=`'s steps at 8, about 75 a second, which a 60-a-second swipe reaches about 70% of. Each tick's size is owed in the finest step, a frame makes a whole number of least steps and keeps the rest, so a speed between two sizes comes out between them. This replaces decision 162's square of the ticks in the last 100 ms, whose jumps between rates were the stutter. **Bounces:** a tick the other way within 100 ms of the turn's last is let go, unless it's the third in a row, a quick and deliberate turn; after a pause it turns at once, from a standstill. The log showed no bounces on this trackpad, but the filter costs nothing. **Pacing:** ticks are owed, and `App::wheel_frame`, each frame, makes what they owe as one press with a count, so the value moves once a frame, not in a jump a tick; a key makes what's owed before it acts, and the gesture still ends after 300 ms still. **Not done:** sideways ticks aren't weighted for kitty's narrower columns, so a sideways swipe moves about twice as far as the same swipe up; the author found it fine. **Tuning:** every constant is in `wheel::feel`. **Tests** (`tests/wheel.rs`): the curve pinned, the least at or below slow, the most at or above fast and even rises between; up or right raising and down or left lowering, Shift either way the large step; natural scrolling flipping both axes, `Direction` flipping them back, kept and put back with `x`; a sideways drift joining an up turn with no large step; a bounce let go, a turn after a pause or on its third tick taken; nothing made before the frame, one change at it, and a key making what's owed before it moves; and decision 162's tests moved to the new least steps. Added on 2026-11-11.

164. **The Roto-Control's MIX mode is vimtracker's mixer, step 3 of M30 ([`roto-control.md`](roto-control.md) §4.4), following the vendor's manual exactly: Level on the knobs and mutes on the buttons, SEL held for Pan, Send A or B and solos, FUNC for one track's Level, Pan and sends, the arrows paging banks of eight, both together the returns and the master, and meters from the engine's levels; its knobs edit the song as keys do, its Level knob's notch is 0 dB, its transport button's PLAY, STOP and RECORD are vimtracker's in either mode, and only one vimtracker on a machine has the Roto** (#412). **Why:** step 3 of M30, the author's rule that the Roto works beside the screen, never through it, and decision 159's answer 4. **Prior art:** the Roto's manual, its Ableton script's `_update_mixer` and its Bitwig script's mix layers, followed as written: the lists are Live's tracks then its returns and master; a mute button lit while muted and an empty slot lit, as Ableton's lights them; track focus's knobs Level, Pan, then six sends and eight a page after; meters 12 times a second, the highest since the last; values shown 12 times a second; a fader's default on CC 64. **The lists:** the sound tracks then the four groups, named as FUNC's list names them and in their groups' colours, a group marked; the returns then the master; all 68 of a full song in `NUM TRACKS`, nothing left off (whether the firmware shows a ninth bank, past its manual's 64, wasn't reached on the hardware: the author paged to tracks 33 to 40).
