The groups' folding (`TOGGLE TRACK GROUPING`) is ignored: every group's tracks are listed, as there are at most 68. **The track touched** is the Roto's chosen track, which PLUGIN mode then shows, as Live's selected track is both modes'. **Edits:** a knob's move goes through `App::apply`, one undo step from touch to let-go (decision 160); mutes and solos are the keys' `Space m` and `Space s`, live and saved with the song, not undo steps; the master can't be muted, so its button is dark and does nothing; Arm stays dark. A meter is lit while its track is heard by every mute and solo (`Song::heard_by`, `Song::heard` generalised). **Level's notch:** the firmware's Level knob has a notch where Live has 0 dB, measured at 13004 of 16383 (a knob let go in it read 13004 every time; the API doesn't say), where vimtracker's plain scale read -6.8 dB; so MIX mode's Level has its own curve both ways, `00` at the bottom, `E0` (0 dB) in the notch, `F8` (+6 dB) at the top, even in dB either side. Pan's centre is the knob's middle and the sends have no notch. **Meters:** each shown track's falling peak on the app's meter scale, 0 to 127, thresholds at -6 dB (95) and 0 dB (111), where the app's own meters turn yellow and peach. **Sends:** `NUM SEND TRACKS` 2 and the returns' names, sent as the Roto connects, as Ableton's script does, and with every view; SEL's SEND button is a list, each press stepping to the next send (the author's check found Send B there once it was pressed again). Track focus's page comes in multiples of 8, as the SysEx API has it. **On connecting** vimtracker sends `REFRESH MIX MODE`, which a Roto already in MIX mode answers by saying so, so it's shown the mixer at once. **Transport:** PLAY plays from where it stopped, STOP pauses in place and, pressed again, goes back to the start, as Live's does, RECORD arms recording as the keys do; from the Roto's `REQUEST TRANSPORT STATUS` until it next says its mode, PLAY's and RECORD's lights follow the app; the other five and the arrows are left for #415. **One vimtracker:** two copies on a machine both answered the Roto on the hardware check, so the edits seemed not to reach playback: they reached the copy not being heard. Now the one holding `roto.lock` in the cache folder locked has it (`File::try_lock`, which the system lets go of however the program ends); a second turned on leaves the Roto alone, says so on its status line and its Roto row ("On, but another vimtracker has it: turn it off there"), and looks every second, taking it once the first lets go. **Checked on the author's Roto** (firmware 3.3.0, 2026-11-11, live MIX messages only, nothing written; the 5 Ableton and 9 `vt ` setups the same before and after, read with the read-only `roto-probe`): names, colours, levels and their values, pans, both sends, mutes, solos, banks, track focus with Send B, the returns and the master, the meters, the motors following keyboard level changes, Level's notch at +0.0 dB, and the transport playing, stopping and lit. **Tests:** in `vt-roto`, track focus's layout and pages in eights with their sends' names, the banks and the returns and master, only what changed sent and a touched knob left alone, meters only as they change; against the fake Roto, the first view's names, positions, sends, lights and meter states, a Level knob editing its own track whatever the cursor is on with one undo step a touch and no echo, a mute lit and the keys' mute shown, SEL's Pan, Send B and solos, track focus and PLUGIN mode following its track, banks to the groups and the returns and master with the master unmutable, all 68 tracks counted, keyboard edits and undo moving the knobs but never a touched one, meters from the engine's levels, a fader's default, the transport playing and pausing with its light, the engine's output silenced by the Roto's Level and mute and back, Level's notch at 0 dB both ways, and a second vimtracker leaving the Roto to the first and taking it once it's let go. Added on 2026-11-11.

165. **A track's three envelopes and three LFOs become six typed mod slots, `Track::mods: [ModSlot; 6]`, as the M8's 3.0 made its modulators: each slot has a type (`ADSR` or `LFO` so far), a destination, a depth, a switch and a polarity, which every type shares, and keeps each type's own settings, so trying another type and going back finds the first as it was; the slots work out in order, each reaching only those after it; the defaults are today's three envelopes and three LFOs, and every song with them renders bit for bit as before; and the song format goes to 9** (#427 and #434, step 2 of M20, the mod slots study [`mod-slots.md`](mod-slots.md) and its Decided note, #422 and the author's four answers of 2026-11-11). **Why:** the fixed split of decision 138 can't give a track a fourth envelope or a pitch envelope beside a filter one, and M20's types (AHD, Drum, LPG, Steps, Track) each need a place; the M8, Bitwig and Vital all let any slot take any type. **Prior art:** the M8's slots and one amount each (manual v6.5.2); Elektron's LFO modes (Digitakt II §11.9), of which `Free` and `Trig` replace `Retrig` now and #86 adds `Hold`, `One` and `Half`; tracker-ce's fields read again under a new type, as the warning that made each type keep its own. **The model** (`vt-model`'s `modulation.rs`): `ModSlot { kind, destination, depth, bypassed, adsr: Adsr, lfo: Lfo }`, `Copy`, a type's settings saved only where they differ from its defaults; `Adsr` is `envelope.rs`'s `Envelope` without the slot's parts, and `Lfo` is `lfo.rs`'s with `mode: LfoMode` for `retrig`; `default_mods()` gives slots 1–3 today's `note_shape`, `decay` on `Cut` and `decay` on nothing, and slots 4–6 today's LFO; `SetMod` replaces `SetLfo` and `SetEnvelope`, so every edit, `b` and `K` is one undo step. **The note's shape** is the first envelope-type slot on `Vol`, whichever slot that is (answer 2), switched in or not, as one switched out leaves the plain shape rather than handing the job on (`modulation::note_shape_slot`, `Engine::note_shape`); its depth shows `--` and its `DEP` lock does nothing, so `DEP` *n* isn't on the track there. **The engine** (`track_state.rs`): one ordered loop over a fixed `slots: [SlotRun; 6]`, which replaces the envelope runs, LFO starts, held values, shares and wave places; each slot reads its depth and phase as held, plus what earlier slots added to them, and adds to its destination through the fast smoothers for an envelope and the slow ones for an LFO, as before; every slot's envelope runs and cycles count whatever its type, so a slot changing type picks the new one up as though it had always been it, and the difference between what the old type last added and what the new one first adds is kept in `glide` and faded out over `BYPASS_FADE_MS`, 5 ms, as the switch is; a destination that's another slot before this one is skipped. An envelope's depth is read as a whole number, as its page sets it, so default songs keep their exact sums. Nothing allocates: `link.rs`'s `changing_a_slots_type_every_block_never_allocates_in_the_callback` publishes 400 versions flipping two slots' types on four tracks with none. **Same results:** slots 1–3 run before 4–6 exactly as envelopes ran before LFOs, and a slot's random waves are keyed by `(slot + 3) % 6`, so slots 4–6 keep LFOs 1–3's keys and even `Random` and `Smooth` render as before: every golden render is unchanged, and the author's `confusion-pump-panel` and `daft-punk-03`, converted by a one-off script into `.v9` copies beside the originals, render bit for bit as the format-8 build renders the originals. **Labels and keys:** `Owner::Lfo` becomes `Owner::Mod` over six, keys `mod1.depth` to `mod6.phase`, labels `DEP1`–`DEP6` and `PHS1`–`PHS6`, names `M5 Depth`, groups `Mod 5`, `SettingId::modulator` and `SettingId::for_mod` for `lfo`, `for_lfo` and `for_envelope`; `PHS` is only on an LFO slot, and an envelope's depth is now lockable and reachable from an earlier slot; the label tests run over six, and no label clashes (only these end in a digit, and an FX slot's begin with one). **The Mod page:** six half-width devices, `Mod 1`–`Mod 3` down the left beside `Mod 4`–`Mod 6`, so the default page is today's picture; a title is `Mod n`, the switch, the type, the type's mode if it has one, and `→` the destination, which keeps "what it is" apart from "where it goes", in that order for `h` and `l`; `K` on the type lists the types, `=` and `-` there refuse, as only `K` chooses what a device is, and `x` puts back the slot's first type; the LFO's grid is `Shape`, `Rate` and `Depth` down the left and `Phase` with two holes on the right, `Fade`'s (#86) among them; panels are keyed `PanelKey::Mod(n)` and drawn by the slot's type, and `LiveValues::mods` has a wave's place for each LFO slot. **The Roto:** a slot's layout is its type's, `ADSR` (the approved envelope layout, stored as `adsr`) or `LFO`, and a slot's type is never on the Roto (decision 161's rule); devices are named `M1 ADSR` to `M6 LFO`; while `LfoMode` has two modes the LFO keeps its approved button under `Phase` for the mode, lit for `Trig`, which #86's five make a knob. **The song:** format 9 saves `mods` as six slots and no `lfos` or `envelopes`; a format-8 song is refused with decision 138's message, with no conversion in the program (the author's rule). **Polarity** (#434, the author's addition while this was open): `Uni` moves what a slot reaches one way only and `Bi` both ways around it, as Bitwig's and Vital's modulators have it; `ModSlot::polarity` is an `Option`, saved only once chosen, and until then a slot follows its type's (`ModKind::polarity`: an envelope `Uni`, an LFO `Bi`), so a type change keeps a chosen one and follows the type otherwise, and every song sounds as it did. `Polarity::of` maps a type's output, an LFO's -1 to 1 into 0 to 1 and an envelope's 0 to 1 into -1 to 1, and leaves it exactly as it was in the type's own, so the golden renders are untouched. It's in the title after the mode, as the ADSR's grid is full and a polarity is a choice made once, like a mode; `=` flips it, `K` lists both and `x` puts back the type's. It's a third registry entry, `polarity` (`POL1`–`POL6`), which steps lock and nothing modulates (`for_mod` lists only depths and phases); the engine holds it with the depth and phase (`SlotRun::values`). The pictures' dotted line is a baseline for `Uni` and a centre line for `Bi`, at the wave's rest for a flipped LFO; on the Roto it's a button under `Depth` in the ADSR's and the LFO's stored setups, lit for `Bi`. **Cost:** a `Track` grows from 344 to 432 bytes, so an undo version of a 15-track song copies 9.7 KB rather than 7.7, 1.9 MB more over the 1,000 kept; `vimtracker bench --tracks` on `confusion-pump-panel` with a filter envelope and an LFO on every track, best of five in a release build, plays at 3.81% of a core against main's 3.77%, about 4 µs a 512-frame block. Added on 2026-11-11.

166. **`VDLT` moves each note's velocity up or down from its own as it starts, `LDLT`'s twin, and the track's `Velocity` on the Play device is where it starts, so the Roto has a knob for it** (#436, M19, from the sequencing research). **Prior art:** the Cirklon's velocity accumulator and `Rndmz velo` (manual pp. 13-2, 13-14, 13-24) and its knob grab `knob n to velo` (p. 13-26), and the Hapax's velocity lane (p. 62). Velocity was stored per note and nothing moved it while the song played. **The setting:** `velocitydelta` in files, `VDLT` on a step, named "Velocity up or down" and listed under Machine after `PTCH`, with "Velocity, up or down from the note's own" as its meaning. It's centred on `80`, which leaves a note alone, and a step of it is a step of velocity, as `LDLT`'s is of `Gate`: `90` adds `10`, `70` takes `10` off, and `00` and `FF` take any note to `00` or `7F`, the range it's kept to; the status line shows it as `+10 vel`. It's read once as each note starts, after the step's FX have run, so a lock on the note's own row counts, a lane's value counts, and a lock on a later row doesn't change a sounding note. The groove's velocity then scales what it gives, as it scaled the note's own. The stored velocity stays the note's, and the track's level stays out of patterns, so it keeps to the velocity-vs-mixer rule. As a registry setting of the track's it gets locks, holds, glides, lanes, `K`, the step text and the `S` panel's FX shapes with nothing new: a column of `VDLT`s in a lane is an accent pattern that drifts against the notes, and `VDLT 40g` to `VDLT C0` many rows on gives each note on the way its own share, a crescendo. **Modulation:** unlike `LDLT`, which is left out of a mod slot's `Dest` as moving it would only move `Gate`, `VDLT` is offered, since velocity isn't a setting anything else can move: an LFO on it swells the notes, and what a slot adds is read with the held value as the note starts. **The track's `Velocity`:** the issue asks for a live dynamics knob on the Roto, and a knob needs something on the track to turn, so `VDLT` has a base where `LDLT` has none: `Track::velocity_delta`, `80` unless changed and saved only then, so no format change. It's `TrackControl::VelocityDelta`, under `Choke` in the Sound page's Play device, in the column of what the track does to its notes as they start, drawn as `Pitch` is with a bar either side of the middle, and knob 8 of the Roto's `play` page, which was free, coloured as a level. **`Acid`:** it reads velocity only as the accent at `70` (decision 121), so `VDLT` there only switches accents, which the guides say. **Left out:** the issue's Contrast, a `Dynamics` setting that scales velocities around a centre as the Cirklon's `velo%` does, until the offset proves too blunt; the `S` panel's `Scale` covers the one-shot version. **Alternatives:** `VDLT` with no base, the exact twin of `LDLT`, was rejected as the Roto would have had nothing to turn. A delta scaled to half a step, so `00` and `FF` were `-40` and `+3F`, was rejected because a lock of `90` adding `10` reads straight off the hex. Added on 2026-11-11.

167. **`CND` builds: `>n` plays its row from pass `n` on and `<n` only before it, from the pass count decision 84 already keeps, as `9n` and `An` for passes 2 to 15 and `Bn` and `Cn` for 16 to 31** (#441, M19, from the sequencing research). **Prior art:** the Cirklon's accumulator mask events, `Mask gate,dAcc>n` and `n>dAcc`, and `mute trk, dAcc>n` (manual p. 13-25); Elektron's `A:B` cycles but never builds. **What:** two more kinds of condition, `Condition::From(n)` and `Condition::Until(n)`, labelled `>n` and `<n` as the Cirklon writes them, meaning "From pass n on" and "Before pass n only", in the `K` list's groups **From** and **Until** after **Passes**. A pass counts from 1 here, as `1:2`'s does, so `>2` is `!1ST` and `<2` is `1ST`, which this generalises; `>1` and `<1`, always and never, aren't conditions. There's no counter of their own: they read the same pass as `1:2`, worked out from the count, so they start again with the chain, a section's reset, `:reset`, the track's `Reset` and a realign, a section with `Rpt 8` builds across its repeats unless its Start is `reset`, playing from a step hears the build as playing to it would, a `CND` in a lane counts the lane's loops, and `PRE` and `NEI` chain other rows to a built one. **The encoding:** the second digit is the pass, so the hex reads as the label: `95` is `>5` and `A3` is `<3`, from 2 to `F`. **The range:** 15 passes falls one short of a 16-bar section of a one-bar phrase, and a build across 32 bars needs twice that, so `Bn` and `Cn` carry `>` and `<` on from 16 to 31 (`B4` is `>20`), taking the next two unused rows rather than making `9x` and `Ax` count from 1 or 2 and losing the readable digit; `D0` to `FF` stay free. `CND`'s highest value is now `CF`. **Moving:** `=` and `-` go through the list, past `8:8` into `>2` and from `>31` into `<2`, and `+` and `_` move a build's pass by four, as a large step moves a cycle's length. **Alternatives:** a hidden counter per row, the Cirklon's `dAcc`, was rejected as the issue asks, as the pass count already starts where a build should. Added on 2026-11-11.

168. **A fourth counted `Where`, `Euclid`, spreads `Hits` by Bjorklund's algorithm over the rows the column could change, and `Rotate` moves every hit that many of those rows later, going round; on Velocity that's among the notes** (#440, M19's sequencer logic, built to the issue's proposal). **Prior art:** Toussaint's *The Euclidean Algorithm Generates Traditional Musical Rhythms*, whose patterns the tests check: E(3,8) `x..x..x.`, E(5,8) `x.xx.xx.`, E(5,16) `x..x..x..x..x...`, E(7,16) `x..x.x.x..x.x.x.`. tracker-ce's Fill has a `Euclidean` step choice (`Sequencer::isEuclideanStep`) that rounds each row to the nearest of `length / density` evenly spaced points, which isn't Bjorklund's and spaces some counts differently. Bresenham's line gives a rotation of Bjorklund's (5 in 8 as `x.x.xx.x`), so Bjorklund's was chosen for the published patterns at a rotation of 0. **Counting:** the issue has it count the rows it can change, `Alternate`'s and `List`'s rule (decision 96), so the pattern's length is the number of rows the column could change in the selection (`shape::open_rows`): the notes on Velocity, and on an FX column every row but those holding another command. A selection is one pattern; there's no length of its own to repeat, as the issue gives only `Hits` and `Rotate`. **The panel:** `Euclid` is last in `Where`'s turn and list on both kinds of column, starting at 3 hits and no rotation (`EUCLID_HITS`), so 8 notes give the tresillo straight away. `Hits` and `Rotate` take `Amount`'s place below `Where`. `Hits` shows the count it spreads over, as `5 of 16`, runs from 0 to that count and stops there, as `Every` stops at 256; `Rotate` goes round within the count, as `Phase` goes round, and typing a larger number takes it round too. Large steps move either by 4, `x` puts back 3 hits or no rotation. **Direction:** a positive rotation moves the hits later, as a positive `Nudge` moves a note later, so `Rotate 1` on 3 in 8 gives `.x..x..x`. Added on 2026-11-11.

169. **Four transforms, `Scale`, `Invert`, `Shuffle` and `Reverse`, are the last four `Fill`s, reworking the values already on the rows `Where` chooses rather than making new ones; a chosen row with no value of the column's is left as it is** (#437, M19's sequencer logic, built to the issue's proposal; the author chose fills over separate commands). **Prior art:** the Hapax's centroid, minimum and maximum of selected automation points (p. 64), its `Shaper`'s `EXP` and `SIGMO` (p. 107), its `Symmetry` algo (p. 115) and `Shuffle` in `STEP` mode (pp. 113-114); tracker-ce's reverse, which turns whole steps round and stays a plain edit here. Ableton Live 12's MIDI transformations work on clips' notes, and wasn't checked in the program for this. **The rules:** `Scale` takes each value's place between the selection's own lowest and highest, from 0 to 1, through `curved`, as a ramp's row position goes, and lands it between `From` and `To` with the ramp's rounding (`along_line`, which `Ramp` now shares), so `20 40 60` onto `00`-`FF` gives `00 80 FF`; values all the same have no range and go to `From`. `Invert` gives each value the lowest plus the highest less itself, so `10 30 70` gives `70 50 10`. `Shuffle` is Fisher and Yates from the shape's seed, so `r` deals again and `.` deals each selection anew, as it rerolls a random fill. `Reverse` gives the rows their values in the opposite order. All four read the value with its fine part (`ShapeColumn::exact`), so a setting's fine part moves or flips with it; `Scale` makes a new fine part as a ramp does. **The panel:** they come after `List` in `Fill`'s turn and list, described `The values there, ...`; `Scale` shows `From`, `To`, `Curve` and `S`, and the other three only `Fill` and `Where`, with `From the values there` (or `r deals again` on `Shuffle`) where the hint goes. `.` keeps `Scale`'s ends, as it keeps a wave's. Nothing new on screen but the names, as the issue asks, and no second menu or keys. Added on 2026-11-11.

170. **`Run` holds a `Ramp`, `Random` or `Wave` fill's value for that many of the rows it changes, `Smooth` joins the wave shapes, and a wave's `Rate` can be a whole number of cycles across the selection, typed and shown as `x3`** (#439, M19's sequencer logic, built to the issue's proposal). **Prior art:** the Hapax's `Curves` with `STEPS`, `REPEAT` and `PERLIN` (pp. 112-113), and automation's maximum rate as a sample-and-hold (p. 69); the LFO's own `Smooth` (`lfo::wave`), which a `Wave` fill now calls as it calls the others. **Run:** it counts the rows changed, as `Alternate`'s and `List`'s `Run` does (decision 96), so it's the same row with the same meaning on every fill that has it, and a run is kept as `Fill` moves through them. Each row takes the value of the first row of its run: a wave is sampled there, a random fill picks there. A ramp's ends are the first rows of the first and last runs, so the stairs are even and the last reaches `To` however short it is: `00` to `30` over 8 rows with `Run 2` gives `00 00 10 10 20 20 30 30`, and over 7 rows with `Run 3`, `00 00 00 18 18 18 30`. With `Run 1` every fill is as it was. `Scale` has no `Run`, as a transform keeps each row's own value. **Smooth:** last in `Shape`'s turn and list, seeded by the shape's seed, so `r` picks another drift, and the hint says so. `Random` as a wave shape was left out: `Random` with a `Run` is the same sample-and-hold, counted in rows rather than time. **Cycles:** `Shape::rate` becomes a `WaveRate`, a time (`lfo::Rate`, still never in hertz) or `Cycles(n)` from 1 to 64, so the LFO's `Rate` and the song format are untouched. A cycle is the whole selection, first row to last plus one, rather than the first row filled to the last, so the row after the selection starts the next cycle and a looped phrase joins up; the rows it fills fall where they are along it, as with a time. `i` takes `x3` (or `X3`), `K` lists `x1` to `x8` after the times, `=` and `-` add or take a cycle, `+` and `_` four, and `x` puts back `1/2`. Shapes aren't saved, so the song format doesn't change. Added on 2026-11-11.

171. **`S` works on the Note column, re-pitching the notes already there in the scale's degrees while it's on and in semitones while it's off, with `Snap` as a transform that moves each note to the nearest degree, and on the Nudge and gate length columns too** (#438, M19's sequencer logic, built to the issue's proposal). **Prior art:** tracker-ce's Fill on the note column, with its scale filter, and on its micro-timing column; the Cirklon's slope edit on note, length and delay (p. 3-8). **Notes:** `ShapeColumn::Note` carries the scale the status line shows (`App::scale`, which is entry's and not the song's undoable state) as it was when the panel opened, and `.` takes it as it is then. Its rows are the notes, as Velocity's are: `Where` chooses among them, so the rhythm, velocities, gates, `OFF`s and rests stay as they are; writing notes into empty rows, the open question, is left until re-pitching has been used. A ramp, random fill, wave, `Scale` and `Invert` work along a `Ladder`, the notes in range in the scale (or all of them with it off), so a step is a degree, every random pick and wave point is in key as it's made, and an end outside the scale counts from its nearest degree, the lower if two are as near (`Scale::fitted`). A note outside the scale falls between two steps of the ladder, so `Reverse` and `Shuffle`, which move notes about, and `Invert`, which mirrors them, keep a chromatic note chromatic and find it again; `Invert` in degrees is the melodic inversion the transforms issue (#437) asks for. `Alternate` and `List` write their notes as they're given, so a motif can hold a passing note. `List`'s values are notes, typed by name with spaces between, like `c3 d#3 g3`, and the panel grows from 38 to 42 columns so eight show. From and To are typed as `C-3`, `c3`, `c-3` or `c#3`; `=` and `-` move one a degree with the scale on (`Scale::moved`) or a semitone, `+` and `_` an octave (as many degrees as the scale has, or 12). When the selection holds one note, the ends start an octave apart from it. **Snap:** a tenth `Fill`, offered only on the Note column (`ShapeColumn::fills`), with no settings but `Where`; ties go to the lower note, as `Scale::fitted` has it, so `C#3` in C major goes to `C-3`. With the scale off it changes nothing, and its hint says so. Playback still never forces notes into the scale (decision 24): this is the explicit after-the-fact quantise, as `:scale fit` is for a whole phrase, here for a selection and the rows `Where` chooses. **Out of scale:** From, To and a list's notes outside the scale are drawn in `theme::out_of_scale`, the grid's colour for one. **Nudge:** held in a shape as 0 to 22, `-B` to `+B` with 11 for none, so it's a plain range for every fill; shown and typed with its sign as the grid shows it (`-3`, `0`, `+B`), and moved by one or four. **Length:** the gate cell's length, `00` to `FF`; writing one to a row with no gate makes a `SGL1` gate with it. A row with no length of its own, `--`, has no value, so the transforms leave it, as they leave an FX row without the command, and a fill gives it one. Both narrow among the notes, as Velocity does, and `S` on the gate's type and pulses is refused with a message. Added on 2026-11-11.

172. **`Space g m` and `Space g s` mute and solo the cursor's track on the launch grid while playing: armed, the switch waits for the next line of the grid `Space A` waits for, and every switch waiting for that line lands together, on the same sample, before the line's notes** (#442, M19's sequencer logic). **Prior art:** the Cirklon's `SHIFT` and a track button arms that track's mute or unmute for the next global bar, beside the plain button, which switches at once; the Hapax switches its mute groups at the bar; Ableton waits for its global quantisation for clip launches but switches a track's activator at once. So the instruments built for jamming keep both at hand, the quantised one a modifier away, rather than a mode that changes what the plain key does. **A modifier, not a setting:** a drop on the bar and a kill right now are both wanted in the same jam, so a setting would mean going to the project page between them, and it would take the plain keys' hold, a mute until it's let go, away whenever it's on, as a switch that lands later can't be held. **The key:** `Space g m` and `Space g s`, `g` for the grid, after Vim's own `g`, which gives a variant of the key after it, as `g p` is a paste that lands after itself and `g v` a selection again; here it's the mute or solo that lands on the grid. The capitals were the other thought, but the author's rule is that a capital is the wider version of its lowercase key, as `Space M` clears every mute and `Space A` realigns every track, and a mute that waits isn't wider, and `Space M` is taken. They're in every view, with the other live controls, and `Space k` lists them after `Space m` and `Space s`. **When it lands:** on the next line of `:launch`'s grid, counted from the start of the section's repeat, a bar unless changed, or the next bar with `:launch chain`, as realigns do (decision 92), so one setting says when every played change lands. It lands in `Engine::fire_step` where a realign does, after a section's start and resets and before the line's notes, so the note on the line is the first heard or the first left out, and every waiting switch of every track lands in one pass, with the mixer's audibility worked out once after, so they're heard from the same sample, which the engine's tests check against switching them by hand there. A groove that works steps out early has the line's switch with it, as its notes are. **Take-back:** pressing it again before the line takes it back, as `Space A` does, and the engine decides from its own state, as it does a realign, so a press right on the line is never lost between the two threads. `Space m`, `Space s` and `Space M` switch at once and take back any waiting switch of theirs, and stopping, pausing or playing from somewhere forgets them. **Stopped**, there's no grid coming round, so it switches at once, as `Space m` would, and says so, rather than refusing as a realign does, as a mute set while stopped is still what's wanted at the start. **The live controls follow** the engine: each track's `JamState` carries its mute and solo, whether each waits, and a count of switches landed, which the app compares with the last it saw, and when it moves, the track's live mute and solo take the engine's, marking the song changed, as a mute is saved with it; a count rather than the flags going off, as an arm and its landing can come in the same block, and a count can't be confused with the app's own switches. **Shown:** the status line says `Track 01 mutes on the 1bar grid; Space g m again takes it back`, or `unmutes`, `solos` and `unsolos`, then `Track 01's mute switch taken back`; while any waits the transport shows `󰔟 MUTE` or `󰔟 SOLO`, as it shows `󰔟 REALIGN`, and the track shows the waiting glyph, dim, in place of its mute or solo glyph, in the phrase view's overview and on the mixer's `Mute` or `Solo` row, so it's clear which of several is armed. **Not here:** mute groups switched together by one key, as the Hapax's, which a group track's own mute already gives for its tracks; arming from the Roto's buttons, which still switch at once; and a hold that arms a switch and takes it back when let go, which would land after the key is up. Added on 2026-11-11.

173. **`:loop` keeps a range of sections playing in turn: `V` in the song view selects sections and `:loop` loops them, first to last and round again, each from its start; while playing a section outside it, a new loop waits for the next line of the launch grid, where its first section starts; `:loop` alone on a looping section goes back to the song at once** (#444, M19's sequencer logic). **Prior art:** the Cirklon's scene loops, a range of scenes played round while jamming, set by holding two scene buttons, which also cues the loop's start; Ableton launches a scene on its global quantisation, which is when a jump to the loop lands here. **Selecting:** `V` in the song view starts a selection of whole sections, as `V` selects whole steps in a phrase, and `j`, `k` and the other moves extend it; `V` again, `Esc`, leaving the view or running a command ends it, as Vim's Visual ends with `:'<,'>`. It's drawn as a selection over the section rows, with `VISUAL` as the mode, and lives in the app, not the song, so it's no undo step. Only `:loop` reads it for now; deleting or copying sections over it is left for when it's asked for. `:loop` was chosen over a key of its own, as the issue allowed, as it's what loops one section already, so the range is the same command over a selection. **Looping:** the engine keeps a `SectionLoop`, a first and a last section, in place of the one section it kept; one section is a loop of one, which plays exactly as before. When a section in the loop ends, the next of the loop's sections with a length plays, and after the last the first, going back round as the song does at its end, so every chain starts from its top and `2, 3, 2, 3` sounds like the song's `2, 3` each time; a loop of one goes on leaving its chains running, as before. A section outside the loop ending moves into the loop's first. **When it starts:** while playing a section that isn't one of the loop's, the loop waits for the next line of the launch grid, `Launch` (a bar, or the next bar with `:launch chain`), as realigns and grid mutes do (decisions 92 and 172), and a section ending is a line too; there the loop's first section starts, running its `Start` and ending launches, as a section the song moves into does. While playing one of the loop's sections, it's the loop at once, as it only decides what comes after that section, whose end is a line anyway, so waiting would change nothing but show a wait. So changing the range while it plays takes effect at the next line. This applies to one section too, where `:loop` on another section used to wait for the one playing to end; on a bar it's quicker and what a jam wants, and with the section playing it's unchanged. Stopped or paused there's no line to wait for, so it's the loop at once, and stopping or pausing makes a waiting loop the loop. The first launch's hold on the playing section and `C`'s capture set a loop of one at once, as before. **Back to the song:** `:loop` with no selection on a section of the loop goes back to the song at once, as it did for one: the section playing plays to its end and the song moves on from it; on a section outside the loop it loops that section. **Shown:** a bar, `│`, after the section numbers of the sections looping, in the active colour, dim while the loop waits; the status line's `Sec 2/4 loop` names a range, `loop 2-3`; the transport shows `󰔟 LOOP` while the loop waits, from the engine's report, as `󰔟 REALIGN`; and `:loop` says `Looping sections 02-03 on the 1bar grid` while playing. **Not here:** a loop that follows the song's edits as sections are added or removed above it, which a single `:loop` didn't either; a loop saved with the song; and a key to loop without the command line. Added on 2026-11-11.

174. **A phrase automates the mixer as an Ableton clip does: `LEV` sets the fader, the track's `Level`, from a step with lock, hold and glide, as `PAN`, `SNA` and `SNB` set the others; every fader, on the mixer, the Sound page's Output device and the Roto, shows where the steps have it while they drive it, with modulation a thin mark beside it rather than a move of it; and a hand moving a fader the steps drive takes that fader of that track over until `Space e` gives the track's back, or `Space E` every track's** (#430, the author's request that phrases may automate the mixer, rescoped after an audit of the lock model, [`mixer-automation.md`](mixer-automation.md)). **Why:** marks, absolute `PAN` and sends, and relative `VOL` and modulation were there; only the fader itself couldn't be set from a step, and nothing on screen or on the Roto followed what the steps did. **Prior art:** Live's Session clip envelopes, which set a mixer control absolutely or, in modulation mode, move it relatively; its automated controls, which move with their automation; its override when a control is changed during playback, and the Re-Enable Automation button that gives every control back at once; and its clip modulation, which leaves the fader where it is and shows a thin indicator. **`LEV`:** a track setting, key `level`, in `Output`, on the level scale, whose base is the track's `level` and which every track has, the master too; the engine's level stage, after the slots and scaling the sends, is now `LEV`'s, so it follows the song as every setting does, rather than apart. A mod slot's `Dest` doesn't list it, as `Vol` is where modulation moves the volume. **When a phrase stops:** keeping the last value is the hold mark (the author, 2026-11-11), with no new rule: the transport stopping, the chain moving on to a phrase with nothing for the fader and the track being muted all leave the steps' values where they are, as a muted track's steps run on underneath; only playing from the top puts a fader back. **Shown:** "the steps drive it" is a step having set it since playing last started from the top. Then the mixer's strip shows the step's value in the playing colour and its fader column moves with it, the Output device's value and bar are the step's, the status line reads `-24.0 dB, from the steps`, and the Roto's knobs in MIX mode, and on the Output device in PLUGIN mode, show it and move their motors, sent at most 12 times a second, as the meters are, and never under a touched knob. The engine reports every track's faders each block (`Faders`: where each is, what `VOL` and modulation make of it, and which the steps drive and a hand has taken). **Modulation is a mark, not the fader:** what `VOL` and the mod slots make of a fader is a one-row line in the playing colour across the mixer's fader, and on the Output device the bars are filled to the fader with the thin mark at what's heard (the other devices' bars keep theirs, filled to what's heard with the mark where it's set, as they aren't faders). A fader is what a hand moves and a touch takes over: moved by an LFO, a motor would chase it under a finger, the digits would flicker unread, and a touch couldn't take modulation over, as it's on top of the fader, not the fader; and the meter already shows what's heard. **Touch override:** an edit by the keys, the wheel, `i`, `x`, `:tvol` or a Roto knob that moves a fader the steps drive sends `TakeOver` for it, from `App::apply`; the engine then hears the track page's value for that fader of that track and keeps the steps' values underneath, unheard, and the keys and the wheel move it from where it was heard, so it doesn't jump. A fader no step has set is only edited. The engine keeps each fader's track page value and the steps' last hold of it apart from its layers, so the takeover hears the hand's value and giving back hears the hold whichever of the command and the new version arrives first; a change of the track page's value with no takeover, as an undo, replaces a hold as it always has, until the next step sets it. A taken fader has `󰝁` (`nf-md-gesture_tap`, U+F0741) in peach, as a lit Re-Enable button, after its value on the mixer and after its name on the Sound page, and the status line says `taken over from the steps: Space e gives it back`. **The keys:** `Space e` gives the cursor's track's faders back and `Space E` every track's, as `Space m` and `Space M`, and `Space a` and `Space A` (decision 172), are the cursor's track's and every track's: `Space` leads the playing keys, the capital is wider, and `e` is Live's re-Enable. Given back, a fader is heard at once where the steps have it now. **Not an edit:** a takeover is live, as a mute is, kept in the engine: not an undo step, so `u` doesn't give it back, not saved, and given back when a song is opened; playing from the top leaves it, as in Live. **Relative modulation needs no code:** the FX guide's new section puts fader automation (`LEV`, `PAN`, `SNA`, `SNB`) beside relative modulation (`VOL` and the mod slots). **Saved:** only `LEV` locks, in the phrases. **Tests:** a `LEV` lock sets the level heard and a note puts it back, still driven until the top; a hold lasts through a note and a change of the set value, and playing from the top puts it back; `VOL` moves what's heard around the fader and not the fader; a takeover hears the hand's value and not the steps', leaves the other faders following, and giving back hears the hold underneath, whichever of the takeover and the hand's edit came first, or after a lock the hand's value, with the steps driving it again; `LEV` is lockable on every track and no mod slot lists it; and in the app, a held `LEV` and `PAN` show on the mixer and the Output device and not in the song, the status line says `from the steps`, `=` on the mixer goes a step up from where it's heard and takes it over, marked and said, the pan and the other track still following and `u` leaving it taken, `Space e` gives back the cursor's track's only and `Space E` every track's, and a send no step has set is only edited. Added on 2026-11-11.

175. **A value on a fixed scale with natural steps is two hex digits, and a continuous position four: everything on the level scale, `Level` and `Trim` as before, `Send A` and `Send B`, the `VOL` stage, and the `LEV`, `VOL`, `SNA` and `SNB` locks, an effect's level-scale `Gain` too, is two digits with no fine part, while `Pan`, `Cut`, `Res`, `PTCH` and the voices' settings keep four; a level-scale glide still sweeps continuously** (#430, the author's addition of 2026-11-11: "we want things as consistent as possible"). **Why:** `Level` and `Trim` were two digits and the sends and level locks four, for the same scale, whose step, a quarter of a dB, is already finer than an ear tells apart, so the fine pair dialled in nothing anyone heard. A position like a cutoff or a pan has no natural step, and a fine part between two values can be heard. **Rule:** `Kind::has_fine` is true for a continuous setting whose units aren't the level scale's (`Units::Decibels`), and false for those and every list; `FxCommand::has_fine` follows it, and `FxCommand::glides` is any continuous setting, so a level-scale value still glides. **Dropped, not hidden:** the fine byte is gone from what's stored, edited and shown, not only from the screen: a step's `sna:2080` is refused, the fine digits are passed over and the shape panel's ramps fill whole steps, `Track::set_fine` keeps none for the sends, the Sound page shows the sends as `20`, and the Roto's sends and MIX mode's Level knob move in whole steps. **The song format** goes up to 10, with no migration: a song saved before keeps its sends' and level locks' fine parts, which a conversion drops. **Tests:** the level scale's settings have no fine part and glide, the positions have one, and a list has none; `sna:2080`, `vol:E001` and `level:8040h` are refused, `level:80g` reads and writes as it is, and `pan:8040` still reads; a phrase shows `SNA   20   h`; and a format 9 song is refused by name. Added on 2026-11-11.

176. **An envelope's curve is its mode, `Exp`, `Lin` or `Snap`, one curve for the whole envelope rather than one for each stage: `Exp` is the Peaks curves envelopes always had and the default, `Lin` is straight, and `Snap` keeps `Exp`'s attack and falls with Vital's `powerScale` at `p = -8`, about twice as fast at first and then a longer tail; it's in the slot's title after the type, the diagram draws the curve the engine plays, it isn't a lock, and on the Roto it's a knob of three after `Depth`** (#46 and its comment, step 3 of M20, the mod slots study [`mod-slots.md`](mod-slots.md) §2's curve table and §8). **Why:** #46 found the fixed curves sometimes wanted a straight release or a snappier decay; one curve per envelope, as #46 suggested trying first, needs no more cells on a grid that's full. **Prior art:** Vital's `powerScale`, `(e^(p t) - 1) / (e^p - 1)`, of which our Peaks fall, `(1 - e^-4t) / (1 - e^-4)`, is the case `p = -4` (#46's comment); Mutable Instruments' Peaks for the attack's `t^3.32`; Stages' per-segment `WarpPhase`, left aside as one curve a stage would add three cells. **The model:** `envelope::Curve` with `rise` and `fall`; `Adsr::curve`, saved with a serde default of `Exp`, so format-9 songs load and sound as they did and the format stays at 9; `Exp`'s fall is written exactly as before, so every golden render is unchanged (a test checks it to the bit). Halfway through a decay to nothing, `Lin` is 6 dB down, `Exp` 18.5 and `Snap` 35. **Why a mode:** it changes the envelope's character and is set once, and ADSR's six cells are full, which is the FX page's rule for an effect's `Type` (§2). So `ModKind::has_mode` is now true for ADSR, its title reads `ADSR  Exp  Uni  → Cut`, `k` from its right column reaches the curve as it reaches an LFO's mode, `=` and `-` go round the three, `K` lists them with what each does (`Mod 1 curve`), and `x` puts back `Exp`. The status line and the Roto call it `Curve` (`ModKind::mode_label`), an LFO's still `Mode`. **The picture:** `views::curve::level_at` follows the envelope's own curve, so the panel is sent again when the curve changes, and a test checks each stage drawn against `Adsr::held` and `released` at the same share of its time. **Not a lock:** the study keeps a type's own settings out of the registry (§6), and a curve set once has no reason to change from step to step. **The Roto:** the ADSR's stored setup gains `Curve` on knob 6, after `Depth`, a list of three steps named `Exp`, `Lin` and `Snap`, violet as the slot's other modulation settings; a musical list, it leaves the layout as it is, so it's the Roto's by decision 161's rule. The setup's version changes, so it's written again when the Roto next connects. Added on 2026-11-11.

177. **`AHD` is a third type of mod slot, between `ADSR` and `LFO` in the type list: an attack to full, a hold there, and a decay to nothing, with an ADSR's reset and curve; its `Hold` is a time from 0 to 2000 ms, a one-shot that plays through whatever the note's length, or `Note`, held while the note lasts and decaying from its end; its grid is `Attack`, `Hold` and `Decay` down the left and `Depth` and `Reset` down the right, with a hole; its picture has the hold as a stage of its own; it can shape the notes; and on the Roto it's a stored layout of its own, `Attack`, `Hold`, `Decay`, a gap, `Depth` and `Curve`, with an ADSR's buttons** (#85, step 3 of M20, the mod slots study [`mod-slots.md`](mod-slots.md) §2's AHD and §8's table). **Why:** a hold keeps a drum at full before it decays, which an ADSR can only fake with a long attack or a sustain cut by the gate; a type of its own needs no seventh cell on ADSR's full grid, which #85 worried about. **Prior art:** the M8's AHD, a one-shot in ticks; the Digitakt II's amp envelope, whose `HOLD` is a time or `NOTE` (manual §11.7), from which `Note` comes; Vital's DAHDSR, whose delay is what the grid's hole is left for. **The model** (`envelope.rs`): `Ahd { attack_ms, hold: Hold, decay_ms, reset, curve }`, `Hold` being `Note` or `Ms(u16)`, defaulting to 1 ms, 30 ms and 200 ms with reset on, as the study has it; `ModSlot::ahd` beside `adsr` and `lfo`, saved only where it differs from its defaults, so the format stays at 9; and `Envelope`, a small `Copy` enum of an `Adsr` or an `Ahd` with `held`, `released`, `finished_holding`, `finished_releasing`, `reset` and `one_shot`, which `ModSlot::envelope` gives by the slot's type (an LFO slot's being its ADSR, kept running as before). A hold of `Note` plays as an ADSR with no decay, a sustain of 100% and its decay for a release; a timed hold's level is its attack, full for the hold, then its decay, and its note ends when the decay does. **The engine:** `Voice::shape` and `NoteOn::shape` are an `Envelope`, so each note keeps the shape it started with as before; `Voice::release` leaves a one-shot held, while a synth's own gate still closes; `TrackState::release_mods` leaves a one-shot slot's envelope held too; and `modulate` works an AHD out through the envelopes' fast smoothers with a whole depth, as an ADSR. Every default song renders bit for bit as before, and nothing allocates: `link.rs`'s type-change test now flips between ADSR, AHD and LFO. **The page:** `Mod 2  AHD  Exp  Uni  → Cut`; `ModField::Hold` is new, and `Attack`, `Decay` and `Reset` are the slot's AHD's under that type; `Note` is below `0 ms`, so `-` from `0 ms` reaches it and `=` comes back, typing `note` sets it and a number a time, and `x` puts back 30 ms. The picture lays the hold out as an ADSR's stages are, its own time's width on the same log scale or a sustain's for `Note`, with a fainter line at either end, and a test checks each stage against `Ahd::held` and `released`. **The Roto:** the layout follows §8's table, stored as `vt AHD` (`vimtracker:ahd`) for its buttons, `Reset` under `Attack` and the polarity under `Depth` as an ADSR's, so the stored setups are now 10; its `Hold` turns smoothly from `Note` at the bottom through the times the keys step through, its value shown as it turns; and its type is never on the Roto. **Not here:** a `Delay` before the attack, for which the hole is left; and locks of its times, which the study leaves for later (§6). Added on 2026-11-11.

178. **A live generator is the shape panel kept live: a saved `Shape`, seed and all, and its target, the phrase, track, column, and rows or lane, reopened in the same panel, with no interface of its own; and of the three rules issue 18 left open, a hand edit to a column it reads but doesn't own rerenders it, on an FX column with a lane it owns the lane's steps, and across tracks it's one generator per track, each edited on its own** (#18, #446, the author's decisions of 2026-11-11, from the sequencing research of the same day into tracker-ce, the Cirklon and the Hapax). **Why one panel:** the `S` panel already is the one-shot generator the design described, its `Where` the mask and its `Fill` the value source, and a `Shape` holds every parameter as plain `Copy` data, so keeping it live needs storage and rules rather than a second interface, and whatever the panel learns (transforms, the Note, Nudge and length columns, `Run`, `Smooth` and cycles, Euclid) is live with it. Euclid's `Rotate` is the showcase: the knob turned after the part is written. **The design doc** is brought up to date: "One-shot generators with remembered settings" is no longer a rejected idea but what ships, with `.` repeating the last `Shape`, and live generators are its upgrade; the Generators section no longer says masks and value sources wait for a second generator; and its Live state rules gain the three below. **Rule 1, a column read but not owned:** a velocity generator choosing the notes reads the note column; a note added by hand there rerenders it, so the new note gets a generated velocity, as Elektron's Euclidean mode follows the rhythm, and the edit and the rerender are one undo step. The design's rule that an edit to an owned column applies the generator, rather than an edit layer (see Alternatives Considered), stands for the cells it owns. **Rule 2, lanes:** on an FX column with a lane, a live generator owns the lane's steps and fills the lane as its own loop, rather than the rows selected. M12's rule that editing commands work on rows, whichever loop a column plays, still holds for one-shot shaping. **Rule 3, across tracks:** `S` works across tracks (decision 179); kept live, that's one generator per track, each reopened, changed and applied on its own, not one linked generator writing to several, which answers the open question in After the milestones. **Conflicts found** in the design before building, and how they're settled: Live state said changing a shape "never depends on what is currently in the fields", which rule 1 bends for the columns it reads, so it now keeps the owned column's values from under it and reads the notes as they are; it said a generator "owns every column it touches", now split into the cells it owns and the notes it reads; "shortening it past an owned range applies it" dates from before a phrase kept every step, so a length change now leaves it live; and the Fixed Specifications' Shape rows (onset and S-shape on `h l` and `j k`, `r` swapping the ends, and `Enter` leaving the sweep live) were the panel as first planned, and now list its keys as built, with `Enter` for plain values and `L` to keep it live. `Space ga` is written `Space g a`, as the leader's `g` already prefixes `Space g m` and `Space g s`. Added on 2026-11-11.

179. **`S` on a selection across tracks shapes each track's column, with the panel's settings and a seed of its own for each, as one undo; a track whose rows have nothing to shape is passed over, and it's refused only when none has** (#18's third rule, decision 148). **Before,** `S` refused a selection of more than one track, though every other selection edit works across them (decision 148). **Each track** gets the rows the selection covers on its own phrase, counted from each phrase's loop start as on screen, as `d` and `=` do. The column is the same on each, and an FX column's command is the first on that track's own rows, so a `CUT` and a `RES` column can be ramped together. `From` and `To` start from the first track's values, and every track takes the same settings. **Seeds:** the first track keeps the panel's seed, so one track shapes exactly as before, and the others take `shape::track_seed`, the seed mixed with how far along the track is, rather than added to, as a seed one step on would only move a random fill along the rows; so `Random`, `Chance`, `Shuffle` and `Smooth` differ from track to track, and `r` picks again on all of them. **Euclid's** `Hits` go up to the most rows any track could change. **Shown:** the panel's heading says `Velocity on 2 tracks: 10 of 16 rows`. **`.`** repeats it on as many tracks from the cursor's, as the other selection edits do. **Tests:** a ramp and a random fill on two tracks of three, the third with no notes passed over, one undo, and `.`; each track's own FX command; a refusal when no track has notes. Added on 2026-11-11.

180. **A live generator is a `Generator` on its phrase, holding its column, its span (rows, or its FX column's lane), its `Shape` and the column's cells from under it; after every edit, `edit` lets each live generator of each phrase the edit changed follow it, so a hand edit to one of its cells applies it, a note coming, going or changing under one that chooses its rows by the notes rerenders it in the same edit, and nothing else does either; and it's saved with its phrase in a field of its own, read with a default, so the song format needs no new version for it** (#18, decision 178). **Prior art:** Elektron's Euclidean mode, which the design's ownership rule was modelled on; and the design's rejection of an edit layer, which this keeps: hand editing needs no syncing. **Why after every edit:** `edit` is the only path that changes a song, so following there covers Insert, paste, `r`, `=`, the wheel, the Roto and anything added later, with no edit path knowing about generators. Phrases the edit didn't touch are passed over by pointer, so it costs nothing elsewhere. **How it tells:** each generator keeps its rows as it last left them (not saved, as it's what's in the phrase) and compares them with the phrase after the edit. A changed cell of its own applies it, leaving every value as it is, unless its rows are the notes (Velocity, Nudge, a length) and the row's note came or went, when the cell the edit gave is taken as what's under it and it rerenders; a changed note with its own cells untouched rerenders it. A rerender restores what's under it on all its rows and shapes again, so rows Euclid's `Rotate` or `Where` leave get their values back, and a transform reworks the same values every time rather than its own output. Rerendering a generator whose notes didn't move gives the same values, so it's safe to rerender on any note change. **What's under it** is each row's cell of the column: a note, a velocity, a nudge, a whole gate cell, or an FX slot, so typing over a gate's pulses on a live length applies it too. It's saved as steps holding only that column, in the steps' own text, so the file stays readable, with the column (an FX command by its key), the span, the `Shape` and its first row. **Lanes:** a generator with a lane span covers the lane's rows as they are after each edit; when they change, the rows it leaves get back what was under them, the rows it takes keep what's there as what's under them, and it rerenders; no lane applies it. **Edits that move rows:** `]r` applies the generators on the loop's rows, as their values move with the steps; doubling applies all of a phrase's, as rows after the loop move down; sliding the loop past the first step shifts a generator's rows with the steps added in front, as lanes are shifted; a length change keeps them. **A new generator** on rows overlapping one on the same column (an FX column by its slot) applies the old one first, so it starts from the values left, as `S` reshapes from what's there. **Actions:** `Generate`, `Reshape` and `ApplyGenerator`, the last two by the generator's place in its phrase's list, which an open panel reads from the song it opened on. **Format:** an older build reads a song with generators as plain values, as it ignores the field, so no bump and no conversion are needed. **Tests** (`vt-model/tests/generators.rs` and `vt-tui/tests/grammar.rs`): it writes what the panel would and stays live; a rerender changes its own cells only; a hand edit to its cell applies it and one to a note's pitch doesn't; a note added under a velocity generator gets a generated velocity in one undo, and taken away gives its own back; Euclid's `Rotate` moves the hits and gives the rows left their values back, and a transform twice is the same as once; on a lane, it fills the lane, follows a longer and a shorter one, and is applied by `:lane`; two generators on different columns are independent; an overlapping one applies the old; doubling and rotating apply it and a longer loop doesn't; sliding keeps its rows; and three of them round-trip through saving, still live after loading. Added on 2026-11-11.

181. **In the shape panel, `L` keeps the shape live where `Enter` keeps plain values; `gl` on any row of a live column opens the panel again on its saved `Shape`, where `L` keeps the new settings, `Enter` applies it with them and `Esc` leaves it; `Space g a` and `:gen apply` apply the one under the cursor; a live column's values are drawn in teal, the shape panel's own colour on the status line, an FX column's by its command so its values keep their colours, and the phrase's title names its live columns, like `Live:Vel,FX1`** (#18, decision 178). **`L`:** a second exit beside `Enter`, as the issue asked, on a key the panel didn't use; capital, as it's the wider choice, and next to `gl`, which reopens it. Across tracks it keeps one generator on each, and on an FX column with a lane it's live on the lane, whichever rows were selected, as the status line says. **`gl`:** on the cursor's column, the generator covering the cursor's row, or the nearest on that column, so it works from anywhere in the column, and after a reload. The panel says `, live` in its heading and `Enter apply` in its keys. **Applying by hand:** the status line says `Vel was live: it's plain values now. u makes it live again`, from comparing the song before and after in `App::apply` and in the panel. **The marker:** the cells with a value in the generator's rows, as an empty cell drawn in teal would be loud; teal because it's already the colour of the shape panel's mode, so it means the same thing in both places. A Nerd Font glyph was left out, as plain text in the title reads as clearly and needs no change to the manual's font. **Tests:** `L` as one undo, `gl` from another row turning `Rotate`, `L` again, `Esc` and `Enter` there; a hand edit applying it with its message and `u`; `Space g a`, `:gen apply` and `:gen` alone; across tracks, a generator and a seed for each, reopened and applied on its own; `L` on a lane; and the title and the teal cells drawn. Added on 2026-11-11. Changed by decision 182.

182. **A live generator's marker in the phrase's title is a wand, `nf-md-auto_fix` (U+F0068), in teal before the live columns' names, like `󰁨 Vel,FX1`, replacing the word `Live:`, amending decision 181** (#18). **Why:** the user asked for a glyph if it wasn't busy. On every row it would sit beside every value in the column, and the grid has no heading per column to carry it, so it goes in the title once; the teal values still show which rows and column it owns. That reads like the lock: a glyph says what it is, and the colour where. **Prior art:** none; a wand rather than circling arrows, which read as repeat or loop. Added on 2026-11-11.

192. **`Analog` is a fourth envelope curve, after `Exp`, `Lin` and `Snap`: an attack that charges a capacitor toward 1.5 and switches to its decay at 1, `1.5 (1 - 3^-t)`, which starts fast and slows into full, and decays and releases that are true RC curves, timed to fall 60 dB and scaled to land on time as every fall is; and an attack started from a level, as one is with `Reset` off, now starts where its curve reaches that level, keeping its shape and taking only what's left of its time, always, with no setting** (#467, the DSP parts review's oscillators Part B, tricks 7 and 8, after decision 176). **Why:** every curve's attack started slowly (`t^3.32`), which reads as a fade-in; the familiar analog attack is concave and fast-starting. And an attack from a level used to stretch a whole attack from it, `from + (1 - from) rise(t)`, so a fast run of notes with `Reset` off crawled up over the full attack time each note, its curve flattened by how high it began. **Prior art:** Surge's analog envelope (`ADSRModulationSource.h`, `v_cc = 1.5` and the 1 V switch), OB-Xf and TAL charging a capacitor, for the curve; TetraOP's `getMatchingAttackTimeFromRelease` (`Envelope.cpp`), and Surge's analog mode charging on from where it is, for the retrigger. **The curve:** `Curve::Analog`, its attack `bent(ln 3, t)`, which is exactly `1.5 (1 - 3^-t)`, and its falls `bent(ln 1000, t)`, so halfway through a decay to nothing it's 30 dB down, between `Exp`'s 18.5 and `Snap`'s 35, and 60 dB down nine tenths of the way. Surge squares its sustain under this mode; ours doesn't, as `Sustain` is a percent of full on every curve and should read the same whichever is chosen. Saved as `curve: Analog` beside the others, so the format stays at 9. **The retrigger:** `Curve::rise_time` turns each curve's rise round (`L^(1/3.32)` for `Exp` and `Snap`, `L` for `Lin`, and the log for `Analog`), and an ADSR's or AHD's `held` starts its attack there and its later stages as much sooner, so the decay comes when it would have had the attack started from nothing that much earlier. An attack from nothing is worked out exactly as before, to the bit, so every song with `Reset` on, and every golden render, is unchanged. **Default, not a setting:** `Reset` off already means "go on from where it is", and this is what going on means for a capacitor, so it's how `Reset` off should always have behaved rather than a choice beside it; the old stretch has no musical use a setting would keep, and the ADSR's grid has no cell left. **The page:** `=` and `-` on the curve go through four, `K` lists `Analog` as "rises fast and slows, as a capacitor charges", and the picture draws the curve the engine plays, through the same `Curve::rise` and `fall`. **The Roto:** the curve's knob has four steps, so the ADSR's and AHD's stored setups change and are written again when the Roto next connects. Added on 2026-11-11.

186. **A delay's `Retime` chooses how a new `Time` comes in, `Glide` (as before) or `Jump`, a straight crossfade from the old read to the new one over half the new time, between 10 ms and half a second, ignoring changes while it fades; `Wow` is one setting, how deep a fixed slow wobble goes; and the flanger's feedback loses its DC** (#469, the DSP parts review's effects §2, tricks 4, 9 and 13). **Why:** a step-locked time change glided, bending the repeats' pitch, which a groovebox user hears as a mistake rather than a tape effect. **Prior art:** sst-effects' Delay (`effects/Delay.h:200-286`, `:338-378`): its `Clean` line mode, whose fade length and lockout this follows exactly, and whose straight-line fade it keeps for its reason, that the two reads are of the same sound, so equal power would bump the level on every small retime; and its time LFO (`:509-541`), opposite on the two sides. Surge's BBD ensemble and spring reverb block DC in their loops (`BBDEnsembleEffect.cpp:273-275`). **The choices:** `Glide` stays the default, so every song plays as it did, to the bit. `Wow` has no rate of its own: a rounded triangle at 0.5 Hz, up to half a percent of speed (about 9 cents) either way at `FF`, the left read moving one way and the right the other. One knob keeps the delay's grid to four columns and its Roto layout to two pages (`Retime` and `Wow` on the second, as `Retime` has two choices rather than off and on, so it can't be a button). A rate is easy to add as a note division if `Wow` is wanted as a tempo effect. The flanger's DC blocker is at 10 Hz on what goes round only, so what comes out keeps its DC as before, and it's held to twice the line's range first, so a NaN read from the line can't stay in it (#461). **Saved as** `retime` and `wow` on the delay, left out when `0`, so the format stays where it is. Added on 2026-11-11.
